# @elmeragroup/fuse-figma

Syncs the Fuse design tokens into a Figma file's local variables. The repository is the master for theming data, so the sync writes code into Figma and never reads design decisions back. It is a private workspace package with no build step. Node runs the TypeScript directly.

It also builds the Fuse components plugin, a proof of concept that creates the Button, Confirm button, Toggle, Toggle group, Badge, Kbd, Kbd group, Radio icon button, Radio, Radio row, Radio group, Checkbox, Switch, Separator, Skeleton, Loader, Label, Field description, Field error, Field legend, Field title, Input, Input group, Text field, Textarea, Textarea field, Avatar, Avatar group, Meter, Pagination link, Pagination edge, Pagination ellipsis, Pagination row, Breadcrumb link, Breadcrumb page, Breadcrumb ellipsis, Breadcrumb separator, Breadcrumb trail, Select trigger, Select field, Menu group label, Menu separator, Select item, Select popup, Select (open), Popover content, Popover (open), Dropdown menu item, Dropdown menu checkbox item, Dropdown menu radio item, Dropdown menu sub trigger, Dropdown menu popup, Dropdown menu sub content, Dropdown menu (open), Combobox item, Combobox empty, Combobox popup, Combobox (open), Command item, Command list, Command palette, Command dialog (open), Empty, Link, Item, Item link, Alert, Accordion item, Sidebar menu button, Sidebar menu sub button, Sidebar group label, Sidebar menu badge, Sidebar group, Sidebar header, Sidebar footer, Sidebar sub menu, Sidebar menu item with badge, Sidebar, Navigation menu bar trigger, Navigation menu vertical trigger, Navigation menu bar link, Navigation menu row link, Navigation menu popup, Number field stepper, Number field, File upload dropzone, Toolbar, Tooltip content, Tooltip (open), Alert dialog content, Alert dialog (open), Dialog content, Dialog (open), Card, Action bar, row, Table caption, Attachment, Description list item, Description list, Checkbox card, List detail pane, List detail, Data table filter bar, Tour popup, Tour step and Toast component sets, each built from a browser capture of the component, an icon library from Fuse's icons, and the Text, Heading and Span text styles. [Components plugin](#components-plugin) describes it.

## What lands in Figma

The sync owns six variable collections, matched by name.

| Collection        | Modes                  | Variables                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------- | ---------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Fuse tokens`     | `Light`, `Dark`        | 232 variables. Designers bind one per contract token such as `primary`, `radius`, `brand-neutral-100` or `brand-1-800`, one per radius rung such as `radius-md`, one per role tint such as `error/10`, and one per formula over tokens such as `radius-compact`. Two more per formula over tokens and density metrics, such as `radius-inner/lg-pad-sm/dense`, are hidden from pickers. |
| `Fuse identity`   | `Neutral`, `Branded`   | 464 variables, `<scheme>/<token>` for each `Fuse tokens` variable. Hidden from pickers. Earlier syncs named it `Fuse variant`, with `Internal` and `External` modes; see [Renamed collections](#renamed-collections).                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `Fuse segment`    | `Private`, `Company`   | 928 variables, `<scheme>/<identity>/<token>` for each `Fuse tokens` variable. Hidden from pickers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `Fuse brand`      | One per brand, 7       | 1856 variables, `<scheme>/<identity>/<segment>/<token>` for each `Fuse tokens` variable. They hold the theme values. Hidden from pickers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `Fuse primitives` | `Value`                | 25 variables, the neutral ramp and the brand accents. They hold the same value in every theme. The neutral ramp is hidden from pickers, and the brand accents stay bindable.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `Fuse density`    | `Dense`, `Comfortable` | 63 variables, one per density metric: control (`control-h-md`), row (`row-h`), surface (`surface-pad-lg`, `surface-gap-md`) and label (`label-text`), and one per formula over metrics, such as `empty-pad-desktop`, or over tokens and metrics, such as `radius-inner/lg-pad-sm`. Designers bind these.                                                                                |

Each theme axis is one collection, so a designer previews a theme by picking each axis in the frame's Appearance panel, as in the docs theme picker. `Fuse tokens` gets `Light` or `Dark`, `Fuse identity` gets `Neutral` or `Branded`, `Fuse segment` gets `Private` or `Company`, and `Fuse brand` gets the brand, such as `Fjordkraft`. A frame that sets no mode for a collection gets its first mode, so an unset frame shows neutral Fjordkraft private in light, at dense density. Figma resolves an alias that crosses into another collection with the frame's mode for the target collection, inherited from any ancestor. A child frame can therefore change one axis and keep the others.

Every `Fuse tokens` variable takes the same four hops. `primary` in `Light` aliases `light/primary` in `Fuse identity`, which aliases `light/branded/primary` in `Fuse segment` under `Branded`, which aliases `light/branded/company/primary` in `Fuse brand` under `Company`. That variable holds each brand's value for the branded company theme. The path segments are the lowercase code values, so the hidden variables keep their names whichever axes a token varies on.

A brand mode is the brand's display name. Telinet allows only the private segment and Fjordkraft Företag only the company segment, so their modes read `Telinet (private only)` and `Fjordkraft Företag (company only)`. Figma cannot disable a mode, so such a brand holds its only segment's values under the other segment too: Telinet in `Company` shows Telinet private. The docs picker moves the segment instead.

Density is a document axis that no identity implies, as in Fuse, where every document stamps its own `data-density`. `Fuse density` therefore has a `Dense` and a `Comfortable` mode, each holding its own pixels, and a frame pins the one its screen uses: a branded customer dashboard pins `Dense`, a branded promo page `Comfortable`. A frame that pins none gets `Dense`, the first mode.

Earlier syncs also wrote an `Auto` mode, first, which followed the frame's retired variant mode: `Internal` frames dense and `External` frames comfortable. The sync deletes it from a file that still has it, along with the hidden `density/<metric>` variables in `Fuse variant` and the `<name>/auto` values behind each formula over tokens and metrics. Frames pinned to `Auto` lose that pin and fall back to the collection's default mode, the left-most column of the variables view, which is `Dense` once `Auto` is gone. A branded frame that relied on `Auto` for comfortable density must pin `Comfortable` again.

Earlier syncs wrote a `Fuse themes` collection with one mode per theme slug, such as `branded-fkas-private`. The sync deletes a local collection with that name, and `check` reports it as drift. Frames pinned to a `Fuse themes` mode lose that pin, and designers set the four axis modes instead. The sync never deletes a library collection or a collection extension with that name.

The token values come from `resolveThemeCatalog` in `@elmeragroup/fuse/theme-catalog`. It resolves every theme in both schemes from `composeTheme`, the same composition that generates `themes.css`, and hands the sync each value already read: an sRGB color, a length in pixels or a font weight, plus the one `var()` hop when the value is a reference. Fuse's themes name no fonts, so the kit keeps its own in `src/kit-fonts.ts`. `font-sans` is Roboto in every theme. `font-heading` follows it, except in Fjordkraft's branded themes, which set Neo Sans. Fuse sets `--font-mono` itself, to the platform's monospace stack, which Playwright's Chromium on macOS resolves to Menlo. Figma draws only a family in its font list, so `font-mono` is Roboto Mono in every theme. The catalog throws on a token value it cannot read, because token values are Fuse source, and a Fuse test resolves every theme and scheme so CI catches such a value first. The sync maps the resolved values as follows.

- A color becomes an sRGB color, with alpha kept and the channels unrounded. The tokens write `oklch()` or `#rrggbb` literals, and the catalog reads exactly those two forms through `@elmeragroup/color`, which clips a color outside the sRGB gamut.
- `var(--name)` becomes an alias to the matching variable. A role reference points at the role with the same scheme, identity and segment in `Fuse brand`, and a primitive reference points into `Fuse primitives`.
- A `rem` length becomes the catalog's pixels at a 16px root.
- A radius rung becomes the pixels `fuse.css` computes for it, such as `--radius-md: calc(var(--radius) - var(--radius-step, 0px))`, because Figma variables cannot compute. The catalog computes each rung per theme from the theme's `radius` and `radius-step` and the rung's step count, and the sync writes those pixels per brand mode. Neutral themes step 0px, so every rung equals `radius`. Branded themes step 2px, so `branded-fkas-private` with a 12px radius gets 6, 8, 10, 12 and 16px from `radius-xs` to `radius-xl`. CSS clamps a negative `border-radius` to 0, and so does the catalog.
- A density metric comes from the catalog in pixels per density, read from the same metrics the `fuse.css` density blocks must equal. Heights, including `row-h`, get the width and height picker. Paddings, including the `row-px`, `row-py` and `surface-pad-*` paddings, and gaps, including the `surface-gap-*` tiers, get the gap picker. The type pairs, `control-text` and `control-leading` for controls and `label-text` and `label-leading` for labels such as selection row text, get the font size and line height pickers.
- A font stack becomes its first family name, which the catalog reads.
- A font weight, such as `selection-title-weight`, becomes its number, from 1 to 1000, and gets the font weight picker. The sync sends that picker as `FONT_STYLE`. Figma's REST documentation lists `FONT_WEIGHT` for number variables, but a real file stores `FONT_WEIGHT` as `FONT_STYLE`, so a sync that sent it would never read back what it wrote.
- A derived role, such as `secondary-hover`, becomes the literal color that composition computes, because Figma variables cannot mix colors.
- A role tint, such as `error/10`, holds its role's color at the tint's alpha in every theme. A Figma paint takes its opacity from the alpha of the variable it binds, so a component cannot bind `error` at 10%, and `bg-error/10` binds `error/10` instead. The tint's code syntax is the `color-mix(in oklab, var(--error) 10%, transparent)` that Tailwind compiles the utility to. `ROLE_TINTS` in `fuse-variable-set.ts` lists the tints the component kit binds. A tint appears in its role's pickers, and its description names the role and the percent, since the catalog has no entry for it.
- A formula variable holds what a recipe's formula over theme tokens or density metrics gives. Figma variables cannot compute, so `FORMULA_VARIABLES` in `fuse-variable-set.ts` computes each one, and its code syntax is the formula as Tailwind compiles the recipe's class, which the kit binds. The kit's builder reads Tailwind's own theme variables as their values, so the code syntax writes `var(--spacing)` as `0.25rem` and `var(--leading-snug)` as `1.375`.
  - What a formula reads decides where it lands. A formula over tokens alone lands in `Fuse tokens` with a value for each theme. A formula over metrics alone lands in `Fuse density` as a metric does: `Dense` and `Comfortable` hold its pixels. A formula over tokens and metrics lands in `Fuse density` too, but each of its modes aliases a hidden `Fuse tokens` variable, `<name>/dense` or `<name>/comfortable`. That variable takes the four hops of every `Fuse tokens` variable and holds what the formula gives for each theme at its density. Figma resolves each hop with the frame's mode for that collection, so one bound variable follows both the frame's theme and its density. A formula that reads a metric lands in `Fuse density` even when the metric holds one value at both densities, so a later density change cannot move its variable to another collection, which would unbind the layers that use it.
  - The outline Button's shadow lengths, `button-outline-shadow-blur` and `button-outline-shadow-spread`, hold what `max(0px, 4px - 2 * var(--button-outline-width, 1px))` and `min(0px, 2px - 2 * var(--button-outline-width, 1px))` give for each theme's outline width: blur 2 and spread 0 at the neutral 1px outline, which is Tailwind's `shadow-xs`, and blur 0 and spread -2 at the branded 2px outline, which paints nothing outside the border. They get the effect picker.
  - The compact corner, `radius-compact`, holds what `compactCornerClass` in `corner-radius.ts` gives, `min(radius-md, max(10px, radius - 1000 * radius-step))`. Neutral themes step 0px, so it equals `radius`, 8px for Fjordkraft. Branded themes step 2px, so it is `radius - 2px` capped at 10px: 4px for Elmera's 6px radius and 10px for TrøndelagKraft's 16px. The xs Toggle rounds with it, and it gets the corner radius picker.
  - The checkbox corner, `radius-checkbox`, holds what `checkboxCornerClass` in `corner-radius.ts` gives, `min(radius-md, 4px)`. Every theme's `radius-md` is at least 4px, so it holds 4px in each. The Checkbox box rounds with it, and it gets the corner radius picker.
  - The field box corner, `radius-field-box`, holds what `fieldCornerClass` in `corner-radius.ts` gives, `clamp(radius - 1000 * radius-step, radius-field, radius + 1000 * radius-step)`. Neutral themes step 0px, so both bounds equal `radius` and it holds `radius`, 8px for Fjordkraft. Branded themes step 2px, so the bounds lie 4000px apart and it holds `radius-field`. Every theme sets `radius-field` to `var(--radius)`, so that is 6px for Elmera and 16px for TrøndelagKraft. Fuse rounds Input, Textarea and the other field boxes with that class, and the variable gets the corner radius picker.
  - An inner corner is the `--shell-inner` that a shell in `inner-corner/` publishes and `rounded-inner` reads, `max(0px, rung - border - padding)`. Shells of several components compile to the same formula, and the kit binds by code syntax, so each corner is named for its shell's rung, border and padding. The kit compares code syntax with each number spelled one way, so the shipped CSS's `.25rem` binds a formula's `0.25rem`. `radius-inner/lg-pad-8px` reads tokens alone: a floating Sidebar's group in the icon rail pads 8px inside `radius-lg`, which leaves 0 in neutral themes, 4px for Fjordkraft's branded 12px radius and 8px for TrøndelagKraft's 16px. The others read `surface-pad-md`, `surface-pad-sm`, `surface-gap-sm` or `control-px-icon-md` too: `radius-inner/sm-pad-md` inside a default Accordion item, `radius-inner/lg-pad-md` inside a Toast or an Accordion item with the lg radius, `radius-inner/xl-pad-md` inside an Accordion item with the xl radius, `radius-inner/lg-border-pad-md` inside a card Accordion item or a SelectionItem, `radius-inner/xl-border-pad-md` inside a card Accordion item with the xl radius, `radius-inner/lg-pad-sm` inside a floating Sidebar's header, footer or group, or a Select group, `radius-inner/md-pad-sm` inside a DropdownMenu popup or a Combobox list, `radius-inner/xl-gap-sm` inside a Command list, and `radius-inner/field-border-pad-addon-button` on a button in an Input group addon. `radius-inner/md-pad-sm` is 4px in neutral themes, 6px for Fjordkraft's branded theme and 10px for TrøndelagKraft's at both densities. TrøndelagKraft's branded theme gives `radius-inner/xl-border-pad-md` 7px dense and 3px comfortable. `radius-inner/md-border-pad-md` and `radius-inner/md-border-pad-row` sit inside an Attachment's border around its media, `radius-inner/field-border-pad-md` inside a field box around a DateField segment, `radius-inner/field-border` inside a field box's border around a DatePicker's calendar trigger, and `radius-inner/lg-pad-4px`, which reads tokens alone, inside a Tabs list around its trigger. They get the corner radius picker.
  - The calendar corner, `radius-calendar`, holds what Calendar's `rounded-[clamp(radius - 1000 * radius-step, 4px, radius + 1000 * radius-step)]` gives: `radius` in neutral themes, which step 0px, and 4px in branded ones. It gets the corner radius picker.
  - Empty's desktop padding, `empty-pad-desktop`, is twice `surface-pad-lg`, 32px dense and 48px comfortable. The space above an open Accordion panel's text, `accordion-panel-pt`, is `surface-gap-lg` plus 6px, 22px and 30px. Both get the gap picker. One snug line of label text, `label-line-snug`, is `label-text` times 1.375, 19.25px and 22px, which is how tall an Alert's icon box is beside a description. It gets the width and height picker. A menu row's room for its 16px indicator and the 8px gap beside it, `row-indicator-room`, is `row-px` plus 24px, 32px dense and 36px comfortable, and gets the gap picker. The end padding of a row with a trailing indicator and the start padding of an inset row compile to that one formula, and the kit binds by code syntax, so both bind it. An Input group addon that holds a button pads its outer side by `addon-px-button`, `control-px-icon-md` less the 4px its negative margin takes back, 4px dense and 8px comfortable, with the gap picker. A Command palette's footer pads its hints across by `command-text-px`, `surface-gap-sm` plus `row-px`, 16px dense and 24px comfortable, which lines them up with the rows' text, with the gap picker. The height inside the md field box's 1px borders, `field-box-content-h`, is `control-h-md` less 2px, 34px dense and 42px comfortable, which a standalone Number field stepper's `h-full` binds through its definition's preview height, with the width and height picker. The kit folds the addon's margin into that padding and binds the variable whose formula is the same sum of variables and pixels. The button rounds with `radius-inner/field-border-pad-addon-button`, the field box corner less its 1px border and that padding. A Frame panel's header and footer pad across by `frame-bar-inset`, 1px plus `surface-pad-lg`, 17px dense and 25px comfortable, in the gutter layout, and by `frame-bar-inset-flush`, which takes the flush layout's 1px overhang back to `surface-pad-lg`, and above and below by `frame-bar-py`, half of `surface-pad-lg`. They get the gap picker. Item's own media line reads `text-sm` and `leading-snug`, two Tailwind theme values every theme shares, so the kit's builder reads it as a literal 19.25px and the sync holds no variable for it.
  - A Tabs trigger's `h-[calc(100%-1px)]` takes its list's content height, `control-h-md` less the list's padding on each side, so `tabs-trigger-h` is `control-h-md` less 9px in the default list, which pads 4px, and `tabs-line-trigger-h` less 7px in the line list, which pads 3px. They get the width and height picker.
- The variables designers bind in `Fuse tokens`, `Fuse primitives` and `Fuse density` carry web code syntax. A token, primitive or metric carries its `var()`, such as `var(--primary)`. A radius rung carries the `calc()` that `fuse.css` declares, such as `calc(var(--radius) - 3 * var(--radius-step, 0px))`. `fuse.css` declares the rungs in `@theme inline`, so Tailwind inlines them into its utilities, and the built CSS declares no `--radius-sm` property. A test requires every `var()` in the code syntax to name a property that the built `styles.css` or `themes.css` declares.
- A color appears only in the pickers for where Fuse paints it. The catalog gives each color role and primitive its paints from `TOKEN_PAINTS` and `PRIMITIVE_PAINTS` in `@elmeragroup/fuse`. A `surface` gets the frame and shape fill pickers, an `ink` gets the text fill, shape fill and stroke pickers, because icons take the text color, and a `line` gets the stroke picker. A role with every fill gets `ALL_FILLS`. `feature-foreground` is a surface only, so the text fill picker leaves it out.
- The neutral ramp gets no picker scope. It holds one value in every theme and scheme, so a layer bound to it breaks dark mode and brand switching. It stays an alias target, as the hidden collections do. The brand accents keep the fill pickers and their foregrounds the text pickers, for artwork that shows several brands.
- Font families get the font family picker. Figma's REST documentation says scopes currently apply only to FLOAT and COLOR variables, but a real file keeps `FONT_FAMILY` on a STRING variable.
- Each length token names its picker scope in a table in `fuse-variable-set.ts`, so a new length token does not compile until someone picks one. `radius`, `radius-button`, `radius-field` and the radius rungs get the corner radius picker, `button-outline-width` gets the stroke picker, the paragraph line heights, `paragraph-leading-sm`, `paragraph-leading-base` and `paragraph-leading-lg`, and the heading line heights, `heading-1-leading` to `heading-3-leading`, get the line height picker, and the heading sizes, `heading-1-size` to `heading-3-size`, get the font size picker.
- `radius-step` gets no picker scope. It is a length, but it spaces the radius rungs and switches between the neutral and branded corner families, so no layer rounds with it. It keeps its code syntax, `var(--radius-step)`.
- Every variable designers bind in `Fuse tokens`, `Fuse primitives` and `Fuse density` carries the catalog's description, which Figma shows when a designer hovers the variable. The descriptions live beside the tokens in `@elmeragroup/fuse`, and the docs tokens page shows the same text. The hidden variables carry an empty description. A real file stored `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` and `'` in a description HTML-escaped on 2026-10-19, through the REST API and the Plugin API alike, so the sync refuses a description that holds one of them.

The sync writes no text styles. Figma's REST API reads only published styles, through `GET /v1/teams/:team_id/styles`, `GET /v1/files/:file_key/styles` and `GET /v1/styles/:key` (developers.figma.com/docs/rest-api/component-endpoints, read on 2026-11-16), and has no endpoint that creates or changes one. The Plugin API creates one with `figma.createTextStyle()`, so the components plugin keeps the Fuse text styles. [Text styles](#text-styles) describes them.

## Running it

The Variables REST API needs an Enterprise plan. Create a personal access token under Settings → Security in Figma. The token needs the `file_variables:read` and `file_variables:write` scopes and belongs to a Full seat with edit access to the target file. Figma caps these tokens at 90 days, and a plan access token cannot write variables.

Copy `packages/fuse-figma/.env.example` to `packages/fuse-figma/.env`, which git ignores, and fill in the token and the file key:

```sh
FIGMA_TOKEN=figd_...
FIGMA_FILE_KEY=AbCdEf123   # the part after /design/ in the file URL
```

Then run one of these commands from the repository root:

```sh
pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:check   # print the plan, write nothing, exit 2 when Figma differs
pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:sync    # apply the plan
```

`--file-key <key>` overrides `FIGMA_FILE_KEY`, and a branch key targets a Figma branch. `figma --help` lists every flag.

A new file can refuse every REST variable write until Figma itself has made one. Each write then fails with `500 (Error updating file, please try again later)`, even one that creates a single collection, while reads and `check` work. Before the first sync, open the file in Figma, create a variable collection and delete it. The sync prints these steps with a write's 500 and does not retry the write.

`check` exits 2 when the file differs from the tokens and 1 when it cannot run, for example when the token has expired, so a CI job can tell the two apart. It groups the plan by collection and kind of change. A group of up to 10 changes lists each variable and mode, and a larger group prints its count.

The token travels in the `X-Figma-Token` header. The sync masks that header on the trace spans Effect's HTTP client records, and a failed request keeps only its method and URL.

After a sync, publish the library in Figma. The REST API cannot publish, so consuming files see the new values only after someone publishes by hand.

## How a sync decides what to write

The sync reads the file, plans one change batch and sends it. Figma applies a batch completely or rejects all of it. The sync then reads the file again and fails unless the plan is now empty, so a successful run means the file was checked.

- It matches collections, modes and variables by name and updates them in place. Ids survive, so every layer bound to a Fuse variable stays bound.
- A mode keeps its id only while the tokens keep its name. The sync deletes a mode the tokens no longer name and creates the modes they add, and it never renames one into another, because a dropped brand and an added one are unrelated. Frames pinned to a deleted mode lose that pin. Renaming a brand's display name in code is therefore a delete and a create in Figma, and designers repin the frames that used the old name. The one exception is a collection the set renames, below.
- Mode order does not matter to the plan. A collection whose modes the file holds in another order than the tokens counts as in sync, because the REST API cannot reorder them.
- Variable order does not matter to the plan either, because the sync cannot move one. [Variable order](#variable-order) explains the order a sync creates and how to change it.
- The sync orders mode changes so that no collection goes over Figma's 40 modes or runs out of modes while the batch applies. It deletes the stale modes before creating new ones. When no existing mode survives, it keeps the last stale mode until the first new mode exists. Besides the modes of a renamed collection, the only mode it renames is the one Figma gives a new collection, which becomes the collection's first mode.
- Inside the six Fuse collections, the tokens are authoritative. The sync deletes any mode or variable the tokens do not define, and the next sync resets any value a designer edited.
- It never touches another collection in the file, except that it deletes a local collection named `Fuse themes`, which earlier syncs wrote, and renames `Fuse variant` to `Fuse identity` in place. The sync skips library collections, collection extensions and deleted variables that layers still reference, even when their names match.
- It refuses to change a variable's type, because Figma can only do that by deleting the variable and unbinding every layer that uses it. The error names the variable to delete by hand.
- Renaming a token in code is a delete and a create in Figma, so layers bound to the old name lose their binding.
- It refuses to sync tokens whose aliases form a cycle. A cycle counts even when each alias closes it in a different mode, because the modes a layer combines decide whether Figma would loop.
- It writes the values of each alias target before the values that alias it. Figma applies values in order and refuses a batch that passes through a cycle after any one value, even when the batch ends without one. This order never passes through a cycle, even when the tokens reverse an alias.
- A value shape or variable type newer than the sync does not stop a read. A variable the sync owns gets such a value rewritten, and a type it does not know is a type conflict.
- It owns every variable's description, so the next sync resets a description a designer wrote. It sets web code syntax on the variables designers bind in `Fuse tokens`, `Fuse primitives` and `Fuse density`, and sends no code syntax for the hidden values in `Fuse tokens` or for the `Fuse identity`, `Fuse segment` and `Fuse brand` variables, which only feed aliases. An update replaces a variable's code syntax, so when the sync sets the web entry, it sends the file's Android and iOS entries with it.
- The sync retries a read that hits a rate limit, a server error or a network failure. It waits as long as `Retry-After` asks and logs a warning for each retry. It retries a write only after a 429, because a write that failed on the server may still have been applied. When `Retry-After` asks for more than 60 seconds, the sync fails at once and reports the wait.

The alternative was kumo-figma's approach, which deletes every variable and recreates it on each run. That gives every variable a new id and breaks the bindings in existing designs.

### Renamed collections

The sync matches collections and modes by name, so renaming either in code would delete the old one and every frame's pin on its modes. A collection the set declares renamed is therefore renamed in place by id instead: when the file has exactly one local collection with the old name and none with the new one, the sync updates the collection's name and the names of the modes the rename maps, and every id stays, so the pins stay. `check` lists these as `rename collection` and `rename mode` changes. `Fuse identity` was `Fuse variant`, and its `Internal` and `External` modes became `Neutral` and `Branded`, so a frame pinned to `External` now shows `Branded`. The hidden variables inside it change their path segments from `internal` and `external` to `neutral` and `branded`, which the sync writes as deletes and creates; designers bind only `Fuse tokens`, `Fuse density` and `Fuse primitives` variables, whose names and ids stay. A file that holds only `Fuse identity` keeps it. If its modes still carry the old names, as when a designer renamed the collection by hand, the sync renames `Internal` and `External` to `Neutral` and `Branded` by id, so their pins stay; a fully migrated file needs no rename. The sync refuses to plan, and writes nothing, in the cases it cannot rename in place: the file holds two local collections named `Fuse variant` (`AmbiguousRenameSource`), it holds both `Fuse variant` and `Fuse identity` (`UnresolvedLegacyCollection`; repin the frames on the old collection, delete it, then sync), or a mode rename would give two modes one name, such as a `Fuse variant` or `Fuse identity` that holds both `Internal` and `Neutral` (`RenameModeConflict`). Each prints what to change in Figma. Creating a fresh collection or deleting the clashing mode instead would drop the frames pinned to the old modes.

### Variable order

The sync cannot place a variable in Figma's variables panel. `POST /v1/files/:file_key/variables` takes no position, index or sort field for a variable, a collection or a mode (developers.figma.com/docs/rest-api/variables-endpoints, read on 2026-11-19). In the Plugin API, `VariableCollection.variableIds` is read-only, and neither `VariableCollection` nor `Variable` has a member that moves a variable (developers.figma.com/docs/plugins/api/VariableCollection and developers.figma.com/docs/plugins/api/Variable, read on 2026-11-19). Both APIs say `variableIds` lists a collection's variables in roughly the order Figma Design shows, without groups (developers.figma.com/docs/rest-api/variables-types). The sync creates new variables in the set's order.

`Fuse segment` and `Fuse brand` list branded before neutral inside each scheme. `Fuse brand` reads `light/branded/private`, `light/branded/company`, `light/neutral/private`, `light/neutral/company`, then the same four under `dark`. Branded values differ from brand to brand, while most neutral values repeat across brands, so the rows worth comparing come first. Scheme and segment follow their modes, light before dark and private before company, so the values a frame that pins no mode shows come first inside each group. The paths nest the scheme outside the identity, so branded cannot come before light neutral without renaming every hidden variable. The `Fuse identity` modes keep their order, so a frame that pins no identity still shows `Neutral`. `Fuse identity` and `Fuse tokens` name no identity in their paths, so their order stays. A first sync into an empty file gave `Fuse brand` a `variableIds` in this group order.

A file synced before this order keeps neutral first, and the sync does not report it. The sync cannot recreate the variables in the new order in one batch. Figma checks a created variable's name against the collection's existing names before it applies the batch's deletes, and refuses the batch with a 400, `Invalid name: collides with an existing variable in variable collection`. Renaming, creating, repointing and deleting over several batches would change the order, but a failed batch would leave a published library half migrated, and the panel order is not worth that risk. No Figma document says where the panel puts a variable a later sync adds, so its place is unverified.

To change the order of an existing file, drag the groups in Figma. The Help Center says designers can "click and drag groups in the sidebar of the Variables modal to reorder groups" (help.figma.com/hc/en-us/articles/15145852043927, read on 2026-11-19). The sync matches by name, so a drag does not affect the next sync.

## Components plugin

The plugin creates two component sets of Button and one of Confirm button on a page named `Button`, two of Toggle and one of Toggle group on a page named `Toggle`, one of Badge on a page named `Badge`, two of Kbd on a page named `Kbd`, one of Radio icon button, one of Radio and two of Radio row and RadioGroup's Field frame on a page named `Radio`, one of Checkbox on a page named `Checkbox`, one of Switch on a page named `Switch`, one of Separator on a page named `Separator`, one of Skeleton on a page named `Skeleton`, one of Loader on a page named `Loader`, one of Field label and four of Field's description, error, legend and title on a page named `Field`, one of Input, one of Input group and one from TextField's Field frame on a page named `Input`, one of Textarea and one from TextareaField's Field frame on a page named `Textarea`, two of Avatar on a page named `Avatar`, one of Meter on a page named `Meter`, four of Pagination on a page named `Pagination`, five of Breadcrumb on a page named `Breadcrumb`, one of Select trigger, one from SelectField's Field frame, two of the shared menu rows and three of Select content on a page named `Select`, two of Popover on a page named `Popover`, seven of Dropdown menu on a page named `Dropdown menu`, four of Combobox on a page named `Combobox`, four of Command on a page named `Command`, one of Empty on a page named `Empty`, one of Link on a page named `Link`, two of Item on a page named `Item`, one of Alert on a page named `Alert`, one of Accordion on a page named `Accordion`, four of Sidebar's rows, five of its sections and one of its panel on a page named `Sidebar`, five of Navigation menu on a page named `Navigation menu`, two of Number field on a page named `Number field`, one of File upload on a page named `File upload`, one of Toolbar on a page named `Toolbar`, two of Tooltip on a page named `Tooltip`, two of Alert dialog on a page named `Alert dialog`, two of Dialog on a page named `Dialog`, one of Card on a page named `Card`, one of Action bar on a page named `Action bar`, one of Sheet content on a page named `Sheet`, one of Calendar, one of Range calendar and one of Calendar day on a page named `Calendar`, one of Date field on a page named `Date field`, one of Date picker on a page named `Date picker`, one of Date range picker on a page named `Date range picker`, one of Timeline list on a page named `Timeline list`, three of Tabs on a page named `Tabs`, one of Selection item on a page named `Selection item`, one of Aspect ratio on a page named `Aspect ratio`, one of Code on a page named `Code`, four of Table on a page named `Table`, one of Attachment on a page named `Attachment`, one of Description list item and one of Description list on a page named `Description list`, one of Checkbox card on a page named `Checkbox card`, one of List detail pane and one of List detail on a page named `List detail`, one of Data table filter bar on a page named `Data table filter`, one of Tour popup and one of Tour step on a page named `Tour`, one of Toast on a page named `Toast`, and an icon library on a page named `Icons`. It edits them in place on every later run, and binds each field it can to the variables `figma:sync` writes.

- The `Icons` frame holds one 16px component per icon `@elmeragroup/fuse/icons` publishes that takes the color of the text around it: the 130 Phosphor icons in their bold default weight and the 5 bespoke icons that paint with `currentColor`, and `CheckCircleFill`, `CheckCircle` in its solid `fill` weight, which Checkbox card's checked icon renders. The library draws each weight a manifest's icon names (`weightedIcons` in `components/icon-library.ts`). The other bespoke icons are artwork with colors of their own, such as the Vipps mark and the Signing illustration, which read as blots at 16px or vanish on a light fill, so they stay out with the brand logos.
- `Button` holds every variant the recipe declares at the default, xs, sm and lg sizes, in 5 states (rest, hover, focus, disabled and pending) and with no icon, a start icon or an end icon: 420 variants. Its `Label` text property sets the label, and its `Start icon` and `End icon` swap properties pick the icons, Plus and ArrowRight to begin with.
- `Icon button` holds every variant at the four square sizes in the same 5 states, 140 variants, each an icon with no label, which its `Icon` swap property picks.
- `icon-inline` stays out, because it takes its size from the surrounding text line.
- `Confirm button` holds the destructive and success variants, the two `confirmButtonVariants` gives an armed face, at the default, xs, sm and lg sizes, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled), armed off and on, never armed while disabled: 56 variants. Its `armed` property sits beside `state`. A disarmed cell draws the Button of its variant, size and state. An armed one fills with `error` or `success` and draws its label in `error-foreground` or `success-foreground` inside the `/20` border. `data-[armed=true]:` weighs (0,2,0) and `enabled-hover:` (0,1,0), so on hover an armed button keeps its fill and only its border turns to the role. Disabling a ConfirmButton disarms it, so the set holds no armed disabled cell. Pending disarms it as well, and the set draws no pending state. Its `Label` text property sets the label, `Delete` to begin with, the resting label of the docs' Destructive demo. The demos swap in a label while armed, such as `Confirm delete`, and a designer types it on an armed instance, since a text property holds one default. The set has no icons, since no docs demo places one on a ConfirmButton with a label.
- `Toggle` holds both variants the recipe declares at the default, xs, sm and lg sizes, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled), off and on, and with no icon, a start icon or an end icon: 192 variants. Its `pressed` property sits beside `state`. Its `Label` text property sets the label, and its `Start icon` and `End icon` swap properties pick the icons, Star on both sides to begin with, as the docs place it.
- `Icon toggle` holds the same variants, sizes, states and pressed values, 64 variants, each one icon with no label, which its `Icon` swap property picks. The icon carries `data-icon="inline-start"`, as in the docs, so it takes the start icon's inset.
- `Toggle group` holds the docs Multiple demo's spaced group, Bold pressed, Italic and Underline, at the Toggle's 2 variants and 4 sizes: 8 variants. The root hugs a row of exposed `Toggle` instances of the group's variant and size, centred across. Its gap is `--spacing(var(--gap))`, which reads the `--gap: 2` ToggleGroup writes in the root's inline style, so the items sit 8px apart at every size and density, as Chromium measured. The group's `item` classes paint only in the segmented look or on a focused item, so each instance draws as its Toggle variant with no override. The segmented look, `spacing={0}`, stays out: its items take the icon inset and outline items drop their left border, overrides of a nested instance's padding and stroke weight that Figma does not take, and the set omits its `data-[spacing=0]` arms with that reason.
- `Badge` holds every variant the recipe declares at the default, sm and lg sizes: 57 variants. A Badge is not interactive, so the set has no state axis, and its columns are the sizes. Its `Label` text property sets the label. It has no icons, since no docs demo places one.
- `Kbd` holds a text cap and an icon cap: 2 variants. The recipe declares no axes, so the set's one property, `content`, says what the cap holds, `text` or `icon`, and its columns are those values. Its `Label` text property sets the text cap's label, `K` to begin with. Its `Icon` swap property picks the icon cap's 12px icon, Backspace to begin with, because the roster has no Command icon.
- `Kbd group` holds the docs Combination demo's first combination, `Ctrl` and `K`: 1 variant. The recipe declares no axes, so the set's one property is `keys`, with the one value `2`. `KbdGroup`'s `group` slot lays the caps out in a row 4px apart on `radius-sm`. Each cap is an exposed instance of the `Kbd` text cap, the first with its `Label` set to `Ctrl`, so a designer retypes either from the group's panel. The group's `*:data-[slot=kbd]:rounded-[inherit]` gives each cap the group's `rounded-sm`, the cap's own corner, so the instances override nothing.
- Radio icon button, Radio, Checkbox, Switch, Input, Textarea and Number field draw `state=invalid-focus`, the invalid cell with focus forced, where the 3px `error/20` ring grows outside a 1px `error` offset line, `ring-offset-1 ring-offset-error`.
- `Radio icon button` holds the recipe's five square sizes, icon, icon-xxs, icon-xs, icon-sm and icon-lg, off and on, in 6 states (rest, hover, focus, disabled, invalid and invalid focus): 60 variants. Its `checked` property sits beside `state`. Each variant is one icon with no label, which its `Icon` swap property picks, List to begin with, as the docs demo places it. icon-xxs and icon-xs share the xs square and differ by icon size, 12px and 14px. The invalid state draws the `error` border and ring of a radio inside an invalid field.
- `Radio` holds the circle off and on, in 5 states (rest, focus, disabled, invalid and invalid focus): 10 variants. The recipe has no hover face. Its `checked` property sits beside `state`. The circle is 16px at both densities. A checked circle fills with `primary` and holds the 16px `radio-group-indicator` frame, which centers the 8px `primary-foreground` dot. An off circle has no indicator, as Base UI mounts it only while checked. The invalid state draws the `error` ring, and the border stays `primary` only on a checked circle.
- `Radio row` holds `Radio`, the labelled row, hugging or filling, off and on in Radio's 4 states: 16 variants. The root is Base UI's `field-item` row around `Field.Label`, which lays out a `Radio` instance in the cell's checked value and state and the content, `Fixed` in 14/20px type, which the `Label` text property sets, 8px apart and centred. A disabled row dims the content to 50% through `peer-data-disabled:`, and an invalid one turns its text `error`, the color an invalid group's Field root passes down. A horizontal group lays its rows out at their own width, so `width=hug` hugs; a vertical one stretches them, so `width=fill` is 384px. There the label and its content fit their text to the room, so both draw as fills and a longer label wraps at the row's end, as Chromium wraps it. Base UI's visually hidden radio `<input>` draws no layer.
- `Radio group` holds `RadioGroup` with the docs Basic demo's legend, `Contract`, and its options, `Fixed` selected, and the Pending demo's description, vertical and horizontal, at rest, disabled and invalid: 6 variants. The root is the `Field.Root` around FieldFrame's legend mode, a `Field.Set` whose parts sit `surface-gap-md` apart. The legend row holds a `Field legend` instance at its `label` variant, and the description, a `Field description` instance, sits flush under it, its negative top margin cancelling the gap, so the two draw in a frame with no gap. The group primitive stacks three `Radio row` instances at `width=fill` `surface-gap-sm` apart, or lays them at `width=hug` in a row `surface-gap-lg` apart, which Chromium measured on one line at both densities. The invalid cells add a `Field error` instance, `Pick a contract.`, after the group, and turn the legend `error`. `RadioItemGroup`, the stacked-card variant, is left out.
- `Checkbox` holds the box unchecked, checked and indeterminate, in 5 states (rest, focus, disabled, invalid and invalid focus): 15 variants. The recipe has no hover face. Its `checked` and `indeterminate` properties, Checkbox's props, sit beside `state`, and no cell is both checked and indeterminate, which Base UI draws as indeterminate. The box is 16px at both densities. A checked or indeterminate box fills with `primary` and holds the `checkbox-indicator` frame around a 14px `Check` or `Minus`, which the state picks, so the set has no swap property. An unchecked box has no indicator, as Base UI mounts it only while checked or indeterminate. The invalid state draws the `error` ring, and the border stays `primary` only on a checked box.
- `Switch` holds the track at the default and sm sizes, off and on, in 5 states (rest, focus, disabled, invalid and invalid focus): 20 variants. The recipe has no hover face. Its `size` and `checked` properties sit beside `state`. The track is 32px by 18.4px at default and 24px by 14px at sm, at both densities. It holds the `switch-thumb` frame, a transparent 16px or 12px slot, which centers a `dot` frame. Off, the track is a `muted-foreground` outline, and the slot sits at the start and holds a 12px or 9px `muted-foreground` dot. On, the track fills with `primary`, and the slot sits flush with the end and holds a 16px or 12px `background` dot. The invalid state draws the `error` border and ring.
- `Separator` holds a horizontal and a vertical line: 2 variants. A Separator is not interactive, so the set has no state axis, and its columns are the orientations. The line is 1px thick and fills with `border`. The recipe takes the line's length from its container, which the kit does not draw, so each cell passes a docs demo's length as consumer classes, merged over the recipe as Separator merges its `className`: `data-horizontal:w-64` makes the horizontal line 256px long, and `data-vertical:h-4 data-vertical:self-auto` makes the vertical one 16px.
- `Skeleton` holds a line and a circle: 2 variants. A Skeleton is not interactive, so the set has no state axis, and its columns are the shapes. The block fills with `muted`. Its pulse drops, as Figma components do not animate. The recipe gives no size, so each cell passes a docs demo's shape as consumer classes, merged over the recipe as Skeleton merges its `className`: `h-4 w-48` makes a 192 × 16 line on the `radius-md` corner, and `size-10 rounded-full` makes a 40px circle, whose `rounded-full` replaces the recipe's `rounded-md`.
- `Loader` holds the spinner at the default, small, medium, large and xl sizes: 5 variants. A Loader has no state axis, and its columns are the sizes. The box hugs a `SpinnerGap` in the `foreground` role with 16px of padding on every side. The spinner is 16, 12, 24, 32 or 40px, so the boxes are 48, 44, 56, 64 and 72px square. Its spin drops, as Figma components do not animate, and the visually hidden label is not drawn.
- `Label` holds the label a form shows above its control, at rest and disabled: 2 variants. Fuse has no standalone Label, so the set draws `Field.Label` from the `label` slot of `fieldVariants`. Its `Label` text property sets the text, `Email` to begin with, as the docs' field label. The label hugs its text, set in the `label-text` and `label-leading` type pair at Medium, which is 14/20px dense and 16/24px comfortable, in the `foreground` the page sets. Disabled draws at 50% opacity. The classes for a label that wraps a Checkbox or a Field card drop, since that is Field composition, which waits for wave 3. The set sat on a page named `Label` of its own until Field's text parts joined it.
- `Field description` holds the copy below a control: 1 variant. It draws the same in every state of its Field, so the set's one property is `state`, with the one value `rest`. `Field.Description` is a `<p>`, a block box, so it fills the 384px the Field gives Input and Textarea and wraps its text there, its height following its lines. The text is the label type pair at Regular in `muted-foreground`, its lines at the left. Its `Text` text property sets it, `Work address preferred.` to begin with, the docs' Basic demo's description. The set draws the description alone, so its margins after a legend and as the Field's last part drop. So do the balanced lines it takes in a Field that holds a horizontal Field, and the underline of a link inside it.
- `Field error` holds the message an invalid Field shows: 1 variant. Its one property is `state`, with the one value `invalid`, the Field it shows in. `Field.Error` is a `<div>`, so it fills the Field's 384px and wraps, in the label type pair at Regular in `error`. Its `Text` text property sets it, `Enter a work email.` to begin with, the docs' Error message demo's error.
- `Field legend` holds a fieldset's legend in its `legend` and `label` variants: 2 variants. `Field.Legend` is a `<div>`, so it fills the Field set's 384px and wraps, at Medium in the page's `foreground`. The `legend` variant sets Tailwind's `text-base`, 16/24px, and the `label` variant the label type pair. Its `Text` text property sets it, `Notifications` to begin with, the docs' Field set demo's legend. Its `mb-3` spaces it from the fields below it, which the set does not draw, so it drops.
- `Field title` holds a choice's heading at rest and disabled: 2 variants. It hugs its text in the label type pair at Medium in the page's `foreground`, with Label's 8px gap, and a disabled Field dims it to 50%, as it dims Label. Its `Text` text property sets it, `Fixed price` to begin with, the docs' Choice card demo's title.
- `Input` holds the field with a placeholder and with a typed value, in 6 states (rest, focus, disabled, read-only, invalid and invalid focus): 12 variants. The recipe has no hover face. Its `content` property sits beside `state`. The box is 384px wide, the docs' `max-w-sm`, at both densities, and `control-h-md` tall, with the `control-px-md` padding on each side. It rounds with the field box corner `radius-field-box`, strokes 1px with `input`, fills with `card` and casts `shadow-xs`. Its `Text` text property sets the text, `name@example.com` to begin with, as the docs' placeholder. The text fills the row between the paddings in the `control-text` and `control-leading` pair at Regular, centered down the box, and ends in an ellipsis after one line. A placeholder draws in `muted-foreground` and a value in `foreground`. Focus draws the 2px `ring`. Disabled draws at 50% opacity on the `input/50` fill, and read-only fills with `muted`. Invalid draws the `error` border and its 3px `error/20` ring, and a value there draws in `error`, the text color an invalid Field passes down.
- `Input group` holds the forms of `InputGroup` that Combobox and Command render, in 4 states (rest, focus, disabled and invalid): 16 variants. It sits on the Input page. `form=caret` is `Combobox.Input`, `form=clear` is that input with `showClear` and no trigger, `form=search` is `Command.Input`, and `form=caret-open` is `Combobox.Input` while its popup is open, whose caret draws CaretUp: Base UI marks the open trigger, and the CaretDown's `in-data-popup-open:rotate-180` turns it half a turn, which Figma cannot do inside an instance (PT). The matrix draws the open form disabled too: a Combobox opened through `open` or `defaultOpen` stays open when it is disabled. The box is Input's 384px field box at `control-h-md`, with no padding of its own: Input's corner, stroke, fill, shadow and state faces, which the root takes from its own control through `has-[>[data-focus-ring-control]…]:`. The control fills the room its addon leaves and the field's content height, padded `control-px-md` on its free side and 6px beside the addon, and its `Text` property holds the placeholder `Search…` in `muted-foreground`. Combobox's trailing addon pads its end by `addon-px-button` and holds the caret or the clear button, an `Icon button` instance of the `ghost` variant at `icon-sm` with CaretDown or X, rounded with `radius-inner/field-border-pad-addon-button` and its glyph in `muted-foreground`. Inside an invalid Field Base UI marks the caret invalid too. One caret layer serves every cell, and the invalid cells' instance overrides Button's `error` stroke and ring. Command's leading addon follows the control in the DOM and draws first, padded `control-px-icon-md` at its start and 6px down, holding MagnifyingGlass at 16px. Block addons, a textarea, a kbd and `aria-disabled` controls drop with their reasons, since neither Combobox nor Command renders them. The read-only fill drops too, a gap #524 tracks.
- `Text field` holds `TextField` as the docs Basic demo renders it, in its default variant, holding the placeholder or a value in Input's 5 states: 10 variants. The root is the `Field.Root` with FieldFrame's part spacing, a 384px column whose children sit 4px apart. The heading row fills the column and holds an exposed `Label` instance, `Email`. TextField's content wrapper, a column whose children sit 4px apart, holds the input's `relative` container, the error in the invalid cells and the description. The container is a bare `<div>` around the `<input>`, an inline-block as wide as it, so it draws as a column that the exposed `Input` instance fills at the control height; Chromium measured the container exactly as wide and tall as its input at both densities. The `Input` instance follows the cell's content and state, and the description `Work address preferred.` and the error `Enter a work email.` come from `field-frame-kit.ts`, with the label's `error` color in the invalid cells. The card variant waits for a Card set. The inline variant is left out: its `fieldGroup` gives the input `w-auto`, so the input takes the width its `size` attribute and the font's character widths give it, 168px dense and 217px comfortable in Chromium, which no Figma length holds.
- `Textarea` holds the field with a placeholder and with a typed value, in Input's 6 states: 12 variants. Its `content` property sits beside `state`. The box is 384px wide at both densities and draws Input's corner, border, fill, shadow and state faces, with the `control-px-md` padding on each side and 8px above and below. `field-sizing-content` makes its height hug the text, and `min-h-16` keeps it at least 64px tall. Its `Text` text property sets the text, `Tell us more…` to begin with, as the docs' placeholder, and the value cells show it on one line too. The text fills the row between the paddings from the top, in the `control-text` and `control-leading` pair at Regular, and wraps, its height following its lines. It draws a placeholder, a value and an invalid value in Input's colors.
- `Textarea field` holds `TextareaField` as the docs Basic demo renders it, in Textarea's 5 states: 5 variants. The root is the `Field.Root`, a 384px column whose children sit 4px apart. The heading row fills the column and spreads its children with an 8px gap: an exposed `Label` instance, `Bio`, and the character counter, `0/120` in 12/16px `muted-foreground`, which the `Count` text property sets. The counter sits behind the `Counter` BOOLEAN property, which starts off, since the Basic demo sets no `maxLength`. An exposed `Textarea` instance in the cell's state follows, holding the placeholder `Write a short bio.`, and an exposed `Field description` instance, `Shown to other users.`, closes the column. The invalid cell adds an exposed `Field error` instance, `Keep it under 120 characters.`, between the textarea and the description, as Base UI renders the error only in an invalid Field. There the Field passes its `error` text color down, and the Label instance carries it as an override. A disabled Field dims the label through its disabled variant. Base UI stamps the Field's state on the label and the description as data attributes. The label and the description are one instance layer each, and a cell that carries a stamp holds what the stamp draws differently as overrides on that layer.
- `Avatar` holds the initials and a picture at 32, 24, 40 and 48px: 8 variants. An Avatar is not interactive, so the set has no state axis, and its columns are the sizes, the recipe's 32px first. Its `content` property is `fallback` or `image`. The root is a `muted` circle that clips what it holds. Each cell passes the docs size demo's `size-8`, `size-6`, `size-10` or `size-12` as consumer classes, merged over the recipe's `size-8` as Avatar merges its `className`. The `avatar-fallback` frame fills the circle and centers the initials, `AL` to begin with, in the 14/20px Medium `muted-foreground` text the root sets. Its `Label` text property sets them. The label holds initials and draws them on one line, where a browser would wrap a longer name and the circle would clip it. The `avatar-image` frame fills the circle with the docs portrait as an image paint at FILL, which covers the circle and crops what overflows, as `object-cover` does. A designer replaces the picture by changing that fill on an instance.
- `Avatar group` holds the docs Group demo's three avatars: 1 variant. The recipe declares no axes, so the set's one property is `avatars`, with the one value `3`. Each avatar is an exposed instance of the 32px fallback avatar, whose `Label` shows the person's initials, `AL`, `GH` and `KJ`. The demo's avatars hold pictures, but an instance property cannot set the image avatar's picture, so each shows its fallback. The group's `-space-x-2` draws as an item spacing of -8, each avatar 8px over the one before and the later on top. Its `[:where(&>*)]:ring-2 [:where(&>*)]:ring-background` gives each instance the 2px `background` ring as a shadow override, which the avatar's `muted` fill and clipping let draw (PR2).
- `Meter` holds the 4 modes, default, inverted, success-only-when-full and neutral, at the docs Modes demo's 4 levels: 16 variants. A Meter is not interactive, so the set has no state axis, and its columns are the levels: `low` at 50 of 100, `medium` at 85, `full` at 100, and `exceeded` at 130 of 120, which Base UI clamps to a full bar. The cell takes its tone and icon from `meterToneCell`, the table Meter reads, so a designer picks what the meter means and how full it is. The root is a column with a 4px gap, 256px wide, as `w-64` would make it, since no docs demo gives a Meter a width. Its header, a `<div>` with no `data-slot` named `header`, fills the width and spaces the label and the value apart, at least 8px. The label `Storage used` is 14/20 Medium in `foreground`, and its `Label` text property sets it. The value is the level's share, `50%`, `85%`, `100%` or `100%`, at 14/20 Regular in the tone's color, per cell with no property, since a text property holds one default. The tone's Warning or CheckCircle icon sits before it, one space away: 3.47px, Roboto's space at 14px. The bar fills the width, a 6px `muted` pill. The fill is an absolute layer at the bar's top left, the level's share of 256px wide and 6px tall in the tone's color, which keeps its share of the bar and the bar's height when a designer stretches an instance.
- `Pagination link` holds a page number at Button's four square sizes and four label sizes, current off and on, in 3 states (rest, hover and focus): 48 variants. Its `size` and `isActive` properties, the props of `Pagination.Link`, sit beside `state`. `size` is an added axis, so an `icon` link keeps the key it had before the set drew sizes. `icon-inline` stays out, as it does from Icon button. `Pagination.Link` is an `<a>` with Button's recipe at its `size`, `icon` by default, so each square cell is the Icon button box of its variant, size and state, and each label cell the Button box, with the 4px gap of `paginationVariants`. A link that is not current is the ghost square, and the current one is the outline square, as `aria-current="page"` marks it. A square link sets no type, so its number takes the host page's: the browser's 16px at Tailwind preflight's 1.5 line height, 24px. A label-size link takes the type of Button's control at its size, as the edge at the same size does: 14/20px dense and 18/24px comfortable at `default`. Its `Label` text property sets the number, `1` to begin with, the current page of the docs' Basic demo. An `<a>` has no disabled state and Pagination renders no pending one, so the set draws neither.
- `Pagination edge` holds the previous and next links at Button's four label sizes in the same 3 states: 24 variants. Its `size` and `direction` properties sit beside `state`. `size` is an added axis, so a `default` edge keeps the key it had before the set drew sizes. The edge also takes Button's square sizes, which the set leaves out. Each is the ghost Button at its label size, `default` by default, with the 4px gap, a 16px CaretLeft before the copy or CaretRight after it, and 10px of padding on the caret's side from `pl-2.5` or `pr-2.5`. The copy is the en-US default, `Previous` or `Next`, per cell with no property, since a text property holds one default. The component holds it in a bare `<span>`, which the kit draws as the root's own text. The carets are fixed, so the set has no swap property. Until #858's Button label floor (PR #900) is on the same base, Figma draws less padding than Chromium in branded dense modes on both sides of a label-size link's number and on the copy side of Previous and Next.
- `Pagination ellipsis` holds the md control square around a 16px DotsThree in the page's `foreground`: 1 variant. The recipe declares no axes, so the set's one property is `state`, with the one value `rest`, as Kbd's `content` gives its axis-less cap a property. Its copy, `More pages`, is visually hidden, so the set draws no text.
- `Pagination row` holds the docs Basic demo's row, previous, the links to pages 1 to 3 with 1 current, and next: 1 variant. Its one property is `pages`, with the one value `3`. `Pagination.Root` is the root, a column that centers `Pagination.Content`, a row that spaces its items 4px apart. Each `Pagination.Item` carries no class and hugs an instance: the previous and next edges at `default`, and the page links at `icon`, which are exposed, with `isActive=true` on page 1 and `Label` set to `2` and `3`. The root's `w-full` sizes it as its unset width, so it hugs the row, and an instance a designer sets to fill keeps the row centered, as `items-center` does. Its `mx-auto` drops with the root's other margins, and its `space-y-4` drops, since the root holds one child. The root is in flow with no preview width, so nothing bounds it, and the row hugs its items inside it, so the row's `max-w-full` and `flex-wrap` drop. A Figma row never wraps, so an instance a designer narrows below the row clips it where the browser would wrap it.
- `Breadcrumb link` holds a link in 3 states (rest, hover and focus): 3 variants. `Breadcrumb.Link` is an `<a>` that hugs its text, which its `Label` text property sets, `Home` to begin with, the first link of the docs' Basic demo. The text is Regular in the 14/20px type and the `muted-foreground` that `Breadcrumb.List` sets, and turns `foreground` on hover. Focus draws the 2px `ring` around the link.
- `Breadcrumb page` holds the current page: 1 variant. The page has no states, so the set's one property is `state`, with the one value `rest`. `aria-current="page"` marks it. Its `Label` text property sets the text, `Breadcrumb` to begin with, the current page of the docs' Basic demo. The page draws in the list's type, in `foreground` at Regular from its own `text-foreground font-normal`.
- `Breadcrumb ellipsis` holds the 20px box around a 16px DotsThree in the list's `muted-foreground`: 1 variant. Its one property is `state`, with the one value `rest`. Its copy, `More`, is visually hidden, so the set draws no text.
- `Breadcrumb separator` holds the CaretRight a separator draws when it is given no children, 14px in the list's `muted-foreground`: 1 variant. Its one property is `state`, with the one value `rest`. The separator sizes a consumer's own icon to 14px too, and text such as the docs' `/` takes the list's type. The chevron is fixed, so the set has no swap property.
- `Breadcrumb trail` holds the docs Basic demo's trail, `Home`, `Quick start` and the current page: 1 variant, its one property `state=rest`. Its `Ellipsis` BOOLEAN property, which starts false, shows the Ellipsis demo's ellipsis after `Home` and the separator after it. Captured with and without them, the trail's other boxes keep every style, so the two layers hide behind a property rather than an axis, and the hidden state draws the old `ellipsis=false` variant layer for layer. `Breadcrumb.List` is the root, a row that spaces its entries 10px apart from `sm:gap-2.5`, since every desktop width holds `sm:`. Each entry is a `Breadcrumb.Item` row around an instance of a part's set: the links and the page, which are exposed, the second link with `Label` set to `Quick start`, and the ellipsis, with a separator instance between each two. The list hugs its entries and is an in-flow root with no preview width, so nothing bounds it and its `flex-wrap` drops, and the texts of its links and page hug it too, so its `wrap-break-word` drops at each of them. A Figma row never wraps, so an instance a designer narrows below the trail clips it where the browser would wrap it.
- `Select trigger` holds the trigger hugging its value or filling its container, at the default and sm sizes in 5 states (rest, focus, disabled, invalid and readonly), holding the placeholder or a value, closed and open: 80 variants. `Select.Trigger` is a `<button>` that draws the field box chrome at the size's control height, inset and gap. At `width=hug`, the recipe's `w-fit`, it hugs its value and its caret and packs them at its start with the size's gap between them, since a box that hugs its layers leaves `justify-between` no room to spread. At `width=fill` it takes the `w-full` SelectField passes it and fills the 384px preview width, the value's `flex-1` grows into the room the caret and the gap leave, and the caret sits at the end. The width axis comes first, so the hugging cells keep their rows, and it is an added axis, so they keep their keys too. A composite whose trigger fills, such as Select field, shows the fill variant, since an instance cannot change its inner root's packing or its parts' sizing. The value is a part, `Select.Value`, whose `Value` text property sets the text in every cell, `Pick a fruit` to begin with, the placeholder of the docs' Basic demo. The placeholder paints `muted-foreground`, a value the page's `foreground`, and an invalid value the `error` of the Field root around the trigger. The caret is a fixed 16px CaretDown in `muted-foreground`, which an open cell turns half a turn. A read-only cell sets the root's `readOnly`, and the trigger's `data-readonly` fill binds `muted` in place of `card`. Base UI's `readOnly` blocks only a commit, so a read-only Select still opens, and Chromium renders the read-only open cell with `aria-expanded="true"`, which the set draws. The popup and its rows wait for the overlay sets.
- `Select field` holds `SelectField` as the docs Basic demo renders it, in 5 states (rest, focus, disabled, invalid and readonly), holding the placeholder or a value: 10 variants. The read-only cell sets `isReadOnly`, and its trigger instance takes the read-only trigger's `muted` fill. The root is the `Field.Root` with FieldFrame's part spacing, a 384px column whose children sit 4px apart. The heading row fills the column and holds an exposed `Label` instance, `Plan`, which `justify-between` packs at its start, as it packs a lone layer. An exposed `Select trigger` instance at `width=fill` follows in the cell's state and content, its `Value` set to the placeholder `Choose a plan` or the Form demo's selected plan, `Pro`, and an exposed `Field description` instance, `You can change plan later.`, closes the column. The invalid cell adds an exposed `Field error` instance, `Choose a plan to continue.`, between the trigger and the description. Base UI's visually hidden form `<input>` after the trigger draws no layer. The label and description instances, one layer each in every cell, and the error instance, which only the invalid cell shows, come from `field-frame-kit.ts`, which Textarea field shares. The invalid cell carries the error text color on the label as an override.
- `Menu group label` holds the caption of a menu's group, which every menu family shares: 3 variants. Its one property is `kind`. `plain` is Select's `Select.Label` in a `Select.Group`, `text-xs` inset by `row-px` across and 6px down in `muted-foreground`. `menu` is Dropdown menu's `DropdownMenu.Label`, which adds `font-medium`, and `menu-inset` is that label with `inset`, which pads its start by `row-indicator-room`, the row inset plus the 16px indicator and its 8px gap. Each is 136px wide, as the Groups demo's 144px popup less 4px on each side gives it. The root names its element by the `-label` its `data-slot` ends with, since each family names the slot its own way. A TEXT property, `Label`, holds the demo's `Citrus`.
- `Menu separator` holds the hairline between a menu's groups: 1 variant, its one property `orientation`, Select.Separator's prop, with the one value `horizontal`. It is `Select.Separator`, 1px of `border` with 4px above and below and side margins of minus `surface-pad-sm`, which cancel a popup's padding. Alone it sits in a 136px container, a 144px popup that pads 4px across, so its margins widen it to 144px.
- `Select item` holds a row of the open Select, selected off and on, at rest, highlighted and disabled: 6 variants. It is `Select.Item`, the shared menu row 144px wide in an ungrouped popup, with Base UI's `ItemText` filling it and, while selected, the Check in the end room the row's padding keeps, drawn in flow. A highlighted row has DOM focus, which `focus:` paints `accent`. A TEXT property, `Label`, holds the Basic demo's `Apple`.
- `Select popup` holds the open popup with the docs Basic demo's rows or its Groups demo's groups: 2 variants, its one property `content` (`flat`, `grouped`). Base UI's listbox holds the rows, each an exposed instance of `Select item` with nothing selected, and in the grouped cell the captions and the separator are instances of the menu sets. The popup is the shared surface, `bg-popover`, `shadow-md` and a 1px `foreground/10` ring, rounded `radius-lg`, 144px wide, its minimum, since every demo's trigger is narrower. A group pads its rows by `surface-pad-sm`, and each of its rows overrides its corner with the synced `radius-inner/lg-pad-sm`.
- `Select (open)` holds the open trigger and its popup below it, at both trigger sizes: 2 variants, its properties `side` (`bottom`), Select.Content's prop, and `size`. The column places the popup as Base UI does when it does not align the selected row over the trigger, the kit's choice at both densities (Q-M1): 4px below the trigger and centered on it. Its root clips nothing, so the popup's shadow shows past the column.
- `Popover content` holds the popup the docs Basic demo opens: 1 variant, its one property `content` (`basic`). The popup is the shared surface on the Popover shell, `popover` with `shadow-md` and a 1px `foreground/10` ring, rounded `radius-md`, padded `surface-pad-md`, 288px wide from `w-72`, its parts 16px apart. The header stacks the title, `Dimensions` at Medium, and the description, `Set the dimensions for the layer.` in `muted-foreground`, 4px apart in 14/20px type; its `Title` and `Description` properties set them. Two vertical Fields follow, `Width` and `Height`, each a column 4px apart of its label and an exposed Input instance at `content=value` whose `Text` is empty, since the demo's input holds neither a placeholder nor a value, filling the Field. The label is a frame with its own text in the label type pair at Medium, set by its `Width label` or `Height label` property, and not a `Label` instance: the Field stretches the label and its text fills it, and an instance cannot carry an inner layer's sizing. The set repeats Label's composition omissions and holds the Field enabled. `showArrow` draws the arrow as a rotated, bordered pseudo-element, and the demo shows none, so the set draws none.
- `Popover (open)` holds the outline Button trigger, `Details`, with the popup open below it: 1 variant, its one property `side` (`bottom`), Popover.Content's prop. The column places the popup as `Popover.Content` does by default, 4px below the trigger and centered on it. The trigger is an exposed Button instance that carries `Popover.Trigger`'s `data-slot` and the open trigger's `aria-expanded` and `data-popup-open`, so it draws the outline Button's expanded face, `aria-expanded:bg-muted`, as a fill override. Its root clips nothing, so the popup's shadow shows past the column.
- `Dropdown menu item` holds a row of the open menu, default and destructive, plain and inset, at rest, highlighted, focused and disabled: 16 variants. It is `DropdownMenu.Item`, the shared menu row 160px wide, rounded with `radius-inner/md-pad-sm`, holding an optional User icon, its text and a `DropdownMenu.Shortcut` after the text, which `ml-auto` ends the row with, so the text fills the room before it and the row packs at its start with its 8px gap. The shortcut is 12/16px type 0.1em apart in `muted-foreground`, `accent-foreground` while the row has focus. The `Icon` BOOLEAN property shows the icon and starts false, its `Icon glyph` swap property picks it, since one name serves one property, the `Shortcut` BOOLEAN property shows the shortcut and starts true, and the `Label` and `Shortcut keys` TEXT properties hold the Basic demo's `Profile` and `⇧⌘P`. The kit draws a shortcut's ⇧ and ⌘ in Inter and its letters in `font-sans`, in the row and in each popup row that sets its own keys. A shortcut a designer types into an instance resets to `font-sans`, where Figma draws ⇧ and ⌘ blank, so the designer sets those characters to Inter. A destructive row paints its text and icon `error` and its highlight the synced `error/10`; `*:[svg]:text-error` drops with its reason, since the icons take the row's `error` already. An inset row pads its start by `row-indicator-room`.
- `Dropdown menu checkbox item` and `Dropdown menu radio item` hold the checkable rows, checked off and on, in the same 4 states: 8 variants each, 160px wide, padded at the end by the indicator's room. Base UI mounts the Check only while the row is checked, inside the indicator `<span>` that sits first in the row, `absolute right-(--row-px)`, which the kit draws after the text with the row's end padded `row-px`. While unchecked the `<span>` waits empty and draws nothing. The radio row sits in its classless radio group. A TEXT property, `Label`, holds the demos' `Show toolbar` and `Status bar`.
- `Dropdown menu sub trigger` holds the row that opens a submenu, at rest, highlighted, focused, open and disabled: 5 variants. It is `DropdownMenu.SubTrigger`, the shared menu row 120px wide, as the docs Submenu demo's popup at its 128px minimum less 4px on each side gives it, rounded with the synced `radius-inner/md-pad-sm`, holding its text and a CaretRight that `ml-auto` ends the row with. A highlighted row has DOM focus, which `focus:` paints `accent`, a focused one adds the focus ring, and Base UI marks the trigger of an open submenu `data-popup-open`, which paints the same `accent`. The set omits `data-open:` with its reason, since only the popup around the row carries it. A TEXT property, `Label`, holds the demo's `Share`.
- `Dropdown menu popup` holds the open popup of each docs demo, `content` basic, checkboxes, radio, destructive and submenu: 5 variants. The demo's children are exposed instances of the row sets, the shared group label and the shared separator, with their labels, shortcuts and icons set as properties, and each group or radio group is a frame. The popup is the shared surface on the menu shell, `popover` with `shadow-md` and a 1px `foreground/10` ring, rounded `radius-md`, padding `surface-pad-sm`, at least 128px wide from `min-w-32`. A separator cancels the padding across, so the runs between separators sit in padded sections. CSS sizes the popup to its widest row past its minimum, so the row the docs demo draws widest at both densities (`Keyboard shortcuts`, `Show minimap`, `Activity bar`, `Profile` and `New window`) hugs with the group and section around it, and every other row, section and separator fills. That row keeps the popup's 120px content minimum, its `min-w-32` less `surface-pad-sm` on each side, as its own `minWidth`, so it fills to the minimum where its text fits and hugs past it (PH3).
- `Dropdown menu sub content` holds the Submenu demo's submenu: 1 variant, the same surface with `shadow-lg`, at least 96px wide, its three rows filling. Its widest row, `Messages`, hugs from the 88px content minimum: it fills to it at dense and hugs 0.23px past it at comfortable, where filling would wrap its text (PH3).
- `Dropdown menu (open)` holds the outline Button trigger, `Open`, with the Basic demo's popup below it: 1 variant, its one property `side` (`bottom`), DropdownMenu.Content's prop. Base UI places the popup 4px below the trigger, aligned to its start, so the column packs both at its start. The trigger carries a menu trigger's `aria-expanded`, `data-popup-open` and `data-pressed`, so it draws the outline Button's expanded face as a fill override. Its root clips nothing, so the popup's shadow shows. The submenu form stays out: the sub content's top sits 2 × `row-h` + 10px below the popup's, which no variable holds, and Figma binds no absolute `y`.
- `Combobox item` holds a row of the open popup, selected off and on, at rest, highlighted and disabled: 6 variants.
