It is `Combobox.Item`, the shared menu row with room at its end for the Check, 366px wide in the popup's list, which pads it by `surface-pad-sm` and rounds it with `radius-inner/md-pad-sm`. Its `Label` text property sets the text, which is the row's own and fills it. Base UI mounts the Check's `<span>` after the text only while the row is selected; it is `absolute right-(--row-px)` in the end room, which the kit draws in flow, the row then padding its end by `row-px`. Base UI marks a highlighted row `data-highlighted`, which paints it `accent`.
- `Combobox empty` holds the popup's `No results.` message, centered in `muted-foreground` and padded as a row: 1 variant, 374px wide. Its `Text` property sets the message.
- `Combobox popup` holds the open popup, `content` flat, grouped and empty: 3 variants, the docs Basic and Groups demos' rows as instances, and the empty message alone, since the list with no rows draws no layer. The popup is the shared surface, rounded `radius-md`, as wide as its `<input>` plus 28px. Base UI anchors the popup to the `<input>` inside the 384px Input group, which is 346px wide at dense, so the popup is 374px wide. Its list pads the rows and clips at 252px, where it scrolls in CSS. The separator between the groups spans the list. No popup holds a search field, as the docs Input group anchor demo's does.
- `Combobox (open)` holds the Input group at `form=caret-open` with the Basic demo's popup below it: 1 variant, its one property `side` (`bottom`), Combobox.Content's prop. Base UI places the popup 6px below the Input group, aligned to its start, and 28px wider than the `<input>`, so the 384px column holds the group filling it and the 374px popup, which ends 10px before the column's end. Its root clips nothing, so the popup's shadow shows.
- `Command item` holds a result row, `trailing` none, shortcut and caret, at rest, highlighted and disabled: 9 variants. It is `Command.Item`, the shared menu row 320px wide, a sample since a row fills its list, rounded with the synced `radius-inner/xl-gap-sm`, its text in the palette surface's `popover-foreground`. `trailing=shortcut` holds `Command.Shortcut` after the text as an exposed instance of `Kbd group`, and `trailing=caret` is `Command.PageTrigger` with its CaretRight. Both take the `trailing` slot, whose `ms-auto` ends the row, so the text fills the room before them. Base UI marks a highlighted row `data-highlighted`, which paints it `accent`, its text and caret `accent-foreground`; the caps keep their own muted face. The `Label` property sets the text. The shortcut shows the Kbd group's own caps, `Ctrl` and `K`.
- `Command list` holds the docs Groups demo's `Command.List`: 1 variant, its one property `content` (`grouped`). The demo renders `Command.Input` beside the list in the app's box, so the set is the list alone, 576px wide, `max-w-xl`, padding its rows by `surface-gap-sm` and clipping past 320px, `max-h-80`. Each `Command.Group` holds the shared `Menu group label` at `kind=plain` and its rows, exposed instances of `Command item` at `trailing=shortcut`, each shortcut's caps set to the demo's `Alt` and the row's letter as nested values. The palette dialog is the two sets below.
- `Command palette` holds the docs Dialog demo's `Command.Content`, open: 1 variant, its one property `content` (`commands`). It is fixed in a 640 × 500 viewport, so its `w-[calc(100%-2rem)] max-w-xl` is 576px and its `max-h-[calc(100dvh-2*max(--spacing(4),12dvh))]` 380px, which Chromium measured it under: 289px tall dense and 341px comfortable, so its `flex-1` list hugs. 500px is the shortest height that keeps the palette's 12dvh top at 60px and its cap above 341px with room to spare; 449px is the least that fits the comfortable palette at all. The popover surface, `radius-xl`, clipping, holds the `Input group` at `form=search`, `surface-gap-sm` in from the top and sides, an exposed instance whose `Text` is `Command.Input`'s en-US placeholder, `Type a command or search…`, and whose control carries the open `data-popup-open` and `data-pressed`; `Command.Empty`, which holds its message only while no row matches and so draws no layer; the list with the demo's six commands as `Command item` instances at `trailing=none`, all at rest, since the compare page clears the highlight Base UI puts on the first; and the footer, its hints `surface-gap-sm + row-px` in, bound to the `command-text-px` formula, 16px apart on one line: `↑ ↓ Navigate`, `↵ Run` and `Esc Close`, each key a `Kbd` instance.
- `Command dialog (open)` holds the palette over the scrim: 1 variant, `placement` `top`. The composite is `Dialog.Backdrop`'s scrim, 640 × 500, `black/10` with a background blur of 8, a column that pads its top by the palette's `max(1rem, 12dvh)`, 60px, and centres the palette's instance across, where Base UI's fixed `top` and `left-1/2 -translate-x-1/2` put it.
- `Empty` holds the placeholder a list or a page shows when it has nothing, in the recipe's default, outline and dashed outline frames: 3 variants. Its one property is `variant`, and its columns are the frames. `Empty.Root` is a block-level `flex flex-col` column, so it takes a 512px preview width, wide enough that the header's 384px cap binds inside the padding at both densities. It centers its header with a 24px gap and rounds with `radius-lg`. The kit draws a desktop window, so the padding is the `md:` arm's `2 * --surface-pad-lg`, which binds `empty-pad-desktop`, 32px dense and 48px comfortable. The outline frame strokes 1px with `border`, and the dashed outline draws the same stroke in 3px dashes and 2px gaps. The header is a column that centers its layers with an 8px gap and hugs them up to `max-w-sm`, 384px. It holds the docs Basic demo's media, title and description. The media is the recipe's `icon` box, a 40px `muted` square on `radius-lg` holding a 24px Tray in `foreground`, which the `Icon` swap property picks. Its `mb-2` is the 8px bottom padding of a frame around it, and the `Media` BOOLEAN property hides that frame, so the space goes with the media. The recipe's default media is a frameless box around an illustration, such as the docs' `FkasMeter`, which the kit does not ship, so the set draws the icon media only. The title, `No orders yet`, is 18/28 Medium in the page's `foreground` with `tracking-tight`. The description, `Orders you create will show up here.`, is 14/22.75 Regular in `muted-foreground`, behind the `Description` BOOLEAN property. Their `Title` and `Description text` properties set them, the second named apart from the BOOLEAN, since the plugin finds a property by its name. Both hug their text up to the header's 384px, where they wrap, and center their lines. The docs With actions demo's `Empty.Content` follows the header behind the `Actions` property, which starts false, so each variant draws the Basic demo and a designer turns the actions on: a column that fills the root's width up to 384px and centers a default and a secondary Button 16px apart, instances of Button at the default size, at rest and with no icon, exposed, whose `Label` values are `Create order` and `Import orders`. Their own classes draw them, and each instance centers its label with an override, since the root's `text-center` reaches it.
- `Link` holds Fuse's Link in 6 color variants at the always, hover and none underlines and the normal and bold weights, in 3 states (rest, hover and focus): 108 variants. Its `variant`, `underline` and `weight` properties sit beside `state`. `weight` is an added axis, so a `normal` link keeps the key it had before the set drew weights. `Link` renders an `<a data-slot="link">` whose classes are `linkVariants` at the cell's variant, underline and weight, and the page forces hover and focus on that anchor itself. A link has no disabled state. The set draws every variant the recipe declares. The recipe leaves out two of Text's colors whose class repeats another's: `secondary`, the deprecated alias of `foreground`, and `inherit`, which gives `default`'s `text-inherit`. The link hugs its text, which its `Label` text property sets, `invoice 1042` to begin with, the link of the docs Router links example. Link sets no type size, so the text takes the host page's 16/24px, as Pagination link's number does. A `normal` link's `font-normal` draws Regular, the host page's weight, which a link with no `weight` keeps too, and a `bold` link's `font-medium` draws Medium. `default` inherits the page's `foreground`, and the others draw in `foreground`, `primary`, `brand`, `muted-foreground` and `error`. The underline's offset and thickness are in `em`, which the build resolves at the 16px host size: 3.2px below the text and 1px thick. An `always` link rests its underline in the role tint `<color>/35` of its text color, such as `foreground/35`, the light arm of the recipe's `light-dark()`, as `readDecorationColor` in `components/label-layout.ts` explains. Hover draws the underline in the text's own color, Figma's `AUTO`, at the same 1px. A `hover` link draws the full-color underline only on hover. A `none` link never draws one and fades to 80% on hover through `enabled-hover:opacity-80`. Focus draws the 2px `ring`. The docs demos also set `truncate`, which cuts the text only where the link's container is narrower than the text, as the Truncate demo's `max-w-56` row is. A text in a hugging Figma frame keeps its own width past the frame's limit (P7), so the set leaves `truncate` off. The External link demo's icon is left off, so the set omits the icon selectors.
- `Item` holds the row in the recipe's default, outline and muted variants at the default, sm and xs sizes, without and with a description, with icon and image media: 36 variants. No docs demo shows `muted`, and the set keeps it, since the recipe declares it and its fill binds `muted/50`. Its columns are the sizes. `Item.Root` is a block-level row, so it takes a 384px preview width, on `radius-md` behind a 1px border: transparent in default and muted, `border` in outline. A default row pads with `surface-pad-md`, an sm row with `surface-pad-md` across and `row-px` down, and an xs row with `row-px` across and `row-py` down, and they space their layers with `surface-gap-md` or `surface-gap-sm`. The row holds the media, the content column and the actions, as the docs Basic demo does. Icon media holds a 16px Lightning in `foreground`, which the `Icon` swap property picks. Image media holds the kit's portrait, `components/portrait.png`, in a `radius-sm` square of 40, 32 or 24px by the row's size, under a 1px inside stroke of black at 10%. The recipe's `default` media holds whatever a consumer puts in it, as Empty's default media holds an illustration, so the set leaves it out. The `Media` and `Actions` BOOLEAN properties hide the media and the actions. The content column fills what they leave, with the title, `Spot`, at 14/19.25 Medium in `foreground`, and, where `described` is on, the description, `Hourly price with no lock-in.`, at 14/21 Regular in `muted-foreground`, or 12/18 in an xs row, two lines at most. The actions hold the demo's plain text, `Active`, at the row's 14/20. The `Title`, `Description` and `Actions text` properties set them. Beside a description the media and the content top-align, and icon media is one title line tall, 19.25px. The actions stretch across the row's height there and center their text, where CSS centers them on the row. No docs demo varies a row's state outside a link, so the set has no state axis. The header and the footer wait until kit components can hold instances, since the docs fill them with other components, and so does `Item.Group`.
- `Item link` holds the row rendered as an `<a>`, as the docs As a link demo renders it, in the default and outline variants, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled): 8 variants. It is the default size with icon media and a title, `Open order` to begin with, the demo's first row, and no description. Hover fills the row with `muted` through `[a]:enabled-hover:bg-muted`, focus draws the 2px `ring`, and a link marked `aria-disabled="true"`, as the demo marks its unavailable row, dims to 50%. A row rendered as a `<button>`, as the docs As a button demo renders it, draws the same focus ring and, through `:disabled`, the same 50% disabled face, and no hover fill, since `[a]:` reads only an `<a>`.
- `Alert` holds the alert's five tones, default, destructive, warning, success and info, without and with a description: 10 variants. Its columns are `described`. `Alert.Root` renders an Item row of the `outline` variant at the `sm` size, so the row takes a 512px preview width, the docs Variants demo's `max-w-lg` column, on `radius-md` behind a 1px border, and pads `surface-pad-md` across and `row-px` down with a `surface-gap-sm` gap. Each tone fills, strokes and colors the row: default `background`, `border` and `foreground`, destructive `error/5` and `error`, warning `warning-soft`, `warning` and `warning-soft-foreground`, success `success/5`, `success` and `foreground`, info `info-soft`, `info` and `info-soft-foreground`. The media holds the tone's 20px icon in the tone's color, Info, WarningOctagon, Warning, CheckCircle or Info, which the tone picks, so the set has no swap property. The title, `Scheduled maintenance`, is Medium and the description, `Read-only access tonight from 22:00 to 23:00.`, Regular, both in `label-text`, 14px dense and 16px comfortable, their lines 137.5% and 150% of it, in the tone's text color. The `Title` and `Description` properties set them. Both wrap across the content column, and the description shows every line, since Alert does not clamp it. The description's presence is the `described` axis, not a BOOLEAN property, since `item-described:` reads it: beside a description the media and the content top-align, and the media is one title line tall, the `label-line-snug` variable. The action is an instance of Button's `default` variant at `sm`, at rest and with no icon, in Item's actions, behind the `Action` property, which starts false, so each variant draws the alert as the docs Variants demo shows it and a designer turns the action on. Its label, the docs Action demo's `Retry`, is a value of Button's `Label`, and the instance is exposed, so a designer retypes it from the alert's panel. The tone's `button` slot overrides the Button's fill and label color: default `background` and `foreground`, destructive `error` and `error-foreground`, warning `warning` and `warning-foreground`, success `success` and `success-foreground`, info `info` and `info-foreground`. Beside a description the actions stretch down the row, so the Button stays centered while the media and the content top-align.
- `Accordion item` holds one item of the accordion in the default and card variants at the none, lg and xl radii, closed and open, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled): 48 variants. Its columns are the states. A designer stacks items into an accordion. `Accordion.Item` is a block `<div>`, so it takes a 384px preview width, and it stacks its header and, while open, its panel down it, each as wide as the item, inside `surface-pad-md`. The default item fills with `primary-soft` on `radius-sm`, or `radius-lg` and `radius-xl` at the larger radii, and the card item fills with `card` behind a 1px `card-border` stroke on `radius-lg`, or `radius-xl` at `xl`. The trigger, a `<button>` in the header's `<h3>`, fills the header and spaces the docs Basic demo's `Shipping` and a 16px CaretDown to its ends, in the host page's 16/24px at Medium and the item's text color, `primary-soft-foreground` in default and `foreground` in card. An open cell holds `group-data-[panel-open]/accordion-trigger`, which Base UI stamps on an open item's trigger, so the caret turns half a turn, and it shows the panel, which Base UI mounts only while the item is open. The panel clips and hugs its text, the demo's `Delivered within 3–5 business days.`, which wraps across the panel below `accordion-panel-pt`, `surface-gap-lg` plus 6px. In card the panel fills with `card` and rounds with the item's inner corner, `radius-inner/lg-border-pad-md` at none and lg and `radius-inner/xl-border-pad-md` at xl. The `Label` and `Content` properties set the two texts. A label too long to fit beside the caret wraps in the browser but stays on one line in Figma (#880). Hover underlines the label, focus rings the trigger 2px in the item's `--ring`, which the default item sets to `primary-soft-foreground`, over the clear fill and the clipping a part's ring needs, and a disabled item dims the trigger to 50%. The set keeps the trailing caret. The docs Leading indicator demo drops it for a leading caret the consumer passes as a child, and crossing `hasIndicator` would double the set to 96 variants. The `infodropdown` variant draws a bottom border alone, which needs per-side strokes, so it waits. A stacked accordion waits for kit components that hold instances.
- `Sidebar menu button` holds `Sidebar.MenuButton` in the recipe's default and outline variants at the default, sm and lg sizes, expanded and collapsed to the icon rail, inactive and active, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled): 96 variants. Its columns are the states. A designer stacks rows into a navigation panel or an icon rail. A row is 240px wide, the docs' 16rem Sidebar inside a group's dense 8px row inset. At the default size it is `row-h` tall, padded `row-px` across and 8px down, and at sm and lg it is 28px and 48px tall, padded 8px all round. It holds the docs demos' first row, a 16px House and `Dashboard` in a `<span>`, 8px apart, at 14/20, or 12/16 at sm, on `radius-md`. The `Label` property sets the text and the `Icon` swap property the icon. The label fills the room the row leaves it and ends in an ellipsis after one line, through `[&>span:last-child]:truncate`. The row draws in the `sidebar-foreground` it inherits from `Sidebar.Root`, which its definition names in `inheritedFor`. Outline fills with `background` inside a 1px `sidebar-border` ring, which turns `sidebar-accent` on hover. Hover and active fill with `sidebar-accent` under `sidebar-accent-foreground`, an active row sets its label at Medium, focus draws the 2px `ring`, and a row marked `aria-disabled="true"`, as the docs disable a row so its tooltip still opens, dims to 50%. `collapsed=true` holds `group-data-[collapsible=icon]`, which the collapsed rail stamps on `Sidebar.Root`, so `size-8!` and `p-2!` make every row the rail's 32px square padded 8px, and lg's `p-0!` takes the padding away. The icon keeps its place at the row's start, and the label gets no room, which a real file drew as the icon alone on 2026-10-23 (PC). At lg the unpadded square leaves the label an 8px sliver after the icon and the gap, where the real file drew a clipped "…" and Chromium draws a clipped "D", so the 16 lg collapsed cells differ by that sliver in a comparison. The set leaves out three arms. A row beside a menu action reserves room for the action at its end through `group-has-data-[slot=sidebar-menu-action]/menu-item:`, and that arm lands with the menu action in wave 6, since a cell now would reserve room for a layer the set does not draw, and the default row's `calc(max(var(--control-h-xs),24px)+0.5rem)` would need a density formula variable. A floating Sidebar rounds its rows with an inner corner, which no docs demo shows. A row that opens a dropdown menu holds `data-open:`, which no docs demo shows either.
- `Sidebar menu sub button` holds `Sidebar.MenuSubButton`, an `<a>`, at its md and sm sizes, inactive and active, in 4 states: 16 variants. A sub row is 191px wide, a menu row less the sub menu's 14px margins, 10px padding and 1px border. It is `control-h-md` tall with the control type pair at md, and `control-h-sm` tall at 14/20 at sm, padded 8px across. It holds the rich menu demo's `Open` in `sidebar-foreground`, which the `Label` property sets, and ends it in an ellipsis as a menu row does. Hover and active fill with `sidebar-accent` under `sidebar-accent-foreground`, focus draws the 2px `ring`, and a sub row marked `aria-disabled="true"` dims to 50%. The collapsed rail hides sub rows, so the set has no collapsed cells. A sub row's `-translate-x-px` moves it back over the sub menu's 1px border, which the set does not draw, so it drops. The sub menu's border and margins wait for wave 6.
- `Sidebar group label` holds `Sidebar.GroupLabel`: 1 variant. It is a 240 by 32px row padded `row-px` across, holding the docs demos' `Navigation` at 12/16 Medium in `sidebar-foreground/70`, which the `Label` property sets. The collapsed rail fades the label out and pulls the rows below it up over it, so the set has no collapsed cell. A `<div>` label takes no focus, so its focus ring drops.
- `Sidebar menu badge` holds `Sidebar.MenuBadge` at rest, beside a hovered menu button and beside an active one: 3 variants. It is 20px tall and at least 20px wide, hugging the rich menu demo's `12` at 12/16 Medium, padded 4px across on `radius-md`, which the `Count` property sets. It draws in `sidebar-foreground`, and in `sidebar-accent-foreground` beside a hovered or active menu button, which its `peer-hover/menu-button:` and `peer-data-active/menu-button:` arms read. Its offset, `absolute right-1` and a top that centers it on the menu button's row, places it inside the menu item, which this set does not hold, so the offset drops here, and `Sidebar menu item with badge` draws it. The collapsed rail hides the badge.
- `Sidebar group` holds `Sidebar.Group` with the `Navigation` label and the icon collapse demo's five rows in `Sidebar.GroupContent` and `Sidebar.Menu`, expanded and collapsed: 2 variants. Each row is an exposed `Sidebar menu button` instance in its `<li>`, filling the menu at `row-h`, `Orders` active, with the demo's label and icon as property values. The group is 256px wide, the docs' 16rem Sidebar, padded `row-px`, or 8px in the rail. Collapsed, the rows are the menu button's `collapsed=true` squares, and the label instance draws in no layer, since the rail fades it out and pulls the rows up over it.
- `Sidebar header` holds `Sidebar.Header` with the app frame demo's `Sidebar.Input`, expanded and collapsed: 2 variants. The input is an exposed `Input` instance showing `Search orders`, filling the header at `row-h`, with the `background` fill and no shadow as overrides. The header is 256px wide, padded `row-px`, or 8px in the rail.
- `Sidebar footer` holds `Sidebar.Footer` with a `Sidebar.Menu` of one large menu button, as the landing dashboard signs its seller in, expanded and collapsed: 2 variants. The row is an exposed `Sidebar menu button` instance at `lg`, 48px tall, or the rail's 32px square, with the User icon where the dashboard shows an avatar and the seller's name, `Reodor Felgen`.
- `Sidebar sub menu` holds the rich menu demo's `Sidebar.MenuSub`: 1 variant. It sits in a 240px menu row, 212px wide inside its `mx-3.5`, behind a 1px left border in `sidebar-border` that counts in the layout. Its three rows are exposed `Sidebar menu sub button` instances in their `<li>`s, 4px apart, filling it: `Open` active, `Shipped`, and `Archived (small)` at `sm`. Each row moves 1px left, so the paddings draw 9px on the left and 11px on the right, and 2px down. The sub menu's own `translate-x-px` drops, as a set root's translate does. The collapsed rail hides the sub menu, so the set has no collapsed cell.
- `Sidebar menu item with badge` holds the rich menu demo's first `Sidebar.MenuItem`, inactive and active: 2 variants. It is 240px wide and holds the `Orders` row as a menu button instance at `row-h` and the `12` badge as a badge instance, 4px from the item's end and centred on the row at both densities, MAX × CENTER. Its badge follows the row: at rest beside the inactive row and active beside the active one. The collapsed rail hides the badge, so the set has no collapsed cell, and its menu button stays at the default size, so the badge's `peer-data-[size=*]` offsets hold in no cell.
- `Sidebar` holds the desktop panel, `Sidebar.Root`'s `sidebar-container` and the `sidebar-inner` surface inside it, in the `sidebar` and `inset` variants, expanded and collapsed to the icon rail: 4 variants. The surface stacks the `Sidebar header`, a `Sidebar.Content` that holds the `Sidebar group` and a `Sidebar.Separator`, and the `Sidebar footer`, on the `sidebar` fill. Each section is an exposed instance of its section set that fills the surface across. The content takes the height the header and footer leave and clips. The container is 256px wide, or 48px in the rail: the Provider's style sets `--sidebar-width` and `--sidebar-width-icon`, which resolve to literals. The `sidebar` variant's 1px end border in `sidebar-border` counts in the layout, so the sections draw 255px wide, or 47px in the rail. The `inset` variant pads 8px on every side with no border, and its rail is 66px wide, so the sections draw 240px wide, or 50px in the rail. The 8px band shows the `sidebar` fill that the Provider's wrapper paints behind the panel. The separator is as wide as the content past its 8px start margin, since `separatorClass`'s `data-horizontal:w-full` beats `Sidebar.Separator`'s `w-auto`, so the content's clip cuts its end, and the hairline shows from 8px to the content's end. The panel is 448px tall, the app frame demo's `h-[28rem]` frame that the Root's `h-full` takes. The gap beside the panel is a flow spacer and the Rail paints only on hover, so neither draws. No docs demo shows a floating Sidebar.
- `Navigation menu bar trigger` holds `NavigationMenu.Trigger` in a horizontal Root, in 4 states (rest, hover, focus and disabled), closed and open: 8 variants. Its columns are the states. A designer lines triggers and bar links up into a menu bar. The trigger is the md control size, `control-h-md` tall, padded `control-px-md` across and spaced `control-gap-md`, on `radius-md`, and hugs the docs Basic demo's `Customers` at Medium in the control type pair and the page's `foreground`, and a 16px CaretDown in `muted-foreground`. The `Label` property sets the text. Hover and open fill it with `muted`, focus draws the 2px `ring`, and a disabled trigger dims to 50%. Base UI renders a disabled trigger with `aria-disabled="true"` and `data-disabled`, never the native attribute, so the trigger stays focusable and `disabled:` holds in no cell. `open=true` holds `data-popup-open`, which Base UI stamps on an open trigger, and the caret, whose `data-side` is the Root's default `bottom`, turns half a turn through `data-[side=bottom]:group-data-popup-open/navigation-menu-trigger:rotate-180`, disabled too. The popup waits for the overlay sets.
- `Navigation menu vertical trigger` holds the trigger in a vertical Root that opens its popup on its right, as the docs Nested demo's `<NavigationMenu.Root orientation="vertical" side="right" align="end">` does, in the same 4 states, closed and open: 8 variants. It is a content row 288px wide, the demo's list, at least `row-h` tall, padded `row-px` across and `row-py` down, on `radius-sm`. It holds the demo's `Electric car` at 14/20 Regular, which `font-[number:inherit]` takes from a page that sets no weight, and the caret. The label fills the room the caret and the 8px gap leave and wraps there, and the row packs the two at its start, so the caret ends the row 8px after the label, as CSS keeps the gap under `justify-between`. The demos give the row a title and a description below it, and the set draws the one-line form, the title alone. The caret writes its Root's `right`, so `data-[side=right]:-rotate-90` turns it a quarter turn in every cell, and no open cell turns it further. Hover and open fill the row with `accent` and paint the label and the caret `accent-foreground`, the caret through the `**:` arms. The trigger draws outside the popup on `rounded-sm`, so its `in-data-[slot=navigation-menu-popup]:` inner corner drops, and the `rtl:` arms of a logical side drop with a left-to-right page.
- `Navigation menu bar link` holds `NavigationMenu.Link`, an `<a>`, directly in a horizontal List, in 3 states (rest, hover and focus), inactive and active: 6 variants. It is the bar trigger's md control box around the docs Links demo's `Overview`, which the `Label` property sets. Hover fills it with `muted` and focus draws the 2px `ring`. `active=true` holds `data-active`, which `NavigationMenu.Link`'s `active` stamps, and its `font-medium` and `text-foreground` repeat what the bar box and the page already give, so an active bar link draws as an inactive one. An `<a>` takes no disabled state.
- `Navigation menu row link` holds the link in a vertical List, in the same 3 states, inactive and active: 6 variants. It is a content row 256px wide, the docs Basic demo's list, holding the demo's `Electricity` at 14/20 in `foreground`, Medium while active, which fills the row. Hover fills it with `accent` and paints the label `accent-foreground`. The row holds its text directly, so its `hover:**:text-accent-foreground` reaches no element and drops. In the demos every row sits in the popup and rounds with its inner corner. The set draws a row outside the popup on `rounded-sm`, so the `in-data-[slot=navigation-menu-popup]:` arm drops here, and the popup's set draws it on its row instances.
- `Navigation menu popup` holds the popup the docs Basic demo's bar opens, `Customers` and `Business`: 2 variants, its one property `content` (`customers`, `business`). It is the shared surface, `bg-popover`, `shadow-md` and a 1px `foreground/10` ring, rounded `radius-md`, as wide as Base UI measures its content, 264px and 296px (the demo's `w-64` and `w-72` lists plus `surface-pad-sm` on each side), and as tall as the content. Base UI's viewport and `NavigationMenu.Content` sit inside it, the content padding by `surface-pad-sm`. The list is a grid in one column, its rows 4px apart, and each `<li>` holds a `Navigation menu row link` instance at rest that fills it, with the `Label` the demo gives it. Inside the popup a row rounds with the synced inner corner `radius-inner/md-pad-sm` and takes `popover-foreground`, which the instance carries as overrides. The open trigger with its popup is in no set: Base UI's positioner places the popup 8px below the open trigger against the whole menu, starting at the trigger's offset in the List, and no auto layout draws a layer placed against an ancestor's box.
- `Number field stepper` holds NumberField's increase stepper at rest, hovered, disabled and read-only: 4 variants. It is Base UI's increment `<button>`, which Fuse stamps `data-slot="number-field-increment"`, a column `control-h-xs` wide with a 1px `input` edge on its left, `border-0 border-s`, on a `background` fill, holding a 16px glyph, Plus, which its `Icon` swap property picks. Hover fills it `muted`; a stepper disabled at its bound fills `muted` and mutes its glyph; a read-only field's stepper, which Base UI marks `aria-disabled` and `data-readonly` but leaves enabled, draws as at rest. It has no pressed face. Its `h-full` takes the group's content height, so the set binds it to `field-box-content-h`, 34px dense and 42px comfortable, and Number field's instances fill the group at each density: Chromium measured them 24×34 dense and 32×42 comfortable.
- `Number field` holds `NumberField` with the docs Basic demo's label and description and the States demo's value, `4`, in 6 states (rest, focus, disabled, read-only, invalid and invalid focus): 6 variants. The root is the `Field.Root` with FieldFrame's part spacing, a 384px column whose children sit 4px apart: the heading row with an exposed `Label` instance, Base UI's NumberField root around the group, the error in the invalid cells (the Error demo's) and the description. The group is the field box at `control-h-md`, which takes its states from its input through `has-` variants and fills `muted` when disabled or read-only. The invalid focus cell forces focus on the input, and the group draws Input's invalid focus ring through `has-[>[data-focus-ring-control][aria-invalid=true]:focus-visible]:`. It holds the input, which fills the room left and shows the value, which the `Value` text property sets; the denomination, `kWh` in 14px `muted-foreground`, behind the `Denomination` BOOLEAN, which starts off; and two `Number field stepper` instances that fill its height, the decrease one swapped to Minus. A disabled field disables its steppers, a read-only one shows the read-only stepper, and an invalid one stamps `data-invalid` on them, which the invalid cell's stepper instances carry as overrides. Base UI's NumberField parts carry no `data-slot`, so each is named by its element, except the steppers, which Fuse stamps `number-field-decrement` and `number-field-increment`, and the page forces focus on the input, by its place in the capture-backed build and through `statePartElement` in the recipe's.
- `File upload dropzone` holds the docs Compact demo's dropzone at rest, hovered, focused, as a drop target and disabled: 5 variants. The zone is 512px wide, the demo's `max-w-lg`, a row padded `surface-pad-md` with `radius-md` corners and a dashed 1px `border` stroke, its children `surface-gap-sm` apart and centred across: an exposed small default `Button` instance with the Plus start icon and `Add files`, and the description, the demo's copy in the label type pair in `muted-foreground`, which the `Description` text property sets. The description fits its text to the room the trigger leaves, so it draws as a fill and a longer description wraps there. Hover and focus strengthen the stroke to `muted-foreground/50`; the focus cell rings its trigger. A drop target draws a solid `primary` stroke on a `primary-soft` fill. A disabled zone dims to 50% and disables its trigger. Chromium measured the zone 512×58 dense and 512×70 comfortable.
- `Toolbar` holds the docs Basic demo's bar, horizontal and vertical: 2 variants. The bar is the Toolbar shell, `radius-lg` with a 1px `border` stroke and 4px padding on the `popover` fill, hugging its items 4px apart, centred across. It holds the File group, a frame around two exposed ghost small `Button` instances with start icons (`Download` with Download, `Duplicate` with Copy, which the `Start icon` swap property picks), then a `Separator` instance across the bar in the other orientation, then the disabled `Delete` button with Trash. The separator stretches across the bar 4px short of the items at each end, its `align-self: stretch` and margins drawn as a frame that fills across, padded by the margins. Base UI stamps the bar's orientation on each button, and each Button instance is one layer in both bars, which carries what the vertical bar draws differently as that cell's overrides. Base UI marks the disabled button `aria-disabled`, which the Button set renders only while pending; the instance stamps it over that meaning (see `stamps`), and it draws the Button set's disabled variant with no override of its face. Chromium measured the bar 286.3×42 horizontal and 106.3×119 vertical, dense.
- `Tooltip content` holds the popup the docs Basic demo opens: 1 variant. It inverts the fill, `foreground` behind `background` 12px text on a 16px line, rounded `radius-md`, padded `surface-pad-md` across and `row-py` down, at most 320px wide, its text behind the `Text` TEXT property. Chromium measured the text on one line, the popup 92 × 28 dense and 100 × 32 comfortable, which the capture records as one line. The arrow is Base UI's `<div>`, a 10px `foreground` square with 2px corners, turned -45 and placed by its centre on the popup's centre, 2px above its bottom edge, with CENTER across and MAX down (see the turned-arrow paragraph). The popup leaves the arrow unclipped.
- `Tooltip (open)` holds the ghost icon Button trigger with Info and the popup open above it: 1 variant. Base UI places the popup on the trigger's top side, 4px above it and centred on it, so the composite is a column of the popup's instance and the trigger's, centred, which clips nothing. `Tooltip.Trigger` renders the Button with the `tooltip-trigger` slot and its self focus ring classes, and Base UI marks it `data-popup-open`.
- `Alert dialog content` holds `AlertDialog.Content`'s Dialog popup, destructive and neutral, at rest and performing its action, at the `md`, `sm`, `lg` and `xl` sizes: 16 variants. Its properties are `variant`, `isPerformingAction` and `size`, AlertDialog.Content's props. `size` is an added axis, so an `md` popup keeps the key it had before the set drew sizes. It is fixed in a 640 × 448 viewport, the narrowest where the `md` popup reaches its full `--overlay-width`, `min(28rem, 90%)`, and the footer's `sm:` row holds, and as tall as the compare page's slots allow; so it is 448px wide at `md`, 384px at `sm`, 512px at `lg` and 576px at `xl`, each size's `min(<container>, 90%)`, and capped at 416px tall, padded `surface-pad-lg` on the `dialog` fill, rounded `radius-xl`, its rows 24px apart. The header row holds the title, 20/28, which fills the room the title line and the 16px gap leave, so a longer title wraps there as Chromium wraps it, and at its end the title line, 28px tall (`--text-xl` × `--text-xl--line-height`, theme constants, one length in every mode), around the 20px icon: WarningOctagon in `error`, or Info for a neutral dialog. The description is muted 14px copy. The footer ends its row with the ghost small Cancel and the small action Button, destructive or default, pending while the dialog performs its action. The copy is the docs demos' and differs by cell, so no TEXT property sets the title or the description, and the icon differs too, so no swap property picks it; the description's `*:[a]:` link arms are omitted with that reason. Chromium measured the `md` popup 448 × 160 dense and 448 × 180 comfortable. Every size from `2xl` up also reaches 90% of the viewport, 576px, so the set leaves them out.
- `Alert dialog (open)` holds the destructive `md` popup over the scrim: 1 variant. The composite is `Dialog.Backdrop`'s scrim, fixed over the same viewport, 640 × 448, `black/10` with a background blur of 8, which centres the popup's instance, as Base UI's fixed centring puts it.
- `Dialog content` holds `Dialog.Content`'s popup with the docs Basic demo's and Footer close demo's content, at the `md`, `sm`, `lg` and `xl` sizes: 8 variants. Its properties are `content` and `size`, which is an added axis, as in Alert dialog content. It sits in Alert dialog's 640 × 448 viewport, since it reads the same lengths, so it draws the same widths, 448px at `md`, and is capped at 416px tall, padded `surface-pad-lg` on the `dialog` fill, its header and footer 24px apart. The header stacks the title over the muted description, 8px apart. The footer ends its row with the demo's actions: the Basic demo's outline Cancel, the `Dialog.Close` that carries the `dialog-close` slot, and the default Move reading, or the Footer close demo's default Cancel contract and the outline Close `showCloseButton` appends. The corner close is an exposed ghost `icon-sm` Icon button instance with X, `absolute top-4 right-4`, 16px from the popup's end and 16px below its top. Its size binds `control-h-sm`, which density changes, so it sits in the `close placement` wrapper that the absolute-parts paragraph describes, which keeps both distances in an instance of the set too; its `hit-area-1` grows its hit target with an invisible `::before`. The copy differs by cell, so no TEXT property sets the title or the description, and the description's `*:[a]:` link arms are omitted with that reason.
- `Dialog (open)` holds the Basic `md` popup over the scrim: 1 variant, the same composite as Alert dialog's, 640 × 448, `black/10` with a background blur of 8, centring the popup's instance.
- `Card` holds the docs default demo's vertical card at the default and the lg size: 2 variants. Its one property is `size`, `Card.Root`'s prop, and its columns are the sizes. `Card.Root` is a block-level `flex flex-col` column, so it takes a 448px preview width, the demo's `max-w-md`. It rounds with `radius-lg`, fills with `card`, strokes 1px with `card-border` and casts a 0 1 2 shadow in `card-shadow`, so the root clips. The header is a grid in one column whose `auto-rows-min items-start` drop, since its height hugs its rows, so it draws as a column 6px apart, padded by the size's tier: `surface-pad-md`, or `surface-pad-lg` at lg. It holds the title, `March usage`, 24/33 SemiBold in `card-foreground`, and the description, `Estimated consumption for the period.`, 14/20 Regular in `muted-foreground`, each filling the header's width. Its `@container/card-header` drops, since the column stretches the header across. The content and the footer each hold one `<p>`, `1 240 kWh across two meters.` and `Settled on 3 April.`, 16/24 Regular, and pad by the same tier on every side but the top, since a part below another drops its top padding. The footer is a row that centres its paragraph across. The paragraph fits its text to the footer's width, so it draws as a fill and a longer footer wraps there. The `Title`, `Description`, `Content` and `Footer` TEXT properties set the four texts. The set draws no horizontal card, whose header, content and footer share a wrapping row by flex growth weighted 2, 1 and 1, which an auto layout cannot share out, and no `Card.Action`, `Card.Tag` or sections. Each variant draws the header, the content and the footer, so a card without one of them, as the docs Content only and Large demos show, has no cell yet.
- `Action bar` holds the docs default demo's bar over two selected rows: 1 variant, `state=rest`. `ActionBar.Root` is Toolbar's bar, a row that hugs its items 4px apart and pads 4px, rounded with `radius-lg`, filled with `popover` and stroked 1px with `border`. It renders in its positioner at the bottom of a 576px host, the demo's `max-w-xl` column, where it is wider than 28rem, so each button shows its words. Its `max-w-full` drops, since the capture measured the bar as wide with that maximum 24px lower in every mode. It holds the count, `2 selected`, its number and word joined by the no-break space Fuse's `countOf` sets, padded 8px across behind the `Count` TEXT property, Clear, a ghost `icon-xs` Icon button instance with `X`, the separator, a vertical Separator instance that fills the bar's height less 4px above and below, and Export and Delete, ghost `xs` Button instances whose `Label` and `Start icon` carry the demo's words and its `Download` and `Trash`. The set draws no bar narrower than 28rem, whose buttons hide their words by a container query.
- `Sheet content` holds the panel the docs No close button demo opens, on the right side at the md size: 1 variant, `side=right`. `Sheet.Content` is fixed in a 640×448 viewport, 448px wide by its md `--overlay-width` and as tall as the viewport, filled with `dialog` and stroked 1px on its left with `border`. Its `background-clip: padding-box` stays unmapped: `border` is white at 10% in the neutral dark theme, where Figma shows the fill under the border and CSS does not. Its swipe transform, `translateX(var(--drawer-swipe-movement-x, 0px))`, is a translation by zero at rest and drops. The panel's column fills it, `surface-gap-lg` apart, and holds the header, the title over the muted description padded by `surface-pad-lg` on every side but the bottom, and the footer, padded by `surface-pad-lg`, whose `mt-auto` takes the column's free space, which the capture measured in every mode, so the column spaces the two between its ends. The footer holds `Sheet.Close` rendering an outline Button instance with the demo's `I have read the terms`. The `Title` and `Description` TEXT properties set the panel's texts. The `Close button` BOOLEAN, false by default, shows the corner close `showCloseButton` renders: `Sheet.Close` rendering the ghost `icon-sm` Icon button with X, 16px from the panel's top and right. Its containing block is the popup, and the column it is a child of spans the popup's padding box, so it draws in the column's frame, in the `corner-close placement` wrapper, since density resizes it as it does Dialog's close. The capture of the panel with the close on and off differs only in the close's own subtree, so the property is a BOOLEAN. The set draws no `Sheet.Body`, no panel over its scrim, whose Backdrop the kit places only around a centred or top popup, and no other side or size.
- `Calendar day` holds one day of the month grid that Calendar and RangeCalendar share (#677): 76 variants by `selection`, `outside`, `state` (rest, hover, focus, disabled, unavailable) and `today`.
  - `selection` is none, the one selected day, a range's start, middle or end mid-week, or a range day at a week's edge: a start on the week's last day, a middle day on its first or last day, or an end on its first day. A range's start on the week's first day and its end on the week's last day draw as mid-week, and a range of one day draws as a start on the week's last day.
  - The root is `Calendar.DayGridCell`'s `<td>`, which the capture measured holding its button exactly in every mode (`tableTiles`), so it hugs it. A range's cell fills with `primary/20`, and its start and end round their outer corners by 18px, as CSS scales `rounded-s-full` and `rounded-e-full` on the 36px cell. A range day at the week's first or last day rounds that side too, as `first:rounded-s-full` and `last:rounded-e-full` do, so a start on the week's last day rounds both.
  - The button is `Calendar.DayButton`, `size-9`, round, its number behind the `Day` TEXT property: `primary` with `primary-foreground` where selected or ending a range, `muted` on hover, `primary/30` on a hovered middle day, `muted-foreground` where disabled. Today sets its number semibold.
  - An unavailable day, which `isDateUnavailable` refuses, sets its number in `muted-foreground` with a line through it. A user never picks a range across one, so it sits in a range only where the app sets the range. RangeCalendar checks only a range's ends. Between two available ends an unavailable day shows outside the range, as `selection` none draws it. Where an end is unavailable, every day of the range is invalid, so the middle cells show a range that ends on an unavailable day. An invalid selected day or range end fills its circle with `error` and `error-foreground`, and an invalid range's band fills with `error/10`.
  - An outside day is disabled and its button hidden, which the cell keeps 36px around. Calendar disables every outside day, marks none unavailable, and gives a disabled day no selection, so those cells are left out.
  - A selected day and a range's start and end stay hoverable, and draw on hover as at rest, since no hover rule of the day matches them. So does an unavailable day, which Calendar marks `aria-disabled`. `state` holds one value, so the set draws no day both unavailable and hovered or focused.
  - The `today` property came after the set's first release, so a day that is not today keeps its first key.
- `Calendar` holds the Calendar default demo's month in its card: 1 variant. The root is `Calendar.Root`'s card, `card` with a 1px `border` and the medium shadow, rounded with `radius-calendar` and padded with `surface-pad-sm`, around its header and the month's day grid. The header holds the ghost `icon` Icon buttons with CaretLeft and CaretRight around the visible-range heading, `October 2026` at 18/28 Medium behind a `Heading` TEXT property, its month and year joined by the no-break space `Intl.DateTimeFormat` writes. The grid holds the weekday row, each name in `muted-foreground` behind a `Weekday 1` to `Weekday 7` TEXT property, over October 2026's five weeks as Calendar day instances, the four September days outside and 14 October selected. A `<th>` centers its name, as Chromium's default style sheet does under a parent at the initial `text-align`. The month's heading is visually hidden in a calendar of one month, so the set leaves it out. CSS sizes the card to the wider of the header and the 252px month grid: at comfortable density the en-US header for `September 2026` is 253.5px wide on macOS. So the header hugs its layers with the grid's 252px as its minimum, spaces them out between its ends, and pads the heading by its 8px margin and the header's 4px gap on each side, and the card centers the grid under it, as the sizing rules below describe. A longer month in the `Heading` property widens the header and the card as CSS does. The card's fill shows under its border, where CSS clips the fill at the border, which differs only in the neutral dark theme, whose border is white at 10%.
- `Range calendar` holds the RangeCalendar default demo's month the same way, 12 to 16 October as a range's start, middle days and end: 1 variant.
- `Date field` holds `DateField` with the docs default demo's label and description, empty or holding the Error demo's 5 October 2026, at rest, focused, disabled, read-only and invalid: 10 variants. The root is the Field's column: the label row with a `Label` instance, the field box, then the error in an invalid field and the description, as `Field error` and `Field description` instances. The field box, `data-slot="field-group"`, is a row of the en-US segments in source order, month, `/`, day, `/` and year, each a frame that hugs its text, padded 2px and rounded with `radius-inner/field-border-pad-md`. An empty field shows `mm`, `dd` and `yyyy` in `muted-foreground` and Roboto's italic face. The focus cell focuses the month segment, which fills with `primary`. The box draws no focus ring. Its ring rule, `has-[>[data-focus-ring-control]:focus-visible]`, needs a direct child marked `data-focus-ring-control`, and the capture holds no segment that carries the attribute, so the browser draws no ring either. The set draws no `sm` size and no time segments.
- `Date picker` holds `DatePickerField` closed, with the docs default demo's label and description, in the same 10 variants. The field box holds the segment row, which fills the box beside the trigger, and the trigger, a ghost `icon-sm` Icon button instance with `CalendarBlank`, rounded with `radius-inner/field-border` and disabled where the picker is disabled or read-only. The box draws no focus ring, for the Date field's reason. The set draws no calendar popup, since the kit draws the calendar only in part, no `sm` size, no trigger at the start and no time segments.
- `Date range picker` holds `DateRangePickerField` closed, with the docs default demo's label `Stay`, its description and its range, 5 to 9 October 2026, in the same 10 variants. The root is the picker's column: the `Label` instance, the field box, then the error in an invalid picker and the description, as `Field error` and `Field description` instances. The field box holds the start's and the end's segment rows, the `–` separator and the trigger, Date picker's ghost `icon-sm` Icon button instance. The trigger's `ms-auto` ends the row, so a frame packs the two segment rows and the separator at the row's start, and each segment row stretches down the box. The box draws no focus ring, for the Date field's reason. The set draws no calendar popup or presets, no `sm` size and no time segments.
- `Tabs trigger` holds `Tabs.Trigger` with the docs default demo's `Account` behind the `Label` TEXT property, in `Tabs.List`'s `default` and `line` variants, at rest, hovered, focused, active and disabled: 10 variants. It sits alone in the demo's list, which an active trigger's root value selects. Its height is the list's content height less 1px, `tabs-trigger-h` in the default list and `tabs-line-trigger-h` in the line list, and its `min-h-[max(1.5rem,24px)]` drops, since neither variable falls under 24px in any density. An active trigger fills with `card` and casts the small shadow in the default list. In the line list it fills with nothing, and its `::after` underline, a 2px `foreground` layer stretched across it inside its 1px border and 4px below it, turns opaque, and the root leaves it unclipped; every other trigger keeps the underline at opacity 0. It rounds with `radius-inner/lg-pad-4px` in the default list and `radius-md` in the line list.
- `Tabs list` holds the default demo's `Tabs.List` around `Account`, active, and `Password`, as `Tabs trigger` instances, in its 2 variants: 2 variants. The list is `control-h-md` tall, padded 4px, or 3px in the line variant, and its `min-h-[calc(max(1.5rem,24px)+1px+2*max(var(--spacing),4px))]`, 33px, drops, since `control-h-md` is 36px dense and 44px comfortable. The two triggers' `flex-1` grows each from a zero basis in a list as wide as their content together, which the capture measured in every mode (`itemsAtContent`), and each keeps the automatic minimum of its `whitespace-nowrap` label, so each keeps its own width, 74px and 84px dense, for any label, and each instance hugs it.
- `Tabs content` holds the default demo's first panel, `Tabs.Content`, behind the `Text` TEXT property, at rest and focused: 2 variants. The vertical orientation and triggers with icons stay out.
- `Timeline list` holds the docs default demo's three events: 1 variant, `state=rest`. `TimelineList.Root` is an `<ol>` that stacks its items, each a block with its connector and its dot drawn by their place. An item draws its `mb-10` and `ml-6` in a frame around it, since the next item's zero top margin leaves the bottom margin its length. Its dot, `absolute top-2 left-0`, sits 8px down at its left, MIN on both axes, and the connector, the item's `::before`, sits 4px in from its left, from 32px below its top to 32px past its bottom, STRETCH down, as `before:h-full` reaches past the item by the 32px it starts down. The last item draws no connector, as `not-last:before:` writes it. Each title, time and description is the demo's, behind a TEXT property numbered by event, as `Title 1`.
- `Selection item` holds the docs default demo's row, a Checkbox beside its title and description: 1 variant, `state=rest`. `SelectionItem.Shell` is a grid of an `auto` and a `minmax(0, 1fr)` column, a HUG and a FLEX track, 384px wide, its corners `radius-lg` as `first:rounded-t-lg last:rounded-b-lg` round a lone row, padded across by `surface-pad-md` inside its `selection-border` stroke, on `card`. Its `<label>` generates no box, so the row lays its two items out, the control slot, `label/control`, and the text cluster, `label/cluster`, both `self-start`. The control slot's `box-content` height of one first line, `label-line-snug`, sits inside its 14px of padding as an inner `content` frame that centres the Checkbox instance. The cluster fills its column and holds the title, Medium in branded themes and Regular in neutral ones as its weight binds `selection-title-weight`, and the description, behind the `Title` and `Description` properties.
- `Aspect ratio` holds each docs demo's frame, one per `ratio` in the docs page's order: 7 variants, its columns the ratios. `AspectRatio` is a `<div>` in a 448px host, the default demo's `max-w-md`, and each demo's `w-full max-w-*` sets its width: 448px at 16:9, 240px at 1:1 and 3:4, 384px at 4:3 and 3:2, 208px at 2:3 and 176px at 9:16. The ratio sets its height, as the mapping section says, so 9:16 is 312.89px tall. It holds the portrait Avatar shows, an `<img>` with the demos' `size-full rounded-lg object-cover` that fills the frame on both axes as an image paint rounded with `radius-lg`. Each variant locks its aspect ratio, so an instance a designer resizes, or one that fills a wider or narrower host, keeps the ratio. The set draws no custom `aspect-*` ratio and no `<video>`.
- `Code` holds the docs default demo's two lines: 1 variant, `state=rest`. The root is the `<pre>`, 448px wide in its host, which sets its one text in `font-mono` at 12px on 19.5px lines and clips its content under a 640px `max-height`. The text holds the demo's source as written, its line feed included, in `foreground`, and each token sugar-high colors through a span takes its color as a range bound to its `sh-*` variable, such as `let` in `sh-keyword`. The line feed takes the color of the `;` before it, `sh-sign`, since Figma paints a line break in the fill of the character before it. The text has no TEXT property: Figma does not color the characters a designer types, and on 2026-11-19 a `characters` write, even of the same text, gave every character the first character's fill.
- `Table head`, `Table cell`, `Table row` and `Table caption` are the blocks a designer builds a table from. CSS shares a table wider than its content among its columns by their content (CSS 2.1 §17.5.2.2), so the split changes with density and with the widest cell's text, and no auto layout reproduces it. Each block draws as a table no wider than its content lays it out, which the capture measured in every mode (`tableTiles`), so each cell hugs its text and stacked rows line up only once a designer sets each column's width on the instances. `Table head` is the default demo's `Order` and `Table cell` its `#1042`: 1 variant each, `state=rest`, each a `<th>` or `<td>` that hugs its text across, `row-px` to a side, at its own `row-h-header` or `row-h`, its text in the middle as `align-middle` sets it. Outside a frame a first or last cell's `--table-cell-edge-*` reads the container's `initial` `--table-edge-px`, so it falls back to `--table-cell-ps` and `--table-cell-pe`, `row-px`. `Table row` holds three head or cell instances with the demo's texts: 12 variants by `row` (the header row, a body row, or the body's last, which draws no rule), `selected`, as the selected-row demo's `data-state="selected"` marks a row, and `state` (rest, hover). It hugs its instances, and a hovered or selected row fills with `muted/72`. Its table collapses its borders, so the row's `border-b` sits on the grid line below it (CSS 2.1 §17.6.2) and takes no room. The row stays `row-h` tall, and the stroke draws inside it. Chromium paints that 1px rule on the first pixel row below the row, and the kit on its last; Chromium also takes half a pixel of the cells' content box on each side of the line, which the kit leaves out. `Table caption` is the demo's `Recent orders`, as wide as its table, 512px here, its text centered as Chromium's default style sheet sets a caption's `text-align` and the caption's `revert` rolls back to. Its `mt-4` spaces it from the table, which the set does not draw. The footer, a framed table and VerticalTable, whose fixed layout splits its columns evenly, have no set yet.
- `Attachment` holds the docs default demo's row at each `size`, at rest and waiting to upload: 6 variants, `state=done` and `state=idle`, its columns the sizes. `Attachment.Root` is a row in a 448px host, which it fills as a block-level root fills the docs stage, its stroke dashed while idle. It holds the media with the demo's FileArrowUp, which the `Icon` swap property picks, the content column with the title and the description behind the `Title` and `Description` properties, and the actions with the ghost `icon-xs` Icon button with X. The actions' `relative z-20` paints them over the rest of the row, and they are the row's last layer, so the stacking level drops, as the mapping section says. The set draws no vertical tile, no `error`, `uploading` or `processing` state, no image media and no `Attachment.Trigger` or `Attachment.Group`. Three limits remain. An emptied description is 0px tall in CSS and keeps one 15px line in Figma. A title or description of one word wider than its column is unprobed. CSS cuts the word with an ellipsis, and how Figma truncates an unbreakable word on one line has not been measured. The root forms no stacking context, so CSS also paints the actions over a box the host lays after the row that overlaps them, which no instance can do.
- `Description list item` and `Description list` draw the responsive layout at `sm` and up. `Description list item` is the docs default demo's first Item, `Name` and `Kari Nordmann` behind the `Term` and `Details` properties: 4 variants by `position` (first in its list, or after another Item) and `dividers`, Content's prop, its columns the `dividers` values. An Item after another, in a list with dividers, draws its `border-t` as a 1px top stroke in the layout, so it is 1px taller, as in Chromium; the first Item, and every Item without dividers, wins `border-none`. The Item is a row 576px wide, the host's width, whose term fills it up to a `maxWidth` of 320 and whose details fills the rest, as the next section's subgrid rule says. `Description list` is the demo's heading over its list of five Item instances with the demo's texts: 2 variants by `dividers`. The list is a column whose Item instances fill it, the first at `position=first`. A designer adds or removes a row as an instance, and each row splits its width as the others do, so the cells line up at any width. Two limits remain. A details word wider than its share widens CSS's second column for every Item and pushes the list past its width, where the kit breaks the word. Each cell hugs its height from the row's top, where CSS stretches it to the row, which draws alike since the cells paint nothing. The term and details are padded frames, and a probe of padded frames split the row as it split bare texts (DL4, in the next section). The inline layout, and the stacked one below `sm`, have no set.
- `Checkbox card` holds the docs default demo's card: 12 variants by `variant`, `checked` and `state` (rest, focus and disabled), its columns the states. The root is `Card.Root` in a 384px host, a column around `Card.Content`, the row around the card's `<label>`, which holds the control and the text column. The control stacks Circle and the solid CheckCircle in one grid cell and crossfades them by opacity, so each variant draws the icon its state shows, as the next section's one-cell rule says: Circle unchecked, `CheckCircleFill` checked. The title and the description sit behind the `Title` and `Description` properties. The text column fills the room beside the control, as the next section's rule for a row's last part says, so a description a designer lengthens wraps inside the card as it does in Chromium. Focus rings the control, and disabled dims the card to 50%. The card has no hover style: a forced hover matches no rule, so the set has no hover cell. The set draws no `tags`, `children` or `rightContent`. A title wraps without `text-wrap: balance` and a description without `pretty`, and a word wider than the column overflows it in Chromium where Figma breaks it.
- `List detail pane` is the docs default demo's detail pane in the split layout, `ListDetail.Detail`'s `<aside>`: 2 variants by `content`. `empty` is the demo's muted `Select a customer.`, 14/20px with 24px padding, behind the `Empty` property. `value` is the first customer selected, `ListDetail.Title`'s `Henrik Aasen` in its header row over `Spot price`, the title filling the row so a longer name wraps there as Chromium wraps it, behind the `Title` and `Product` properties, 16px padding with 8px between. The pane is 352px wide, as `w-88` makes it, and 320px tall, the demo's `h-80` row, which the root and the pane stretch to, with the `right-panel` fill and a 1px left stroke in `border`. The empty text sits in the middle, as its `m-auto` keeps it in the browser. The text has one line in the demo, and a longer one wraps at the 303px cap, its frame then as wide as the pane's 351px content box, as CSS draws it.
- `List detail` is the whole default demo in the split layout, 768×320: 2 variants by `content`, which the root passes to its pane. The list is a column with 8px padding and 4px between its rows, filling the room the pane leaves. Each row is the demo's `<li>`, a frame that fills the list's width around an exposed ghost Button instance at the default size, labeled with a customer's name. The Button fills the row, as its `w-full` makes it, and draws its label at the start, an override of the instance's alignment along its row for `justify-start`. The pane is an exposed `List detail pane` instance, 352px wide, which the root's row stretches to its height, as the list too. Button styles no `aria-current`, so the selected row draws as the others do. The set keeps the pane as its own set, a block a designer places alone or switches from the root. The overlay layout, where `ListDetail.Close` shows, has no set yet.
- `Data table filter bar` holds the docs default demo's bar at rest: 2 variants, `state=rest` by `filters`, its columns the `filters` values. `filters=none` is the bar before any filter is added, and `filters=status` holds the demo's Status field filtered to `is Paid`. The bar fills its 768px host and holds its Trigger, an outline `icon-sm` Icon button instance with its Funnel, at the start of its row. In `filters=status` the chip strip and a ghost small `Clear filters` Button follow it. The strip folds its `-m-0.5` into its `p-0.5` and draws with no padding. It clips, as its `overflow-x-auto` does, but CSS clips 2px out from the chip and Figma clips at the chip's edge, so the kit cuts off the chip's `shadow-xs`, 1px down with 2px of blur. The chip, 1px `border` stroked on `radius-md`, holds the subject, the Circle and the field's name behind the `Field` text property, then the operator `is`, the value `Paid` and Remove with X, each a ghost instance stroked 1px on its start side with square corners. The operator's label shows Regular, and the value is at most 224px wide. `filters` is an added axis, so the bar without filters keeps its key. The `filters=none` strip holds no chip, so that variant leaves it out, since Figma keeps an empty hugging frame at its last size (EF1 to EF3). The set leaves out a ghost chip, the layout below 36rem and the popups.
- `Tour popup` draws the Beacon demo's single-step tip. It is 320px wide and uses the Popover shell: 1 variant, `content=tip`, its parts 12px apart. The header row holds the title, `Export your readings` at Medium, and Close, the ghost `icon-sm` Icon button instance with X, at its two ends. The description, `Download every hourly reading as a CSV file.` in `muted-foreground`, follows, and the footer row holds Done, the default `sm` Button instance, at its end, since the tour has one step. The `Title` and `Description` properties set the step's words. The title fills the room that Close and the 8px gap leave, so a longer title wraps there, as Chromium wraps it. Chromium evens out a two-line title's lines through `text-balance`, which the kit drops, so Figma may break such a title at another word. The description wraps without `text-wrap: pretty`, which the kit drops on every label that wraps. In dense modes Chromium sets "Save your hourly readings as a CSV file for your records." with "your records." on its second line, and the kit leaves "records." alone there. Done's padding in branded dense falls short of the Button label floor, 10px a side where Chromium draws 12px, so Done is 54.375px wide where Chromium draws 58.375px (#521). The set leaves out the spotlight, the ring, the beacon and the popup's place beside its step's element (#524).
- `Tour step` draws the docs default demo's step card at the first of its two steps, on the same 320px Popover shell: 1 variant, `content=step`. The header row holds `Tour.Progress`, its two 6px dots 4px apart and the `1 of 2` count in `muted-foreground` at the xs size, and Close at its two ends. The first dot fills with `primary` and the second with the `muted-foreground/30` role tint. The title, `Usage this month`, and the description, `Your consumption so far, updated every hour.`, follow. The footer row holds Skip, the ghost `sm` Button, at its start by `me-auto`, and Back, the outline `sm` Button at `state=disabled`, and Next, the default `sm` Button, at its end, as the auto end margin paragraph below draws them. `Tour.Prev` is disabled at the first step and stays focusable, so Chromium dims it through Button's `aria-disabled:` arm, which draws what the `state=disabled` variant draws from its `disabled:` arm. The `Count`, `Title` and `Description` properties set the step's words. The step card is a set of its own, since a TEXT property holds one default and the two cards' words differ. The set leaves out the last step, where Back is enabled and Next reads Done, and the same parts as `Tour popup`.
- `Toast` draws the toast the docs default demo raises, at rest, in the demo's 384px `max-w-sm` stack: 6 variants, one for each `status` a toast's `type` sets, neutral, info, success, warning, error and loading. `status` is a Figma-only axis, since no Toast part takes it as a prop. The `Title` and `Description` TEXT properties set the text column's title and description. The root hugs its content's height, and the column fills the room the status icon leaves, so a longer description wraps there and the toast grows. The close, `Toast.Close` at `absolute top-2 right-2` in the content column, is the ghost `icon-sm` Icon button with X. Its containing block is the root, so the root lifts it out of the column and draws it as its last layer, in a placement wrapper (`blockSteps`). The wrapper stretches across the root from its top edge, and its padding insets the button 8px from the root's right and top. The button keeps that place as the root widens or a longer description makes it taller. The set leaves out `Toast.Action`, a stack of several toasts, a toast behind the frontmost one and the top placements.
- Four preview frames in each component page's `Themes` section pin Fjordkraft private, neutral and branded, each in light and dark. No identity implies a density, so each preview pins one: the neutral ones `Dense` and the branded ones `Comfortable`. A designer switches a preview's modes to see the other themes. Each preview holds a group per set on its page, so each set's instances start on a row of their own. A group has no padding and does not clip, so a focus ring at its edge still draws.

### Parity with Fuse

`KIT_PARITY` in `src/components/kit-parity.ts` holds one row for each public Fuse component, keyed by its folder under `packages/fuse/src/components`. Each row is one of five kinds. A `sets` row names the sets that draw the component with every variant and axis it exposes. A `partial` row names the sets that draw it, the set, variant or axis they still lack, and the open issue that tracks that gap. An `issue` row names the open issue that tracks a component with no set. A `composed` row names the sets designers build the component from, as instances the kit already draws. An `excluded` row gives the reason the component takes no set. `test/kit-parity.test.ts` fails when a component the package exports has no row, when a row names a folder the package does not export, or when a row names a set the kit inventory does not hold.

### Running it

1. Run `figma:sync` on the file first. The plugin binds only the variables the sync owns, and it stops before writing anything when one is missing. The role tints arrived on 2026-10-19, the outline shadow lengths on 2026-10-20, the compact corner and the `warning/20` and `info/20` tints on 2026-10-21, the checkbox corner, the field box corner `radius-field-box` and the `input/50` tint on 2026-10-22, and the inner corners `radius-inner/lg-pad-8px`, `radius-inner/sm-pad-md`, `radius-inner/lg-pad-md`, `radius-inner/xl-pad-md`, `radius-inner/lg-border-pad-md`, `radius-inner/xl-border-pad-md` and `radius-inner/lg-pad-sm`, the density formulas `empty-pad-desktop`, `accordion-panel-pt` and `label-line-snug`, and the `muted/50`, `error/5`, `success/5` and `sidebar-foreground/70` tints on 2026-10-23, and the inner corners `radius-inner/md-pad-sm` and `radius-inner/xl-gap-sm`, the density formula `row-indicator-room` and the `foreground/10` tint on 2026-10-24, and the density formula `addon-px-button` and the inner corner `radius-inner/field-border-pad-addon-button` on 2026-10-25, and the `muted-foreground/50` tint on 2026-10-26, and the `foreground/35`, `primary/35`, `brand/35`, `muted-foreground/35` and `error/35` tints on 2026-11-03, and the `muted-foreground/30` tint on 2026-11-20, so a file synced before then needs another sync.
2. Build the plugin from the repository root:

   ```sh
   pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:plugin
   ```

   The build writes `plugin/dist/code.js`, the kit spec `plugin/dist/spec.json` and the coverage table `plugin/dist/coverage.md`. Build in the checkout whose `plugin/manifest.json` Figma imported, and run `pnpm install` first after a pull that adds dependencies. On 2026-10-21 the plugin failed to load with ENOENT on `plugin/dist/code.js` for that reason.

3. In the Figma desktop app, open the file, choose Plugins → Development → Import plugin from manifest, and pick `packages/fuse-figma/plugin/manifest.json`. Figma runs a development plugin only in the desktop app.
4. Run the plugin and press Plan. It lists every change an apply would make, and writes nothing.
5. Press Apply. The plugin writes the changes, then plans again and shows any change that remains. A clean run shows none.
6. Publish the library by hand, as for the variables.

To try a few sets before the whole kit, build a probe:

```sh
pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:plugin --sets button,text-field,alert,select-open
```

It keeps the named sets and every set they instance, at any depth, and `buildKit` builds those entries as it builds the whole kit, with their pages, previews and docs, the icon library and the text styles (`components/probe-kit.ts`). A page whose example set the probe leaves out shows its first kept set instead. The build writes the probe to `plugin/dist/probe/` with a manifest of its own, `Fuse components (UX probe)` under the id `fuse-components-ux-probe`, so Figma holds it beside the whole kit's plugin.

Apply a probe only to a file of its own. Both plugins own the same keys, so a probe run in a file that holds the whole kit would delete every set the probe lacks. Create a new private draft in the Elmera org, sync the tokens into it, import `plugin/dist/probe/manifest.json`, then Plan, Apply and Plan again, which lists no change, and publish the file as its own library. Plan stops on a file that holds a kit set the probe lacks, and names the sets. `plugin/src/probe.fake.test.ts` checks that the probe passes the kit check, that Plan after Apply lists nothing, and that Plan refuses a file the whole kit was applied to.

### Frozen keys

Plan and Apply find each node the kit owns by its key: a set or the icon library frame among the kit pages' top-level nodes, a variant among its set's children and an icon among the frame's. A changed key on any of those deletes the node and creates a new one, so every instance of it in another file loses its main component. A text style goes the same way, and a designer's text loses its link to it.

Component properties are matched by name, and the plugin never deletes one. A renamed property is added beside the old one: an instance keeps its value on the old property, and the new one starts at its default.

`oracles/keys.json` therefore freezes each key a main's identity depends on: the page, set, variant, icon library frame and icon keys. It also freezes the text style keys and each component property, as `<set> <TEXT | BOOLEAN | INSTANCE_SWAP> <name>`, each list sorted. Icons count because a designer swaps them into Button and the other sets through their INSTANCE_SWAP properties. `test/frozen-keys.test.ts` fails on a frozen key the build lost, removed or renamed, and on a key the build adds that the file lacks, naming each. Append new keys with:

```sh
pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:keys
```

The script only appends: a frozen key the build lost stays listed, and the script names it.

Layer keys inside a variant are not frozen. A designer's overrides on an instance survive a variant that stays, and a renamed sublayer loses only the overrides on that sublayer. That costs less than a lost variant, and it leaves the kit free to restructure its layers. Doc keys (`docs/…`) and preview keys are not frozen either, since no designer instances them.

The keys freeze at the first publish of the real Fuse kit library. Until then, a PR may rewrite `oracles/keys.json` by deleting it and running `figma:keys`, and its body says so. After it, removing or renaming a frozen key needs the owner's approval. It also needs a migration note: for a set, variant or icon, how files that hold its instances swap to the new main; for a property, how they move their values to the new one.

### How the spec is built

The build reads each set's kept capture, `captures/<set>.json`, and maps the declarations Chromium resolved on every element of each cell's root, as "Building a set from its capture" describes. A kit entry pairs the set's page with its manifest, and `manifestDefinition` turns the manifest into the definition the builder reads: the matrix, the parts by `data-slot` or place, the labels, the icons and the instance parts. The capture names each winning declaration by the class whose rule set it, so the coverage table still lists a row per class, and the rules below still speak of classes where a rule's selector decides what it reaches.

A manifest can declare parts below its root, such as a checkbox's indicator. A part has the cells it appears in, and its own parts, icons and label, and takes the declarations its element wins in the capture. Its layer takes its `data-slot` as its name, or its key when the element has none, as Radio's dot does. A part's children sit in DOM order: its parts first, then the start icons, the label and the end icons, and last the parts whose `after` names the label. A flex box that holds no icon or label of its own lays its parts out in order-modified document order (CSS Flexbox 1 §5.4): by `order`, lowest first, with the DOM breaking ties, so Command's search addon, which follows its input in the DOM, draws first through `order-first`. Elsewhere `order` stays unmapped. Each part becomes a frame layer and cascades its own classes. It inherits its parent's computed `color`, `font-family`, `font-size`, `font-weight`, `font-style`, `line-height`, `letter-spacing`, `white-space-collapse`, `text-wrap-mode`, `text-wrap-style`, `text-align` and `overflow-wrap`, and every custom property whose `@property` does not say `inherits: false`, at the rank the host page has on the root. Tailwind registers every `--tw-*` property with `inherits: false`, so a part takes their initial values. An icon inherits from the part that holds it the same way, so it takes its glyph color from the part's text color, and a size the part gives through a custom property reaches it. A box's `*:data-[slot=x]:` classes style each child part whose `data-slot` is `x`, as Select's trigger lays out its value. Tailwind compiles them to `:is(.… > *)[data-slot="x"]`, at (0,2,0), so Chromium cascades them with the part's own classes by that specificity, and the capture records the winner and beat a bare class of the part's own. A slot no part of the manifest carries, a bare `*:` and a variant after the slot stay unmapped. A box's `[&_img]:` classes style each `<img>` part below it, at any depth, a part with no `data-slot` whose element is an `<img>`, as Item's image media sizes and fits the picture a consumer puts in it. Tailwind compiles them to `.… img` at (0,1,1), so Chromium cascades them with the part's own classes by that specificity, and the capture records the winner. Where no such part sits below the box, they stay unmapped. A box's `[&>input]:` classes style its direct child parts that are `<input>` elements, which Tailwind compiles to `.… > input`. The capture records each element's tag, so Input group's root pads its `<input>` control beside an inline addon. A part deeper down takes none, and the class stays unmapped.
