On 2026-10-27 a real file read the arrow's `relativeTransform` `[[0.7071, -0.7071, W/2], [0.7071, 0.7071, H - 9.0703]]`, its bounding box 14.1421 square and its centre (W/2, H - 2) whether `x` and `y` came before the turn or after it, kept CENTER and MAX through a density switch and text-driven widths in the main and in an instance with no overrides, and exported the overhang in the PNG, 120 × 38 for a 32px-tall node (W5-P11, W5-P11b). The fake keeps the place through CENTER and MAX on a resize (fake rule 59).

A part that its parent's flex row holds alone can move along the row with a translate, as Switch's thumb does. A row that leaves `justify-content` unset packs the part at the start of its content box, and the translate moves the part from there, a percentage taking the part's own width. The build reads a move of 0 as the row's start alignment, MIN, and a move by the content width less the part's width as its end alignment, MAX. The default thumb's `calc(100% - 2px)` is 14px, and the track's content box is 32px less its two 1px borders, so 14px puts the 16px thumb flush with the end. Any other move stays unmapped, and so do a vertical move, a move in a row with no fixed width, and a move in a row whose `justify-content` is set, unless it is `flex-start` and the move is 0. A move in any other layout, such as a grid that centers its one layer, stays unmapped.

A menu row's trailing check, `absolute right-(--row-px)`, draws in flow at the row's end. An absolute box with `right` set and `left`, `top` and `bottom` auto sits that far from the end of its containing block, and across the row where `align-items: center` puts its static position (CSS 2.1 §10.3.7, CSS Flexbox 1 §4.1). The row keeps that room with `pr-[calc(var(--row-px)+1.5rem)]`: the offset, the row's 8px gap and the 16px indicator. Where the row's end padding equals the offset, the gap and the part's width at every value of their variables, and the row's last layer in flow ends at its content box's end because one layer grows or the row hugs, the indicator draws as the row's last layer and the row's end padding binds the offset, `row-px`. The row must be a positioned flex row with no border that centers its layers and holds no label or icon of its own. The indicator may also sit first in the DOM beside the row's own text, as a Dropdown menu checkbox row's does, with no other part and no end icon: it then draws as the row's last layer, after the text, where the same arithmetic holds and the row ends flush, since one layer grows, the row hugs or the text fills. There the indicator may set no width, and the width it hugs, the one icon it holds between literal paddings, counts. A row that does not render the indicator keeps the room, `row-indicator-room`. Figma's `x` takes no variable, so an absolute layer could not follow `row-px` from one density to the other.

An auto start margin, `ml-auto` or `ms-auto`, on the last of a flex row's two layers ends the row with that layer. Auto margins take the row's free space before `justify-content` places the layers (CSS Flexbox 1 §8.1), so the row reads as `justify-between`, and a label first of the two fills the room the other layer and the gap leave, as a vertical Navigation menu trigger's title does. A label a TEXT property sets fills there whatever its sample's word count, under the conditions the label rule names, as the Dropdown menu sub trigger's `Share` does. One word wider than the room is the known limit this section names for three or more layers. The other layer is a length wide, or keeps its own width: it sets no width and neither shrinks nor grows, as a Command row's `shrink-0` Kbd group (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7), which a hugging frame draws. On the last of three or more layers, with the row's own text right before it and the row setting its text from the left, the margin takes the room after the text, which the row's layout records apart from `justify-content`, so a real `justify-between` keeps its label at its own width, so the text fills that room in Figma, which draws what CSS draws since a text paints nothing past its characters, and the row packs its layers at its start with its gap, as a Dropdown menu row's shortcut ends the row. The row must leave `justify-content` unset and the layer its end margin. The two readings need different proofs, each measured in every mode (#773). Two layers spaced to the ends need the row to leave the margin at least 24px (`freeAlongMain`), since where the layers and the gap fill or overflow the row, the margin takes nothing and the row keeps them packed at its start with the gap. A label that fills the room before the last layer needs only the row to fit its layers with a pixel to spare (`fitsAlongMain`): the margin takes whatever free space there is, or a label that grows takes it first (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7), and the filling label draws the same at any free space of zero or more. Where the layers overflow, CSS stops the text at its min-content width while Figma shrinks it. The capture measures that fit on the demo's sample text, and the label is a TEXT property a designer types into. A longer label of several words still draws alike while the last layer's text holds no space, as a menu shortcut's keys do: that layer cannot shrink below its own width, so CSS and Figma both break the label between words inside the room. A word wider than the room is a known limit: CSS keeps the word whole and pushes the last layer past the row's end (CSS Flexbox 1 §4.5), while the filling Figma text breaks the word inside the room. An auto margin anywhere else stays unmapped.

An auto end margin, `me-auto`, on the first of a flex row's three or more parts sets that part at the row's start. The parts after it end the row, with the row's gap between them, as Tour's footer sets Skip apart from Back and Next. An auto start margin, `ms-auto`, on the last of them sets that part at the row's end and the parts before it at its start, as a Date range picker's field box sets its trigger apart from its two inputs. The margin takes the free space before `justify-content` places the parts (CSS Flexbox 1 §8.1), so the row draws the same whatever `justify-content` it sets, and the build drops a `justify-end` beside it.

A Figma row cannot hold one layer at its start and two at its end. So the row packs two layers at its start with its gap: the first part, and a frame that `components/end-frame.ts` draws. The frame fills the rest of the row and packs the other parts at its end, with the row's gap and the row's alignment across. For `ms-auto` on the last part, the frame holds the parts before it, packs them at its start and comes first, and the last part follows it. Across the row, the frame is as tall as the row's one flex line. In a row of a definite height, that line is the row's content height (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.4), so the frame fills it and a `self-stretch` part fills the frame. In a row that hugs its height, the frame hugs its parts. The frame paints nothing, and its key is the first part's with ` end` after it, or the last part's with ` start` after it. Figma keeps an auto layout frame with no children at its last size (PE4, and EF1–EF3 in #821), so the build draws the frame only where the row draws a layer on its side. The frame takes over the fields its layers' reads fill and repeats the row's spacing and alignments, so an instance part's coverage tells what it carries.

The margin reads only where the capture measured the row leaving it at least 24px in every mode (`freeAlongMain`). The capture packs the row's items at its start while it measures. It reads boxes where Chromium paints them, so it records no room where a `transform`, `translate`, `scale` or `rotate` applies to the row, one of its items or a `display: contents` box around one. Where the parts and the gaps fill the row, the margin takes nothing and `justify-content` places them, which the frame would not draw. The row must not hug its width, hold its own label, an icon or a part out of its flow. The part with the margin sets none on its other side, and the other parts set no margin along the row. The part with the margin must not grow: a capped part that grows takes the free space up to its cap before the margin takes the rest (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7), as a `flex-1 max-w-[140px]` last part of a 240px row beside two 30px parts sits at x 100, where a Figma layer that fills beside the filling frame splits the room with it. A grow factor of 0, which `flex-none` and `flex-[0_0_auto]` alike set (CSS Flexbox 1 §7.2), is no growth. The part with the margin must show in every instance: a BOOLEAN property that hides it takes its margin out of the row, so CSS places the others by `justify-content`, where the frame would keep them packed. A row that hugs its height must leave each part's `align-self` unset. A part that stretches there fills a frame that hugs only its own parts (PF), where CSS stretches it to the row's tallest item.

A part the element holds after its box's own text, as a Dropdown menu row's shortcut follows the row's text, names the label in its `after`, a frame or an instance, as a Command row's Kbd group shortcut, and its layer sits after the label and the end icons. The flow and the rooms read it.

A popup that hugs its rows, a block box with no width of its own that stacks its rows and sets a `min-width`, takes the width of its widest row in CSS (CSS 2.1 §10.3.7), while a Figma column whose layers all fill hugs only to its `minWidth` (PM). A part names the row that sets the width in `setsWidth`. That row hugs, and so do the frames and sections around it, and every other part fills, which stretches to it (PH2, PF). The row keeps the popup's content minimum, its `min-width` less its padding across where that padding is the same length in every mode, as its own `minWidth`: a hugging instance with one fills its filling text up to it and hugs past it (PH3), so the row never draws narrower than its siblings and never wraps where they would. A popup that names no row hugs its `minWidth`, which its filling rows take. `components/hugging-rows.ts` owns the rule.

A grid that sets no track, template, alignment or column auto-flow, and whose parts set no placement, alignment or flex share, puts every item in one column, one row each (CSS Grid 1 §7.1, §8.5). Its one `auto` column stretches to a definite grid width (§11.8) and each item across it (CSS Box Alignment 3 §6.1), so the build draws it as a flex column packed at its start, as the Navigation menu popup's `grid w-64 gap-1` list: its parts fill a definite width and hug otherwise, `row-gap` or `gap` spaces them, and `column-gap` drops. The column is no narrower than its widest item's least width (§11.5), so a 256px grid whose item holds a 400px box draws a 400px column in Chromium. The build takes the grid as a column only where the capture measured `trackFits`: its column is its content box's width, and its column at the grid's min-content width is at least a pixel narrower, so every item's least width fits inside it. The pixel is `TRACK_CLEARANCE_PX` in `capture/page-clearances.ts`: Chromium set the same Roboto words 140.859px wide on macOS and 141px in Playwright's Linux image, so the page records the fact only with a whole pixel to spare, as it does for `ellipsisFits`. Without it the grid stays unmapped. Where the grid's height hugs its rows, it may also set `grid-auto-rows` to `auto`, `min-content` or `max-content` and `align-items` to anything but a baseline, as Card's header sets `auto-rows-min items-start`: a grid of indefinite height has no free space to stretch a row into (§11.8), a block's min-content and max-content heights are both its height at the column's width (CSS Sizing 3 §5.1), and `align-items` places an item down a row as tall as the item, so both drop, while the unset `justify-items` still stretches each item across. At a fixed height they stay unmapped. A grid with one layer under `place-content: center` keeps its centered auto layout, and any other grid stays unmapped. A list item, the `display: list-item` the browser's default style sheet gives an `<li>`, stacks its block-level parts as a block box does, since Tailwind preflight's `list-style: none` on its list leaves it no marker (CSS Display 3 §2.3), so a row link in an `<li>` fills it.

A grid of two or more columns draws as a Figma grid, `layoutMode` GRID, where the capture proves each column draws alike for its sample text and for text a designer would realistically type into its TEXT properties (`components/grid-layout.ts`). A literal length maps to a FIXED track. A `minmax(0, fr)` maps to a FLEX track of the same share with no measurement: CSS gives it no floor, and on 2026-11-18 a real file's FLEX track kept its 50px share under an 80px layer, a FILL text whose unbreakable word broke across lines, and a filling frame it squeezed (PG12), so the two draw alike for any content. Flexible columns whose shares sum below 1, or that hold a `0fr` share, stay unmapped: CSS gives them only that fraction of the free space (§12.7.1), so two `0.25fr` columns of a 200px grid take 50px each in Chromium, and no probe has shown how Figma's FLEX tracks share such values. A plain `fr` floors at its items' min-content width (CSS Grid 1 §7.2.4), their longest word, so it maps to FLEX only where the capture measured the column wider than that in every mode (`gridHolds`); a realistic retyped label keeps its words short, and a single word wider than the column, which CSS would widen it for and Figma breaks across lines, is a known limit. An `auto` column maps to a HUG track where the capture measured it ending at its widest item's max-content width in every mode, as rendered and with each text one character longer (`gridHugs`), since an `auto` column grows into free space that no flexible column takes (§11.8); a longer realistic label widens CSS's column and Figma's HUG track alike, and one long enough to meet the grid's other columns is a known limit. The rows are the grid's implicit `auto` rows, a HUG track each, so the grid must hug its height, and a grid that sets a limit on an axis it hugs stays unmapped, since CSS stretches its `auto` tracks to it: a 100px minimum height gives four 20px items two 50px rows in Chromium. A FLEX column in a grid that hugs its width stays unmapped: the typings call a FLEX track invalid there, and a real file kept such a grid's width with no error (PG3), so it would draw the wrong width. A Figma HUG track is as long as its layers that do not fill it: a filling layer never widens it, and a track of filling layers alone keeps its last length (PG2), where CSS sizes an `auto` track by every item, stretched or not (§11.5). So a track that holds a filling item maps only where one item sets it: the track's only item, or the one the capture measured longer than every other item in the track in every mode (`gridWidest`, `gridTallest`) where no item in the track holds text a TEXT property sets, since a longer realistic label in a filling item widens CSS's track and never Figma's. That item draws at its own length, which is the track's, as its stretch would, and the others fill the track it sets. The same Roboto file sets "Selection" 57.59px wide on macOS and 56px in the Linux image, so no grid fact rests on two font-set lengths being equal: a column holds its items, and an item sets its track, only with a 2px lead (`GRID_CLEARANCE_PX` in `capture/page-clearances.ts`). The items are the grid's parts, auto-placed row by row in source order (§8.5), and they must be the items the capture numbers in every mode (`gridItems`, `gridItem`), so a text or `::before` of the grid's own, which would be one more item, leaves the grid unmapped. A grid that sets rows, areas, auto tracks or another flow, holds an icon or text of its own, or holds a part that places itself, as `col-span-2` or `order` does, stays unmapped. So does a grid holding a positioned part, which CSS paints on top and the kit orders so, since Figma places a grid's layers by their order, and one holding an absolute part. An item fills its cell on an axis where the alignment is `stretch` or `normal` and it sets no length there, and sits at the cell's start where it aligns `start`, as `self-start` does, or sets a length. Any other alignment leaves the grid unmapped. A grid frame aligns MIN on both axes, the one value Figma takes there (PG10). `column-gap` and `row-gap`, or `gap`, set or bind the grid's gaps, `flex-wrap`, which only a flex container reads (CSS Flexbox 1 §5.2), drops, and a subgrid stays unmapped, apart from the rows below. `repeat()` is not expanded, so Tailwind's `grid-cols-N` stays unmapped. A grid's cells take the keys of the layers it lays out, so a part drawn inside a frame of its margins, or lifted from a wrapper, is its cell's layer.

A list whose items each span its two columns `min(50%, <length>) auto` as a subgrid of two cells, as Description list's responsive list and Items, draws as a column of rows (`components/subgrid-row.ts`). Figma has no subgrid. The first column is `min(50%, 20rem)` of the list's width and the `auto` column stretches over the rest (CSS Grid 1 §7.2.4, §11.8), and every Item spans both (`1 / -1`), so Chromium sets a 576px list's cells at 288 and 288 and an 800px list's at 320 and 480. In a horizontal auto layout two FILL layers split the row evenly whatever they hold (DL3), and a `maxWidth` of 320 on the first gave 320 and 480 at 800 and 288 and 288 at 576 (DL2, a scratch file, 2026-11-19). Padded FILL frames around FILL texts split it the same way, 16px across and 8px down with the term's frame capped at 320: 320 and 480 at 800, 288 and 288 at 576, and 200 and 200 at 400, for a one-word term and for a long term that wraps inside its cap (DL4). So each Item draws as a row, MIN on both axes with no gap, whose first cell fills it up to the `min()`'s length as its `maxWidth` and whose second fills the rest. The list is a column whose Items fill it, as a grid stretches an item over the columns it spans, so every row computes the same split from the same width and the rows line up at any width. A GRID frame of two FLEX columns, set to FILL in a vertical auto layout, splits its width evenly too (DL1), but its columns cannot take the `min()`'s cap. The list's `grid-template-columns` reads as the first cell's `maxWidth`, the Item's `display`, `grid-template-columns: subgrid` and span as the row, and each cell's `grid-column-start` drops, since it places the cell in source order. The rule maps only that shape: the percentage 50%, which is what two FILL layers split, a length that reads as pixels, no column gap on the list or the Item, which would take its share before the split, no `justify-content` or alignment on the list, a horizontal writing mode on the list, the Item and the cells, since a vertical one turns the columns into rows, no inline margin or padding on the Item, which would narrow a subgrid's edge columns (CSS Grid 2 §9), no border on the Item but the `border-top-width` of its divider, an Item that does not hug its width, two cells in columns 1 and 2 in source order with no width, margin across, border or self-alignment of their own, and cells that paint nothing. The cascade keeps `gap` apart from its longhands, so the rule reads the column gap from both `column-gap` and `gap`, and a `gap-4` on the list stays unmapped as a `gap-x-4` does: Chromium sets that list's cells at 288 and 272. The cascade keeps `border` and `border-width` apart from each side's width too, so the rule reads every declaration that can set a width, longhand or shorthand, physical or logical. A `border-[8px]` Item narrows its cells to 312 and 472 in an 800px list, and a bordered cell paints across the row's stretched height where the kit hugs it, so both stay unmapped. Under `writing-mode: vertical-rl` Chromium sets an Item's cells one under the other, so such a list stays unmapped too. The build passes no `writing-mode` down, so the rule reads it from the nearest box that sets one. An Item that sets a width, a minimum or a maximum stays unmapped too. A subgrid ignores them across its subgridded columns, so Chromium keeps a `w-[400px]` Item 576px wide with cells at 288 and 288, where the row would take the 400. CSS stretches each cell to its row's height (CSS Box Alignment 3 §6.1), and a block cell that paints nothing draws its text from the top either way. A cell must be a block that sets no `align-content`, since a flex or grid cell, or `content-center`, moves its text down the stretched height: in a row 100px tall, Chromium sets a `flex items-center` term's text 42px down instead of 10. Anything else stays unmapped. The Item reads its list's columns through its parent's style, an element around the root in its own set and the list's drawn frame in the list's, so the two give the same row.

A part whose element generates no box, `display: contents`, as Selection item's `<label>` around its control and its text, draws no layer: its parts lay out in its parent's flow (CSS Display 3 §2.5), keyed under its key, as `label/control` (`components/no-box.ts`). The capture records `noBox` where the element generates no box in every mode and adds no box of its own: no text, white space it preserves included, which `white-space: pre` lays out as an anonymous line of its own, and no `::before` or `::after` in flow (`capture/no-box.ts`). The build also refuses to lift a wrapper whose element the capture records with text of its own. Its cascade keeps its parent's path, and what it sets reaches its parts as CSS inherits it. Its `display` reads as the lift, and each of its own declarations that does not inherit and that no layer reads drops, since it has no box to set. One that inherits takes the reason any box's would, `cursor` that a static frame has no pointer (it inherits, CSS UI 4 §5.1), or stays unmapped. A non-painting pseudo-element of its own reads as any box's does. A wrapper the capture did not prove keeps its `display` unmapped, and one that shows a label, icons or a painting pseudo-element stops the build.

A frame part whose `box-sizing` is `content-box` sets its content box's length, with its padding and border outside it (CSS Box Sizing 3 §4.1), where a Figma frame's length holds its padding and stroke. On an axis where it takes a fixed length and pads or strokes, as Selection item's control slot sets one first line's height inside 14px of padding, the frame keeps its key, padding, stroke and paint and hugs that axis, and an inner frame keyed `content` takes the length and the layout of what it holds (`components/content-box.ts`). Where no padding or border sits outside a fixed length, a limit or a percentage, the sizing drops. A frame that sets a limit or a percentage length on such an axis, which CSS resolves for the content box and the build reads for the border box, as `min-h-[20px] py-[14px]` draws 48px tall and `w-full p-[10px]` 220px wide in a 200px column, keeps its `box-sizing` unmapped. So does one that lays out as a grid, sits in an instance part built in place, holds an absolute layer, rounds a corner by an infinite radius or a percentage, which the build resolved from the content length, or holds a frame of a fixed length there, which may be a percentage of the content box.

A radius per corner, by its physical or its logical name, rounds each Figma corner on its own, each bound to its variable: on the kit's left-to-right page `rounded-s-*` rounds the top left and bottom left (CSS Logical Properties 1 §6.2). An infinite radius scales as CSS scales the radii along each side (CSS Backgrounds 3 §5.5), on a box whose sides are literal or that hugs one layer of a literal size, and maps only where the scaled radius is within half the shorter side, which Figma would cap. On a box of a literal width whose height follows its layers, as Selection item's shell around its text, the radii map as written where the box's least height in any mode, as its layers and its own minimum bound it, leaves CSS nothing to scale and Figma nothing to cap: the radii along each side, a variable at its greatest value, sum within its least length, and each sits within half the shorter one. The bound gives each text one line at least, as realistic text does, and a layer a BOOLEAN property can hide no height, so a label a designer would realistically leave out counts as gone; a label emptied through its TEXT property, which sets no line in CSS, is a known limit. A grid's height bounds stack its rows. A corner longhand beside `border-radius` stays unmapped. A table, a row group, a row and a cell draw as auto layouts that hug their parts, rows down a table, cells across a row, a cell around its one child or its own text, only where the capture measured the parts tiling the box exactly in every mode (`tableTiles`), so no column or row stretches a cell (CSS 2.1 §17.5); every other table box keeps its `display` unmapped. A column that hugs one layer in its flow, with no `min-width` or `max-width`, is that layer's width, so its stretch needs no `align-items`. A row's item that grows takes the free space less its margins (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7), which a frame that fills the row, padded by the margins, draws around the item.

An auto margin between the two layers of a flex box, on the last layer's start or the first layer's end, takes the box's free space along its direction (CSS Flexbox 1 §8.1), so the box spaces its two layers between its ends, as a Sheet footer's `mt-auto` ends its column and a row's `me-auto` ends its first layer's side. Where the layers and the gap overflow the box, the margin takes nothing, so a column's margin and a row's `me-auto` and `ms-auto` read only where the capture measured the box leaving the margin at least 24px in every mode. A `max-width` the build cannot read as a length, such as a hugging bar's `max-w-full`, drops where the capture measured the box as wide with that maximum 24px lower in every mode; an intrinsic maximum such as `min-content` stays unmapped. Beside a fixed width, a percentage limit resolves against the preview's width, and it only folds into the width or drops, so a preview's width never becomes a Figma limit. `padding-inline-start` and `padding-inline-end` pad the left and the right of the left-to-right page the kit draws. A `currentcolor` that a custom property passes down resolves where it is used (CSS Color 4 §6.4): on `color` to the color the element inherits, and elsewhere to its own `color`, as Description list's term takes `--description-list-term: currentColor`. A part under `visibility: hidden` draws as a hidden layer where it is the one layer of a box whose width and height are fixed or fill, which keeps its size without it. A box that hugs such a part keeps the part's literal size and its own literal padding and border, which a hidden layer would otherwise give back, as an outside calendar day's cell keeps 36px. Figma's auto layout gives a hidden layer's room back, where CSS keeps it, so anywhere else the visibility stays unmapped.

Auto margins on all four sides of a flex column's one layer, as List detail's empty text sets with `m-auto`, take the room down the column and across it, so the layer sits in the middle at its own width (CSS Flexbox 1 §8.1, §9.6). The column draws centred on both axes. The margins read only where the capture measured the column leaving at least 24px along it in every mode, since an overflowing layer takes no margin and sits at the start, and only on a column that sets no `justify-content` or `align-items`. Across, an overflowing layer takes no margin either, so they read only on a layer that shows its own text and nothing else, sets no `width` and no `min-width` above zero, and lets its text wrap.

The centred layer hugs up to the column's content width, where a longer text wraps as CSS wraps it. A text that wraps is as wide as the cap in both: a CSS fit-content box takes the room it wraps in, and on 2026-11-19 a real file drew a hugging text whose sentence wrapped at its 200px `maxWidth` exactly 200px wide, and a short text at its own 32px (LD2). So a wrapped text sits across the column as CSS sets it. One word wider than the column is a known limit: CSS keeps it whole from the column's left, and Figma breaks it inside the cap.

A box's borders across count their logical inline sides, as `border-s` sets one, which a horizontal writing mode puts on the left and right.

A root that sets no height, inside flex rows on one line that set none either, is as tall as the sample's host: each row's line is as tall as its content box and stretches its child to it (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.4 steps 11 and 15). The root takes the preview height its manifest names, as List detail's pane takes the demo's 320px, where the capture measured the host as tall as that in every mode (`basisAsDeclared`). A row that wraps, aligns its items, pads them or sets a height, and a root that sets a margin down or aligns itself, leave the root hugging its height.

A flex container blockifies an item whose display is `inline` (CSS Display 3 §2.7), as DateInput's segments are in its field box, so an item that holds only its own text draws as a frame that hugs it. The build maps that `display` only where the capture measured `inlineRow`: in every mode, once the mode's faces have loaded, the row is a flex container that neither wraps nor reverses, the item is one of its in-flow items, and each item is one box, keeps its own text on one line, starts where the item before it ends or after it, and shares a band of the line with every other. An `inline` box in a block container, whose line box its font sets, stays unmapped (`components/inline-item.ts`).

Several layers of a row that grow, as Tabs triggers' `flex-1`, share its free space by their grow, which a Figma row draws only as fills in a row of a width of its own. Chromium sizes a row that hugs its layers as wide as their content widths together, which the capture's `itemsAtContent` measures in every mode. Where each layer grows from a zero basis (`flex: 1`), keeps the automatic minimum of its content (`min-width: auto`, with no `width` or `max-width` of its own) and sets text that does not wrap, so its min-content is its max-content, those minimums fill the row, so each layer takes exactly its own content width for any text a designer types into it (CSS Flexbox 1 §4.5, §9.7), and draws hugging its content. A row of a width of its own or with a `min-width` or `max-width`, a minimum of zero, a basis of its own, a width, or text that wraps leave the `flex` unmapped (`components/content-shares.ts`). A variant whose root casts no shadow leaves its layers unclipped where it holds one placed past its own edges, as a line Tabs trigger's active underline 4px below it, which CSS paints there; its set's other roots clip where a cell's root casts a shadow, which Figma spreads only from a clipping frame (`components/root-clip.ts`). A literal `min-*` or `max-*` beside a length a synced variable sets drops where no mode's value of the variable lets it bind, as Tabs list's 33px minimum beside `control-h-md` (`components/variable-limits.ts`).

A flex row's one layer with a `flex-grow` and `flex-shrink` of 1 or more, as Checkbox card's `<label>` sets `grow`, and no `flex` shorthand, basis, width, width limit or margin across, fills a row of a width of its own. Its basis is its content (CSS Flexbox 1 §7.2.3), and with no other layer to share the room it takes all the free space, or gives back what overflows (§9.7), so it is as wide as the row's content, down to its min-content width (§4.5), which a fill does not keep. A row that hugs has no free space, and the layer keeps its own width (`readGrowth` in `components/box-sizing.ts`). A layer with a `flex` shorthand, as `flex-none grow` sets, keeps its `flex-grow` unread: `flex-none` sets a shrink of 0, so Chromium computes `1 0 auto` and the layer overflows the row around wider content. The mapped layer's least width, its widths and minimums with their padding, gaps and stroke, must fit the row's content width less its greatest padding in any mode. Where it may not, or a text in it does not wrap, its width lands unmapped, as a 400px icon keeps its growing row item 400px wide in a 300px row. A column's one growing layer stays unread.

A flex row's part can fit its content to the room the row's other layers leave it (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7, CSS Sizing 3 §5.1). The part shrinks by 1 or more and sets no grow, basis, length, limit or margin across. It draws as a frame. It sits alone in its row, or beside parts that keep their width: `flex-shrink: 0`, no grow, no width limit or basis, no `width` but a literal one or one that binds a synced variable, as an `icon-sm` Icon button's size reads `--control-h-sm`, and no margin across but a literal length of 0 or more, which a frame around the part pads. A negative margin gives the room back in CSS, where no padding draws it. Icons may sit beside it too, as Toast's `shrink-0` status icon sits before its text column, where each keeps its width as such a part does and its margins across draw as literal padding. A `min-width` of zero, as `min-w-0` sets, counts as no limit: it lets the part shrink past its min-content width to the room, as a fill does. A zero margin across, as `m-0` sets on List detail's title, takes no room. The part is the row's last layer, in a row that packs its layers from its start. Or it is the first of the row's two layers, in a row that spaces them between its ends, and holds only its own text, as Tour's header holds its title before Close and Alert dialog's header holds its title before its icon. There a title that fits sits at the row's start and the other layer ends the row, and a longer one shrinks to the room and wraps there, which a fill draws in both cases. The row then packs its layers at its start with its gap, since Figma's SPACE_BETWEEN drops the gap beside a fill (PV). A part with layers of its own keeps its width in that place, since a text inside it can hug, as Tour's progress holds its count, and the build does not measure a hugging text's width. An `<input>`, a `<textarea>`, a `<select>`, a `<meter>`, a `<progress>` or a replaced element keeps its own sizing, since its width does not follow its text; a `<textarea>` is 176px around any text. A `<button>` sizes to its text as a block does, so it can fit. The row has a width of its own and does not wrap or reverse.

Such a part draws as a fill where the mapped part draws alike at any width. No filling frame in it paints, turns or places a layer. Each lays out from its start. A column may instead fill each of its layers with no width cap, and a row that centres or ends its layers may hold a layer that fills with no cap and so takes the room. No instance fills, each filling text aligns left, and each text wraps. No width, minimum, padding, gap or stroke in the part passes the room in any mode. Then its text wraps at the room as Chromium wraps it. Where the layers do not draw alike, the part's `display` lands unmapped, and so does the `justify-content` of a row that spaces it out first (`components/fitting-part.ts`).

Checkbox card's text column fits beside its control, and File upload's description beside its trigger. Card's footer paragraph holds its own text alone in its row. Radio row's label centres its radio and its filling content. Chromium draws each the same as its fill, line for line, in every mode, at the preview width, 120px narrower and wider, and at a width where the text wraps, for the sample and a longer text. File upload's `max-w-lg` holds the zone at 512px at the wider host.

A label of one word too wide for the room overflows in Chromium, where a fill breaks the word. That is a known limit.

A part of a block container is no flex item, so its `flex`, `flex-grow`, `flex-shrink` and `flex-basis` size nothing and drop (CSS Flexbox 1 §7), as the `flex-1` on Checkbox card's title column inside its text column.

A grid of one cell whose icons stack in it draws the one icon that shows, as Checkbox card's control crossfades Circle and CheckCircle (`components/stacked-cell.ts`). The grid sets no track, template, area or gap, has a fixed literal width and height and no padding or border, and holds two or more icons and nothing else. Each static icon sits at line 1/1 with no span, margin, self-alignment, padding or border, as large as the grid within its limits, so it covers the cell (CSS Grid 1 §7.5, §8.3). Each icon's `opacity` resolves to 0 or 1, and exactly one to 1. An icon at opacity 0 paints nothing, its filter and transform included, so it draws no layer and every declaration it wins drops. The shown icon's opacity of 1, a `scale` of 1 on each axis and a `filter` whose functions substitute to nothing, which leaves it at `none` (CSS Variables 1 §3.1), drop too. The grid's `display` reads as a frame around the shown icon. Any other grid of stacked items keeps its `display` unmapped. The crossfade between the two is motion, which the kit does not draw.

The Date range picker's field box wraps its row, which the capture measures on one line at the 384px sample in every mode, so the set draws a row that does not wrap. An instance a designer narrows below that row wraps it in CSS and clips it in Figma, which is a known limit. The sample sits exactly at the picker's 24rem: below it, `@max-[24rem]/picker:h-auto` gives the box a shorter height on one line, 34px dense and 38px comfortable for 36px and 44px, which a narrower instance does not draw either.

An italic label draws in the italic face of its weight, `Italic` or `Medium Italic`, where its family binds `font-sans`, which is Roboto in every theme, and Roboto ships an italic face for each weight in Figma and in `@fontsource/roboto`. Another family may lack one, so its `font-style` stays unmapped (`components/italic-face.ts`).

A label whose text sits in spans that color it, as Code's highlighter wraps each token in a span with an inline `color`, draws each run of a span's color as a range of the text, bound as the span's color binds (`components/text-ranges.ts`). The box cascade hands the label its text run by run, with the style of each element between the box and the text (`textRuns` in `capture/capture-cascade.ts`), only where such an element sets a declaration beyond the base layers', so a label whose spans set nothing keeps its one color and its coverage rows. The runs and the label's captured text come from one walk of the box's text nodes, which leaves out an element a layer draws and a hidden element's text, so the two always hold the same characters. A span's `color` that reads a custom property set nowhere, as `var(--sh-space)` on a space, computes as `unset` (CSS Variables 1 §3.1) and takes its parent's color. A text property a span sets that computes to its parent's, as preflight's `font-size: 1em` and mono `font-family` on Code's `<code>`, drops. One that computes otherwise, and any other declaration a span sets beyond the base layers', stays unmapped. A label whose white space rule collapses the spans' text stops the build, since its characters then give no index for a range. A line break takes the color of the character before it. A live probe on 2026-11-20 showed Figma painting a break that way, whatever fill a write gives it (NL1–NL4 below), so the ranges hold what the text can show and Plan reads them back. A break that starts the text takes the label's color, a cautious choice, since no probe wrote one. `font-size: 1em` and `100%` compute to the parent's font size wherever they sit (CSS Fonts 4 §2.5).

The capture records an element's text nodes joined between its element children, as `texts`, the text before each child and then the text after the last, where its `text` and `textIndex` lose some of them: a text node of white space alone, which DevTools' DOM tree leaves out and `text` trims away, or text on both sides of an element child. The page reads them itself (`capture/element-content.ts`). Code's `<code>` records the line feed between its line spans, and each space span its space.

A label whose `font-weight` reads a synced FLOAT variable, as Selection item's title reads `--selection-title-weight`, binds the text's `fontWeight` to it. Figma resolves the binding in each mode to the face its family names for the weight (the typings' `VariableBindableTextField`). The layer's `fontStyle` is the face of the variable's least weight, and its binding lists every face between the variable's least and greatest weight, so the plugin loads each before it writes the layer. A variable whose weights the synced set does not bound, or whose weights name no upright face the kit knows, stops the build (`components/weight-binding.ts`).

A menu popup pads its rows, and a separator between them cancels the padding with `-mx-(--surface-pad-sm)`, so it spans the popup. Figma has no negative margin. Where a column pads both sides with the length that its filling separators' equal margins cancel, at every value of their variables, the column draws with no padding across, and each run of layers between separators sits in a frame named `section` that fills the column, pads the run with the column's padding across and lays it out with the column's gap. `components/full-bleed.ts` owns the rule. A popup that pads nothing across and clips, as Select's does with `overflow-x-hidden`, clips the separator's overshoot (CSS Overflow 3 §3), so the separator draws the popup's width and its margins drop. So does a column that pads nothing across and fills such a popup, as Select's listbox fills its popup. Any other negative margin stays unmapped. `scroll-my-1` on a Select group drops, since no static frame scrolls. Base UI mounts Select's scroll arrows only while the list overflows, and the kit draws each popup with room, so no arrow mounts and no set draws one. A part at `width: 100%` keeps that width past a positive literal start margin, so it reaches past its column's end by the margin (CSS 2.1 §10.3.3). In a column that clips and pads nothing across, the clip cuts it there (CSS Overflow 3 §3), so it draws in a frame that fills the column across, pads the start margin and holds the part at its fill, as the Sidebar panel's separator shows from 8px to its content's end. Its end margin shows nothing and drops. `components/margin-box.ts` owns that rule. A bound or negative start margin, a column that pads across or does not clip, and a part with no `100%` width leave the margins unmapped.

A translate moves a box's painting and leaves the layout alone (CSS Transforms 1 §6). Where every row of a column, a flex column or a box of blocks, moves by one literal length across and no other transform, the column's paddings draw the move: its left padding grows by the length and its right padding shrinks by it, so each row keeps its width and alignment and paints where CSS paints it. Each Sidebar sub row's `-translate-x-px` turns the sub menu's `px-2.5` into 9px on the left and 11px on the right. A box that paints nothing and pads nothing across shows only what it holds, so it passes its rows' shared move up to its own column, as a sub row's `<li>` does. Rows that move apart, a move on some rows only, a move down, a bound padding and a padding the move would take below 0 leave the translate unmapped.

A part with `scale` draws its paint at that share of its size around its center and keeps its layout size. A part whose classes set `scale` in any cell becomes a transparent slot at its layout size, which keeps the part's name and centers a layer named `dot`. The dot holds the part's fill, stroke, corner, shadows, opacity and layers at the scaled size. Every cell then has the same layer tree, slot and dot, whether the scale holds or not, so Switch's off thumb is a 12px dot in a 16px slot and its on thumb a 16px dot. Only a scale the same on both axes maps, on a part with fixed sides, literal lengths, no shadow and no layers of its own, since Figma cannot scale a bound length or a child. Every other transform but `rotate`, which the next paragraph reads, stays unmapped.

`rotate` turns a part or an icon about its center and leaves its layout as it was. Tailwind 4 compiles `rotate-180` to `rotate: 180deg` and `-rotate-90` to `rotate: calc(90deg * -1)`, and the build reads those two forms and `none`. Figma counts a layer's `rotation` counterclockwise, from -180 to 180. On 2026-10-22 a real file read `relativeTransform` `[[0, -1], [1, 0]]` for a layer at -90, a clockwise turn in Figma's y-down space (PQ), so `rotate: 90deg` is -90 and `rotate: 180deg` is 180. A layer carries its `rotation` in the cells that turn it, as an open caret does. A turn of the root stays unmapped, since a variant sits in its component set outside any auto layout, and so does a turn in another unit or about an axis.

A child selector styles the icons it reaches, each with its own cascade. A child selector on a part reaches the icons of that part and of every part below it. `[&_svg]` reaches every one of them. `[&>svg]` reaches an icon that is a direct child of the part that carries the class, as the checkbox indicator sizes its glyph. `[&>svg:not([data-icon=inline-end])]` reaches only a leading icon that is a direct child of the part that carries the class. On Button that is the root's own leading icon, which `hidden` leaves out while pending, so the spinner takes its place and an end icon stays. An icon can also carry classes of its own, as Loader's spinner `<svg>` carries `animate-spin size-4`. They cascade with the child selectors that reach the icon, each by its specificity, so a box's `[&_svg]:size-4` (0,1,1) beats the icon's own `size-6` (0,1,0). `[&_svg:not([class*='size-'])]` skips an icon whose own classes hold `size-`. The coverage table lists an icon's own classes under the icon's layer name.

The capture resolves each longhand's winner, so the build reads one value per property. A value that equals the web code syntax of a synced variable binds that variable, so `px-(--control-px-button-md)` binds both side paddings to `control-px-button-md`. A radius rung compiles to the same `calc()` that the sync writes as its code syntax, so it binds too, and the compact and checkbox corners compile to the `min()` of `radius-compact` and `radius-checkbox`. A box's own custom property substitutes into its other values, so the field box's `rounded-(--field-corner)` reads the `clamp()` its `[--field-corner:…]` class declares, which binds `radius-field-box`. `rounded-full` compiles to `calc(infinity * 1px)`. CSS scales down corner radii whose sum exceeds a side, so it draws any radius above half the box's shorter side as that half. The build writes that half as a literal when both sides are fixed, 8px on Radio's 16px circle and 4px on its 8px dot. On 2026-10-22 a real file drew a 100px radius on a 16 × 16 frame and on a 64 × 16 frame the same as an 8px one, and stored 100 (probe P10), so Figma draws a radius above half the shorter side as that half, as CSS does. When one side is fixed and the other hugs or fills, the build therefore writes half the fixed side, and Figma draws it as CSS does whichever side ends up shorter. A box that fixes neither side has no length to halve, so its `rounded-full` stays unmapped. A `color-mix()` toward `transparent` binds the role tint whose code syntax it equals, so `bg-error/10` binds `error/10`, and Item's muted row binds `muted/50`. A mix of a variable without a synced tint stays unmapped, and a mix of a literal color reads as that color at the mix's share of its alpha. A length that depends on a percentage, such as `calc(4px + 100%)` in either operand order, stays unmapped too, since a Figma number field cannot hold a share of the containing block. Only a width or height of exactly `100%` reads, as a sizing. `w-fit` compiles to `width: fit-content`, the width of what the box holds when its container has room to spare, which a kit frame always has, so Label's `w-fit` reads as a hug. A `w-fit` box that holds only its text in a column's stretch fills it instead, as the parts paragraph says. A `max()` of a variable and a length binds the variable when the variable holds at least that length in every mode, and a percentage floor never binds. xs uses `max(var(--control-h-xs), 24px)` as its 24px target floor, and `control-h-xs` is 24px or more at both densities, so it binds `control-h-xs`. A shadow's blur and spread bind the same way, so the outline shadow binds the two outline shadow lengths. Values compare with the fallbacks of synced variables dropped, as Tailwind's own substitution leaves them. A background that is a literal color at alpha 0, such as `bg-transparent`, becomes no fill, because it paints nothing in CSS, and the coverage table records it as a literal. Any other value must evaluate to a number or a color, or it stays unmapped. `@elmeragroup/color` reads both the comma form of `rgb()` and the space form Tailwind compiles an arbitrary color to, such as `rgb(0 0 0/0.05)`.

The root inherits what the page and the elements around it pass down, as the capture records them: the docs layout's `font-sans text-foreground` on `<body>`, so the outline Button's label binds `foreground`, and Tailwind preflight's `line-height: 1.5` on `<html>`. Fuse's base layer sets `border-color: var(--border, currentcolor)` on every element, so a border that no class colors binds `border`, as the outline Badge's stroke does, and `coverage.md` names the rule as `fuse.css base layer`.

A border of no width paints nothing, whatever its style and color (CSS Backgrounds 3 §3), so it draws no stroke, as a Sidebar row's `border-0` does. A border on some sides, as the Sidebar container's `border-r`, a sub menu's `border-l` and a Number field stepper's `border-s`, draws a solid stroke with a weight on each side that counts in the layout, where each side's weight takes room on its own side, as the CSS border does (N9, PS). A table row's border on any of its sides, where its table collapses its borders (`bordersCollapse`), sits on the grid line between rows (CSS 2.1 §17.6.2), so its stroke stays out of the layout, as Figma's `strokesIncludedInLayout` false draws. The capture records the fact only where no other box that shares one of the row's bordered edges sets a border there: a cell, the row beside it and its cells, a row group, a column or the table could win that edge (CSS 2.1 §17.6.2.1), as a next row's 3px `border-top` does in Chromium. A row's border without the fact stays unmapped. A row group never records it, since its rows and cells share all its lines, so a row group's border stays unmapped too. A separated table draws neither (CSS 2.1 §17.6.1). Each padding stays as the CSS sets it, bound ones included. A logical side, as Tailwind compiles `border-s`, reads as its physical side on the kit's left-to-right page, the inline start as the left (CSS Logical Properties 1 §4). A `border-0` before a side's own width zeroes the other sides, and the side, written after it, keeps its width, so the stepper draws its left edge alone. A side's own color, as `border-s-border` sets `border-inline-start-color`, colors that side, since the capture records which declaration wins each side's color longhand. A border shorthand that wins a drawing side's color, as `border-left: 0 solid blue` before that side's own width, leaves the stroke unmapped, since the build reads a color only from a color property. Figma strokes every side in one color, so the sides that draw must share theirs, and a color that reaches only sides of no width, as a chip segment's `border-transparent`, draws nothing. A box that also sets a `border-width` other than 0 keeps that stroke, and its side widths stay unmapped. A solid border draws a solid stroke, and a dashed border a stroke whose dash pattern repeats one dash and one gap around the box. CSS leaves those lengths to the browser. On 2026-10-23 the compare run's Chromium drew 3px dashes and 2px gaps along a 1px border, 6px and 4px along a 2px one, and 6px and 3px along a 3px one. So the build writes 3 and 2 times the width below 3px, and 2 times and once the width from 3px. Chromium also stretches each side's gaps by up to half a pixel so the side ends on a dash, where a Figma pattern repeats the same lengths around the whole stroke, so the dashes along a side can drift a few pixels from the browser's. A dashed border whose width binds a variable stays unmapped, since its dashes would take other lengths in each mode, and so does any other border style.

An outline lies outside the border edge, `outline-offset` away from it (CSS Basic UI 4 §5), so a solid outline whose offset is minus its literal width lies over the box's outer pixels, where an inside stroke of that weight lies. On a box with no border it draws as the box's stroke, as Item's image media frames its picture with `outline-1 -outline-offset-1`. A box holds one stroke, so beside a border the outline stays unmapped. The plugin writes the stroke as an inside stroke of that weight and color, as it writes a border's, but with `strokesIncludedInLayout` false, since an outline takes no room in the layout, so the image media's portrait keeps the whole square under it. `outline-black/10` compiles to `color-mix(in oklab, #000 10%, transparent)`, a mix toward `transparent`, which keeps the color's channels and scales its alpha, since a mix interpolates premultiplied colors (CSS Color 5 §2.4). A share outside 0% to 100% stays unmapped. `@elmeragroup/color` parses the color and makes the mixed one, and it has no `color-mix()` parser and no mix toward `transparent`, so the build scales the alpha itself. No synced variable holds black, so the stroke is a literal black at 10%. The build spells Tailwind's shorthand `#000` and `#fff` out to six digits before the shared color parser reads them.

Tailwind composes `box-shadow` from one custom property per layer, the ring before the shadow, and CSS paints the first shadow on top (CSS Backgrounds 3 §7.1). Figma paints a later effect above an earlier one: on 2026-10-23 the outline Sidebar row's focus ring drew under its 1px border, and so did every ring beside `shadow-xs` (PE2). The spec lists a box's shadows in Figma's order, the reverse of `box-shadow`'s, and the plugin writes and compares them as listed. The spec owns the order because it says what Figma draws, as it does for a ring's clear fill, so a spec diff shows the order the file gets and no planner or check needs a rule of its own.

A box that stacks blocks paints each block's background and shadows in tree order, and a flex item above every block's background (CSS 2.1 Appendix E, CSS Flexbox 1 §4.3). So the open Accordion's focused trigger keeps its whole ring over the panel's fill in the browser. Figma paints a later layer above an earlier one: on 2026-10-23 a probe's panel covered the bottom edge of the ring above it, and the ring was whole once the stack set `itemReverseZIndex`, which puts the first layer on top (PZ2). A later block that is positioned or makes a stacking context, as `opacity` below 1 does, paints above the ring, as Figma's own order does. The build sets a box's `firstOnTop` where a flex block's layer casts a shadow and a later block that paints in flow fills, which in the kit is the open card Accordion item in focus. Where a block after the ring paints above it, or the first layer on top would let an earlier fill cover a later shadow, no one order draws the box as CSS does, so the box keeps Figma's order and its `display` stays unmapped with the reason. The kit check stops a spec written by hand that puts the first layer on top in a row, which no probe drew, or over a covered shadow, through the test the build runs.

`underline` underlines the label, `underline-offset-*` sets the underline's offset and `decoration-*` its thickness, in pixels, and its color. A length in `em`, as Link's are, takes the label's literal font size, so Link's resolve at the 16px host size. A color of `currentcolor`, or none, keeps Figma's `AUTO`, the text's own fill. In a mix, `currentcolor` takes the box's text color, so a mix toward transparent binds that color's role tint. A `light-dark()` color draws its light arm, and its coverage row names the dark arm, as `readDecorationColor` explains. A cell that draws no underline drops all three. `line-through` draws as Figma's `STRIKETHROUGH`, which takes no thickness or color of its own and draws in the text's fill at a thickness Figma chooses, so it maps only where its color is the text's own and its thickness is `auto`, which leaves the thickness to the browser, as an unavailable calendar day's number does.

The icon library comes from the same React components apps render. Node cannot load the bespoke icons' TSX, so the build bundles a small module with rolldown that renders each icon with `react-dom/server`. A Phosphor icon renders at 16px. A bespoke icon renders at its own size, and the plugin scales it to fit 16px. The library keeps an icon only when it paints with `currentColor` alone. Figma cannot read `currentColor`, so the icon's SVG paints it black and the plugin binds its color.

`coverage.md` lists every declaration with what became of it, and the part whose classes set it, so one class on two parts gives two rows. A declaration a part inherits and reads lands under the part that set it. Each declaration in a computed style names the part and class that set it, and the build settles a cell's coverage once it has mapped every layer, so a class that only a part two levels down reads still counts as read. Bound and literal declarations reached a Figma field. A dropped declaration has no Figma form, such as a cursor, a transition, the animations of Skeleton's `animate-pulse` and Loader's `animate-spin`, or the `animation-name: none` Base UI writes on an open Accordion panel, or a scrolling list's scroll padding, overscroll and hidden scrollbar, since a static frame does not scroll, or belongs to a state no cell shows. A popup's motion drops the same way: the fade, zoom and slide lengths its `data-open:` and `data-closed:` classes set, the `data-starting-style:` and `data-ending-style:` classes of a transition's first and last frames, which hold in no cell, and a `transform-origin` on a box that sets no transform, such as the popup's `origin-(--transform-origin)`. Beside a transform the origin stays unmapped, since the kit turns and scales a layer about its center. A `transform` of translations by zero, scales by 1 and turns by 0 moves nothing, so it drops with its origin, as a Sheet's swipe transform at rest. So does a transform the capture measured as the identity matrix in every mode (`identityTransform`), whatever expression writes it, as Toast's root nests its stack index in `calc()` and `max()`, which multiply its height and its peek by an index of 0. A `transition` shorthand drops as its longhands do, and so do a scroll-driven fade's `animation-timeline` and `animation-range`, a `caret-color`, and `scroll-padding-inline` and `overscroll-behavior-x`. `unicode-bidi: isolate` drops, since Figma lays out each text layer on its own. `background-clip: padding-box` drops on a box with no border, or a solid one whose color is opaque in every mode, which hides the fill under the stroke; beside a translucent or dashed border it stays unmapped. A list's `list-none`, which its items inherit, drops where an item resolves no marker, `white-space-collapse: preserve` drops, since a Figma text keeps its spaces and line breaks as written, and `flex: none` drops, since the item neither grows nor shrinks from its own size. `tabular-nums` sets `font-variant-numeric`, which has no Figma form either. `forced-color-adjust-none` keeps an element's colors in a forced-colors mode, which the kit does not draw, so it drops. The markers `group`, `peer` and their named forms emit no CSS and drop. A custom property draws nothing itself, and a read of it on its box or on a layer below counts on its declaration, so one that nothing reads only passes its value down to layers that never take it. It drops on a box that holds no part or icon, as the field box's `--shell-inner`, `--shell-corner` and `--inner-corner` do on Input and Textarea, and on a box whose parts and icons read none of it, as the same three do on Select's trigger. A `var()` resolves on the element that declares the property holding it, against the custom properties that element sets or inherits, and a child inherits the computed value (CSS Custom Properties 1 §2 and §3). So a synced variable stays its `var()` and binds, unless the declaring box sets or inherits a local value of it, which the browser paints in its place: `[--card:#f00]` beside `bg-card` fills red, and the default Accordion item's `[--ring:var(--primary-soft-foreground)]` rings the trigger inside it in `primary-soft-foreground`. A box's own local value leaves what it inherits as its parent computed it, so a part's `[--card:#f00]` below a root's `text-card` keeps the part's text in `card`. The root inherits the page's declarations the same way, computed on the page, so a root's `[--foreground:#f00]` keeps the page's `foreground` text. A `var()` with no value and no fallback leaves its property guaranteed-invalid (CSS Custom Properties 1 §3), and a child inherits that, so a part's `bg-card` below a root's `[--card:var(--missing)]` takes no `card` binding: the read stays unmapped, or takes the reading `var()`'s fallback where it has one. A custom property whose own value reads such a property with no fallback holds the guaranteed-invalid value too, so a Table cell's padding, `var(--table-cell-edge-ps,var(--table-cell-ps))`, takes `--table-cell-ps` where `--table-cell-edge-ps: var(--table-edge-px)` reads a container's `--table-edge-px: initial`. A property `@property` registers takes the value `unset` gives it there instead, its parent's computed value where it inherits and its initial value where it does not (CSS Properties and Values API 1). Where it takes its parent's value, a read of it reads the ancestor's declaration that set that value, so the coverage table counts that declaration as read. A local value no declaration at or below its box resolves through paints nowhere, so it drops, whatever a layer outside the box reads, as the warning and info Alerts' `[--ring:…]` reaches only the focus ring of a row that takes no focus, and as the Accordion item's does in its cells without focus. `position: relative` drops, because it only anchors positioned descendants, which auto layout does not need. The centered part, the absolute part at its parent's top left and the end indicator drawn in flow land as literal, and any other position stays unmapped. An absolute or fixed root's position, its `top`, `right`, `bottom` and `left`, and its `z-index` drop, since the root sits alone in its component set and the offsets place it in a containing block the set does not draw, as a Sidebar menu badge's `absolute right-1` and the Sidebar container's `fixed inset-y-0 z-10` do. The cascade splits `inset`, `inset-block` and `inset-inline` into the four physical offsets first. An absolute box with no width shrinks to fit its content, so where the preview width would make the root fill, its position stays unmapped. Opposing insets on an axis whose size is automatic, such as `left-0 right-0` with no width, size the root from its containing block (CSS 2.1 §10.3.7, §10.6.4), so they and the position stay unmapped there. A `translate` on the root drops for the same reason, as a Sidebar sub row's `-translate-x-px` does. `align-self: auto` drops on the root, which sits in no flex row, as Separator's `data-vertical:self-auto` does, and any other `align-self` on the root stays unmapped. `flex` drops on the root for the same reason, as Empty's `flex-1` does. `vertical-align` drops on the root, which sits in no line of text, as Avatar's `align-middle` does. A margin drops on the root, which sits alone in its component set, so the margin spaces it from nothing the set draws, as Field legend's `mb-3` does. A block root that takes the preview width is the exception: CSS makes it the preview width less its horizontal margins (CSS 2.1 §10.3.3). The build subtracts them where each holds one length in every mode, a literal or a synced variable such as `surface-pad-sm`, as Menu separator's `-mx-(--surface-pad-sm)` widens it to 144px, and leaves them unmapped otherwise. A part's `margin-top`, `margin-right`, `margin-bottom` and `margin-left` in a flex parent add to the space around it and never collapse (CSS Flexbox 1 §4.2), so the part draws as a transparent frame that hugs it with the margins as its padding, holding the part's own box as a layer named `box`. The frame keeps the part's name and its BOOLEAN property, so hiding the part takes its margin away too, as Empty's media takes the 8px of its `mb-2`. An icon's margins in a flex box draw the same way, in a frame that keeps the icon's name and holds the icon as `box`, as Toast's status icon sits 2px below the top of its `items-start` row for its `mt-0.5`. In a box that is no flex box an icon sits in a line of text, and its margins stay unmapped. The cascade splits a shorthand or logical margin, such as `m-0` or `mx-1`, into its physical sides, so each side cascades with the others and a style attribute's `margin: 0` resets a class's `mt-1`. A margin of zero adds no space and drops, as Item's title and description reset a `<p>`'s margins with `m-0`. A part that fills a column across draws its margins above and below in a frame that fills across and hugs down, its padding the margins, as a menu separator's `my-1` keeps it 4px from the rows around it. Its margins across stay with the part, for the separator rule above, unless the part sets no width of its own and neither margin across is negative: the column then stretches it to its width less those margins (CSS 2.1 §10.3.3, CSS Flexbox 1 §9.4), which the frame pads across too, as the Command palette's input sits `surface-gap-sm` in from its sides. The same frame draws an instance part's margins, as the Select popup's separator instance shows, and the composite owns the reads of those margins. In a block column, as Select's listbox stacks its rows, the frame draws a margin only where no other margin adjoins it, since adjoining margins collapse (CSS 2.1 §8.3.1). A negative margin across a flex row on a part that paints nothing and hugs its width folds into the part's padding on that side: the part's outer size and its content sit where CSS puts them (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.2), as Input group's trailing addon takes 4px from its end padding for its `-mr-1`. Across the row a part's outer cross size is its margin box too (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.4), which sizes the line and places the part in it, so a part that paints nothing and hugs its height with no height or height limit of its own folds its top and bottom margins the same way, as Data table filter's chip strip takes its 2px focus room back with `-m-0.5 p-0.5`. A height or a height limit can hold the part at a height its content does not set, so the margins beside one stay unmapped, one Figma cannot map such as `h-[10vh]` and one a logical property sets such as `min-block-size` included. A backdrop blur blurs what lies behind the whole part, its padding included, so a part with one paints, and its margins stay unmapped. An absolute part places itself from its containing block's padding edge (CSS Position 3 §3.1), which the fold would move, so a part that is one keeps its margins unmapped on every side. The fold moves the part's edge to its content's edge, so a part that clips cuts what its layers draw past that edge, as the strip cuts its chip's `shadow-xs`. The fold holds where the padding less the margin is 0 or more in every mode, and a padding that reads a variable binds the synced variable whose formula is that same sum of variables and pixels, `addon-px-button`. Any other negative margin, a margin beside a fill, another margin in a parent that is no flex box, and an `auto` margin, such as `mx-auto`, stay unmapped, apart from the auto start margin, the auto end margin on a row's first of three or more parts, the four auto margins of a column's one layer and the separator margins the paragraphs above read. `vertical-align` drops on an icon too, since Figma has no line of text and the box's auto layout places the icon. Meter's `align-text-bottom` sets its icon about 0.2px lower in the 14/20px line than the value's row centers it. An icon's `display: inline-block` or `block` reads as shown, and `none` leaves it out. An icon in a flex box is a flex item, which lays out as a block whatever its display (CSS Display 3 §2.7), as Alert's `block size-5` icon does. An outline in a literal color at alpha 0, such as Meter's `outline-transparent`, paints nothing, so its style, width, offset and color drop, as a transparent background becomes no fill. An outline that lies inside the box draws as a stroke, as the stroke paragraph below says. Any other outline stays unmapped. `aspect-ratio` drops when both sides are fixed, as Radio's `aspect-square` is beside its `size-4`, since the ratio then sizes neither side. Beside a width of literal pixels on a box with no `height`, `min-height` or `max-height` of its own, the ratio sets the height (CSS Sizing 4 §4.2), which draws as a fixed height of the width times the ratio, as Aspect ratio's 448px `aspect-16/9` frame is 252px tall. The build takes it only where the capture's `ratioHeight` measured the box as wide as that width and as tall as the width times the ratio, in every mode: content taller than that height grows an `overflow: visible` box past it (§4.3), and a flex row shrinks a box below its `width` (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.7), so a `w-[200px] aspect-16/9` box in a 100px row is 100px by 56.25px and its ratio stays unmapped. Any other ratio stays unmapped. `readSizing` in `components/box-sizing.ts` alone maps, drops or leaves `aspect-ratio` unmapped. A box the ratio sizes carries `aspectLock`, its width and height, and the plugin calls `lockAspectRatio()` on it once it holds those lengths, so its `targetAspectRatio` is the CSS ratio. On a live file on 2026-11-19 a locked variant kept its lock through `combineAsVariants`, its instances read it, and an instance that fills a host's width followed the ratio from 448px to 300px by 168.75px, as CSS does (PG18, PG19). `container-type: inline-size`, which Tailwind's `@container` writes through the `container` shorthand, sizes the width as if the box held nothing (CSS Containment 2 §3.2), and so drops a flex or grid item's content-based minimum (CSS Flexbox 1 §4.5): in a 100px row, two `w-[100px]` items each holding a 200px box stay 100px wide, and with `@container` Chromium shrinks them to 50px. `readSizing` in `components/box-sizing.ts` drops it only on a box whose width reads no content: one a column, a grid in one column or a block stretches across, as Card's header is, or a block-level root, whose preview's block host sets its width. Such a box may set a `width` of its own where it reads as a fixed length, a length or a percentage of that host's or column's fixed width (CSS 2.1 §10.2), as a Date range picker's root sets `w-full` in its 384px host. A `width` of `auto`, as `w-auto` sets, is the width the box takes with none, so it counts as none. On another declared width, as a percentage of a parent that hugs it, a flex item that grows or shrinks along a row, and a width that hugs, it stays unmapped. So it does beside a `min-width` or `max-width` that content sets, `min-content`, `max-content` or `fit-content`: a `w-[40px] min-w-min` box around a 120px child is 120px wide in Chromium, and 40px with `@container`. The plugin typings let a plugin read a text layer's `openTypeFeatures` and give no setter for `TNUM`, so the Kbd label keeps the font's default numerals. A layer's shadow that reaches past its root's top or bottom draws in CSS, where the root's `overflow` is `visible`, while every Figma root clips so it can draw its own ring (PR2). The build measures each layer's place down the root through rows, from the heights it holds, and lists such a shadow as dropped, naming the variant, how far it reaches and the modes it does so in, as the invalid Combobox caret's 3px ring reaches 1px past the 36px Input group at `Fuse density / Dense` and stays inside the 44px group at `Comfortable`. It measures with each variable's bounds across all modes and then in each mode of every collection with more than one, and trusts a measure only where each height, padding and shadow length it reads holds one value. A column places a layer after the layers above it, and the build holds no width, so it measures through rows only. An unmapped declaration is one the build cannot express yet, and a test holds the count at zero.

### Axis names and impossible cells

A variant's key is `set/axis=value,…`, and Plan and Apply match variants by it, so renaming an axis or a value deletes the variants it keys and orphans a library instance of them. Each name follows one of two rules, and `components/axis-vocabulary.test.ts` fails on an axis that follows neither:

- An axis that mirrors a Fuse prop takes the prop's name and values as the component's `api.json` gives them, a boolean prop's as `false` and `true`, such as Toggle's `pressed`, Checkbox's `checked` and `indeterminate`, Sidebar menu button's `isActive` and a popup's `side`. Each manifest's `api` names the component's docs page and the parts the set draws, and an axis named as a prop of one of them must list only that prop's values.
- Every other axis is Figma-only and takes its name and values from `FIGMA_AXES` in `components/axis-vocabulary.ts`, such as `state`, `content`, `form` and `icon`. A Figma-only on/off axis takes `false` and `true` too, so every on and off axis in the kit reads the same. A set with one static cell names it `state=rest`.

A manifest's `impossible` list names the axis values the component cannot show together, each with the Fuse or Base UI behavior that rules it out, and the set holds no cell with all of one entry's values. The kit rules out two: an armed Confirm button that is disabled, since ConfirmButton disarms during render while disabled, and a Checkbox both checked and indeterminate, which Base UI draws as the unchecked indeterminate box. A disabling prop that leaves a state alone rules nothing out: a Select, Combobox or Navigation menu opened through `open`, `defaultOpen` or `defaultValue` stays open when it is disabled, so those sets draw the open trigger disabled too. The capture, the probe, the build and the previews all read the cells through `possibleCells` in `components/component-spec.ts`, which stops the build on an entry that names no axis value, or an axis or value the set does not declare.

### Page layout

A component page reads as the pages of Obra's shadcn kit, which UX uses. Obra's maintenance plugin (`obra-shadcn-style-helper`) shows that kit's pages: a top-level frame per component named after it, text layers named `Header` that hold a component's name, and section labels such as `Examples`. It does not show axis labels, which the kit adds so a designer can read a grid without opening the properties panel. UX's Obra page for Button runs its sections down the page, each with its label at the left and its content to the right, and the kit lays its pages out the same way. Every section label sits in one column at the page's left edge, level with its section's top. The column is 240px wide, or as wide as the widest label and 32px more, as Figma measures the labels when the operation runs, so every section's content starts at one left edge and grows to the right. Sections sit 64px apart. From the top left down, a page holds:

- the title, a text layer named `Header` holding the page's name in `Heading/2`;
- under it, 8px apart, a text named `Description` holding the lede of the component's docs page in one line, and a text named `View documentation` that links to that page, both in `Text/sm/regular`;
- 32px below them, a rule: a frame named `Rule`, 1px high, filled with the `border` variable, running from the title's left edge to the right edge of whatever on the page reaches furthest right, so it spans the widest section, or the description where that is wider. The first section starts 32px below it;
- a section for each component set, made by a labels frame that spans it and its set: the set's name in `Text/sm/medium` in the label column, a header above each column naming its value on the set's column axis, and a header left of each row that holds a variant naming its values on the other axes, joined by `·`, both in `Span/xs/regular`. A column header sits at its column's left edge and a row header centred on its row. The page layout lays out the set's grid with them: each column is as wide as its widest variant or its header, whichever is wider, as Figma measures the header when the operation runs, so a header such as `disabled` never runs into the next column's. The row headers start at the content's left edge, and the set right of them, so each set's grid starts as far right as its own row headers need. The labels frame has no auto layout, which the plan sets back where a designer adds one, and sits below its set in layer order, so a click inside the set lands on a variant;
- the `Themes` section: the header `Themes` in `Text/sm/medium`, and the theme previews side by side, 32px apart;
- the `Examples` section: the header `Examples` in `Text/sm/medium`, and the page's example, a group, as a preview's, holding an instance of the default variant of the set its kit entry names, the set that draws the docs default demo where the page has one, such as Select (open).

Every node stays a top-level node of its page, as the plan finds it by its key there, so a page's sections are where its nodes stand, not frames that hold them.

The build reads each description from the `lede` in the frontmatter of the docs page's `page.mdx`, through `apps/docs/scripts/lib/page-frontmatter.ts`, the reader the docs generator uses, and drops its inline code marks, which the docs page shows as code. A kit page's docs page shares its slug, or names another in `KitPage.docs`, as Radio's names `radio-group`. A kit page whose docs page is missing or unreadable fails the build. The link opens `https://fuse.elmeragroup.no/components/<slug>`. The origin is the one the merge workflow builds the production docs image with, and a unit test checks the two agree. The plugin writes the link on the whole text with `hyperlink`, only where the text holds another. A real file on 2026-11-19 read the URL back as written and kept it through a `characters` write, a style link and a `fontSize` write (live probe 2026-11-19, HL1–HL4). Figma draws a text linked through the API in its own fill and with no underline, so the link takes the look of Fuse's `Link` at rest: its text bound to `foreground`, which `Link`'s `text-inherit` reads on a page, and an underline bound to `foreground/35`, offset 0.2em and 1px thick at the style's 14px. The plugin writes the underline after the style link, and again whenever it links the style or writes the characters, since a style link resets the underline to none and its offset to unset while an underline write keeps the style linked (live probe 2026-11-19, DS1–DS3). The preflight requires the variables the link and the rule bind, so a file synced without them fails Plan before Apply writes anything.

The docs sit beside the sets, never inside them, so no variant, property or layer of a set changes, and their keys start with `docs/<page>`, never `preview/`, so the snapshot export reads the same instances. A page the plugin first documents starts where its first set stands, and a documented page starts where its title stands.
