The layout reads every size when its operation runs, so a label's width, which Figma measures from its font, places the set right of it. The in-memory fake measures text by a fixed advance, so an empty Plan there shows the layout converges, not that the real labels fit.

The kit holds one docs record per component page, its title, description, docs link and rule, one labels frame per set, the `Themes` and `Examples` headers and its example, and the kit check refuses a page that holds a set and no record or two, labels that miss or repeat a set, and an example of a set or variant the page lacks.

A doc text links its style with `setTextStyleIdAsync`, since the plugin's `dynamic-page` access makes `textStyleId` read-only, and the run plans the text styles before the pages so a style it creates exists when the link runs. The link needs the style's font and every font the text holds loaded, which the run requires before its first write. A plan that creates or rewrites a style the docs link lays their page out again, since the linked texts change size. The title and the section headers are all named `Header`, and an unkeyed layer of that name is adopted by the first that looks it up only: a page's lookups adopt each unkeyed layer at most once.

### Text styles

The kit draws no component set for Text, Heading or Span. Designers set type with local text styles instead, which the plugin keeps beside the kit. Plan lists each style it would create, change or delete under `Text styles`, Apply writes them, and the Plan that follows lists none.

The build calls the recipes for every size and weight they expose. Text and Span come from `textVariants` and `spanVariants`. Heading comes from `headingClass` in `@elmeragroup/fuse`, the function `Heading` renders its classes with, which merges a level's theme roles over `headingVariants`. `src/components/text-styles.ts` applies the compiled rules of those classes, in stylesheet order, to one layer of `src/components/layer-style.ts`, the layer style the capture cascade gives every kit box, and `readType` in `src/components/typography.ts` reads its font family, size, line height, weight and letter spacing, the same reads a kit label's type goes through. A value that spells a synced variable's code syntax binds that variable, and any other value is a literal. The style also takes the element's `text-transform`, `text-decoration-line` and `text-wrap-style`, as Figma's text case, decoration and wrap style. `elementReach` in `src/components/utility-css.ts` reads each class's compiled rule. A rule that selects the class alone, outside any condition, applies. A rule that styles only the element's children or descendants applies nothing, as Text's `*:text-(length:--text-sm)` and `**:text-(length:--text-sm)` do. Any other class, such as `[&]:uppercase` or `sm:uppercase`, fails the build with the style, the class and the reason.

Each name is `<component>/<size>/<weight>`. The size is the recipe's, with `default` named `base` for the Tailwind step it draws, and the weight is the Figma style in lowercase, such as `Text/sm/medium`. Figma shows each `/` segment as a folder. Heading's levels 1 to 3 are `Heading/1` to `Heading/3`, because their size, line height and weight follow the theme. Levels 4 to 6 draw the recipe's default, which `Heading/base/medium` holds. Text's `bold` caps body text at `font-medium`, so it draws the same type as `medium` and adds no style. That gives 54 styles: 3 Heading levels, 27 Heading sizes and fonts, 12 Text and 12 Span. Each style's description names the recipe values it draws.

The styles bind these variables in `Fuse tokens`:

- Every style binds its family: `font-heading` for Heading, `font-sans` for Text and Span.
- `Heading/1` to `Heading/3` bind `heading-<level>-size`, `heading-<level>-leading` and `heading-<level>-weight`.
- `Text/sm`, `Text/base` and `Text/lg` bind `paragraph-leading-sm`, `paragraph-leading-base` and `paragraph-leading-lg`.

The rest are literals, because their classes read Tailwind's scale, which every theme shares and the sync holds no variable for:

- the font size of every Text and Span style, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20 and 24px for `xs` to `2xl`
- the font size of every Heading size style, 14, 16, 18, 20 and 24px for `sm` to `2xl` and 30, 36, 48 and 60px for `3xl` to `6xl`
- the line height of Text's `xs`, `xl` and `2xl` at `leading-relaxed`, 1.625 times the size, of every Span at `leading-snug`, 1.375, and of every Heading size at `leading-snug`, or `leading-tight`, 1.25, for `6xl`
- the weight of every style but the three Heading levels, as the Figma style `Regular`, `Medium` or `SemiBold`

The plugin owns every field a text style applies. Letter spacing, case, decoration and wrap style come from the recipe, which sets none of them today, so each holds Figma's default: no spacing, `ORIGINAL`, `NONE` and `AUTO`. Leading trim, paragraph indent and spacing, list spacing and hanging punctuation and lists, which no recipe reaches, hold `NONE`, 0 and false. A style a designer gave one of those another value is written back on the next Apply.

Probe PT1, on a scratch file in the Elmera organization on 2026-11-16, read how a real file stores a text style:

- A style keeps shared plugin data, so the plugin finds its styles through the same key rules as its nodes, in `plugin/src/identity.ts`. A style carries a key such as `text-style/text/sm/medium`, so a designer can rename it and the next run finds it, names it back and keeps its id. A style without a key is matched by name once, then gets the key. The plugin deletes a keyed style the kit no longer lists, and never touches a style without a key that the kit does not name, such as UX's `paragraph/regular`. Two styles with one key stop the run before any write.
- A style's own fields read a bound variable in each collection's first mode, while a layer that uses the style reads it in its frame's modes. The plugin therefore compares a bound field by its binding alone, and a literal only where the field binds nothing.
- A `fontName` write drops a `fontWeight` binding, and a literal `fontSize` write drops a `fontSize` binding. When any of a style's type differs, one operation writes all of it again: the font, then the literal fields with their bindings cleared, then the bindings.
- A description reads back with `&`, `<`, `>`, `"` and `'` HTML-escaped, so the plugin undoes those escapes before it compares a description with the kit's.

A style reads a bound family and weight in each collection's first mode, so the plugin writes the font they give there, Roboto Medium for `Heading/1` in the synced file, before it binds the weight. A style it creates or rewrites requires that font before the run's first write, and a machine that lacks it stops the run with nothing written, as for any other font the kit needs. A frame that pins other modes can pick another font through the bindings, such as Neo Sans Bold for a Heading level in a branded Fjordkraft frame. The run does not require that font, and Figma shows it as missing in such a frame on a machine without it, as it does for a kit label (#525).

### How the plugin decides what to write

- Plan and Apply run the same planning pass. The pass checks the kit against the file, matches each node and records every write as an operation, and it writes nothing. Plan lists the operations. Apply finishes the pass and loads every font the text writes need, so a duplicate key, a missing variable, a variable of the wrong type or a font this machine cannot load stops the run before any write. Then it performs the operations in order. Plan loads the fonts too, so it fails where Apply would. A label the run creates takes its family from the file's variable, which the pass resolves through the modes the new label will read.
- The pass runs as an Effect. The structural walk (pages, sets, variants, layers, instances and previews) is a set of generators that read the run's file, variables and glyph font as services and record into the `Plan` service. The field planners below them stay synchronous and record into the recorder their `Run` holds, so Effect wraps the walk and not each field comparison. An operation's write is an Effect. One that plans a new node's contents or an instance's later stages when it runs captures the services in scope when the pass records it, and plans into a recorder of its own. The run turns off Effect's cooperative yields, so it hands the thread back to Figma only where it awaits Figma.
- The build runs the half of the kit check that reads only the kit, `checkKitSpec` in `src/components/kit-check.ts`, so `figma:plugin` fails on a spec that contradicts itself before Figma sees it. The plugin runs it again at the start of each pass, beside the checks against the file's variables in `plugin/src/check-kit.ts`.
- Each field planner compares the node with the spec and records an operation only when they differ, so a second run over an applied file plans nothing. A fill, stroke or shadow matches only with the `NORMAL` blend mode the plugin writes. A text field counts as bound only when every character binds the variable.
- Writes that depend on each other share one operation. A resize or a width or height binding turns a box's hugging or filling axis fixed, so the operation that writes a length writes both axes' sizing again after it. A label's font write comes first among its writes and needs only the new font, so the plugin can replace a font this machine lacks. The spec sets the style as a literal, so the font write also clears any variable a character binds to `fontStyle` or `fontWeight`. A new node is one operation that creates the node and everything the spec puts inside it, so Plan lists it once. A new page is the exception: Plan lists it, and then each set, preview or icon library the run creates on it, because the plugin plans a page's nodes in the outer pass.
- The kit draws ⇧ (U+21E7) and ⌘ (U+2318) in Inter, the spec's `glyphFont`, since Roboto, the family the kit's `font-sans` holds, has neither. A browser falls back to a system font for a missing glyph and Figma does not: on 2026-10-26 a Roboto text `⇧⌘P` drew `P` alone, with a combined 13px blank advance for the two glyphs, and reported no missing font, where Inter drew both (G1). Each run of those glyphs in a label's characters takes Inter at the label's own style, and the other characters keep `font-sans`. `setRangeFontName` with another family unbinds the family on its range alone, so the text reads as two segments, Inter unbound beside Roboto bound (G2). A font write, on a range or the whole text, keeps a character's family binding where its family stays, drops it where the family changes, and binds no character (G8, G9, G10). A whole-text family binding binds only the characters already in the variable's family and changes no font, so it leaves the Inter ranges as they are (G10). A range bound to a variable that holds Inter keeps that binding through a write of Inter, and a null range binding unbinds it and keeps the font (G11). The plan reads a text's segments. It compares the label's font and family binding on the characters outside the ranges, and the ranges on their own. It writes the label's font first, then the family binding, then the ranges, which a whole-text font write gives Roboto, and then unbinds any range that still binds a family. Linking a text's characters to its TEXT property resets the whole text to the style the range writes left alone, one Roboto segment bound, at whichever end the glyphs sit, and the ranges set after the link stay (G3, G7). An instance at the property's default shows its main's ranges (G4). A TEXT write on an instance resets its text the same way, whatever the value, the default included (G5, G7), and a range set on the instance's text after the write stays until the next write, listing no font override (G6). So the plan sets the ranges on a main after its characters, and on an instance's linked text after its TEXT writes. The kit check refuses a label that shows a glyph and sets a literal family, and a nested TEXT value that shows one. It also refuses such a label whose size or line height binds a variable: a whole size binding gives the Inter ranges the value but not the binding (G12b), and no probe shows a Plan over that label converging.
- A bound paint matches by its binding alone, because Figma sets a bound paint's opacity from the variable's alpha. Two runs on a real file, on 2026-10-19, wrote `error` at 10% and read it back at 100%, which is why the kit binds role tints.
- An image paint names its image by a hash, and Figma stores the image apart from the layers. On 2026-10-22 a real file named the stored portrait by the SHA-1 of its bytes, read a written image paint back with that hash and Figma's defaults for every other field, and read the same once that readback was written again (probes P9 and P9b). The spec carries the hash, so a held image paint matches by its hash and its scale mode, besides the visibility, blend mode and opacity every paint is held to, and Plan stores nothing. A designer's filters or rotation on the paint stay. Apply stores the image with `createImage` in the operation that writes the paint, and stops before the write when Figma names it by another hash.
- The kit check holds each image to what Figma stores: a PNG whose signature and IHDR chunk are whole, from 1 to 4096 pixels on a side, the limit Figma's reference states. On 2026-10-22 a real file stored the first 100 bytes of a PNG and refused its 8-byte signature alone with `Image type is unsupported` (P9b), so Figma reads the header, not the whole file. The check also requires the spec's hash to be the SHA-1 of the bytes. A new layer stores its image only when the operation that creates it runs, after earlier writes, so the check refuses such an image before the first write.
- The build makes every variant root clip its content, through the box's `clips`, because Figma draws a drop shadow's spread only from a frame that clips and has a visible fill. A fill at alpha 0 does not count: on 2026-10-20 the focus rings of ghost and link held their 2px spread over one and drew nothing. A root with a spread shadow and no fill therefore gets black at alpha 0.0015, which Figma counts and which changes no 8-bit channel. A transparent background reaches the plugin as no fill, so a Toggle's focus ring gets the clear fill too. The plugin also sets a shadow's geometry again after it binds the shadow's color. Two runs stored the spread as 0, and the third, which did this, kept it.
- A box's `backgroundBlur` writes a `BACKGROUND_BLUR` effect, `blurType` NORMAL, after the box's drop shadows, and the plan compares its radius with the spec's. An instance's shadow override writes no blur, since no kit instance whose main blurs what lies behind it overrides its shadows.
- A variant root is an auto layout along the spec's `direction` that places its layers as the spec's `primaryAlign` and `counterAlign` say. `SPACE_BETWEEN` along the direction puts the spare room between the layers. On 2026-10-22 a real file kept it through an `itemSpacing` write and a binding, so the plugin writes the spacing after the alignment as before. The typings give the counter alignment no stretch, and allow a baseline only in a row. Every published root is a row. Each centers its layers on both axes, except Switch's, whose thumb's translate places it at the start or the end. A part below the root is a frame layer with the same box fields, and it can hold frames of its own. The build makes such a frame show its overflow unless its `overflow`, `overflow-x` or `overflow-y` is `hidden`, `clip`, `auto` or `scroll`, as a CSS box does. A `visible` axis beside one that clips computes to `auto` (CSS Overflow 3 §3), so a box clips both or neither, as Figma's `clipsContent` does, and the plugin writes `clipsContent` from `clips` on every box. A part that casts a spread shadow gets the root's clear fill where it has none, and clips, and the build writes both into the part's spec: on 2026-10-23 a real file drew a part's ring only over a visible fill with clipping on, and the owner's Accordion triggers had drawn no focus ring (PR2). `ringFill` in `components/kit-spec.ts` holds the fill rule for the root and the part.
- A box's `sizing` makes each axis fixed at a length, hugging its content, or filling the room its parent's auto layout gives it. The plugin writes it through `layoutSizingHorizontal` and `layoutSizingVertical`, which Figma reads as a projection of the sizing modes, a text's auto resize and the parent's grow or stretch. On 2026-10-22 the real file's Button roots read hugging across and fixed down, their labels hugging both ways, and the Separator and Switch boxes fixed both ways. A hidden child reads FIXED on an axis it fills, while it keeps the fill in `layoutGrow` 1 along its parent's direction and `layoutAlign` STRETCH across it: on 2026-10-24 the owner's file read Alert's and Empty's hidden actions so after an Apply, and a probe read the same of a hidden child in a column, a fixed row and a hugging row, and FILL once it showed (NH). So the plan reads a hidden layer's fill, a frame's, an instance's or a text's, from those two fields, and writes the shorthand as before. The build writes a fill where a part's `100%`, a column's stretch, a `w-fit` text box in that stretch or the one part that grows meets a definite length, and across a hugging row only for a part that stretches beside taller layers that set the row's height, as Item's actions do.
- The plugin writes a box's direction, then its alignments, then its fixed lengths, then its sizing, then its limits. A direction change keeps each sizing mode in its role along or across the direction. On 2026-10-22 a row 40 tall that hugged its width hugged its height once it became a column, and stood 14 tall. So the plan compares the spec with the sizing the box will read in its new direction, and a turn rewrites the box's fixed lengths after it. A turn also moves the fill of each layer inside the box to the other axis, so it rewrites those layers' sizing and fixed lengths too, labels included. On the same file a resize or a width binding turned a filling child fixed, which is why the sizing follows the lengths in their operation. No probe has shown what a box stores in its sizing modes, or how it sizes, when it first gets an auto layout, as every new box does, so a box without one writes its sizing whatever its modes predict.
- A box whose direction is GRID writes, right after its direction, `gridItemsPositioning` ROW_AUTO_FLOW, its column and row counts, each track's `GridTrackSize`, and its gaps, each a literal or a bound variable, and its sizing after them (`plugin/src/grid.ts`). On 2026-11-18 the owner probed grids through the Plugin API on a scratch file (PG, `@figma/plugin-typings` 1.141.0): ROW_AUTO_FLOW put four layers in their cells row by row in layer order, and a 200px grid of `[HUG, FLEX 1]` by `[HUG, HUG]` with 10px and 4px gaps around 30 × 10, 10 × 10, 20 × 10 and 10 × 10 layers was 24 tall with its second layer at `x` 40 (PG1); a HUG track read back as `{ type: 'HUG', value: 1 }` (PG1); a layer that fills a HUG track never sets its length (PG2); HUG beside a FLEX column threw nothing and kept the grid's width (PG3); AUTO put a smaller layer at its cell's start (PG4); a frame that is no grid read its counts as 0 and its size lists as empty (PG5); a bound `gridColumnGap` followed the mode (PG6); a hugging row that turned into a grid read FIXED both ways, took 2 FLEX columns and rows, placed MANUAL, and left its layers in no cell until ROW_AUTO_FLOW placed them, and an `insertChild` reorder reflowed them (PG7); a layer set to FILL in a FLEX column took its 160px share and followed a resize (PG9); `primaryAxisAlignItems` and `counterAxisAlignItems` threw `must be 'MIN' when layoutMode is 'GRID'` for any other value, `itemSpacing` took a number that lays out nothing, padding inset the cells, and `gridChildHorizontalAlign` CENTER and `gridChildVerticalAlign` MAX placed a layer exactly (PG10); a frame with no auto layout turned into a grid as an auto layout did, its layers keeping their old place until ROW_AUTO_FLOW (PG11); and a FLEX track kept its share whatever its layers held (PG12). So the plugin writes the placement and tracks before the sizing, and a box that turns into a grid writes its sizing whatever its old modes predict. The kit check holds the cells to the layer order and refuses a FLEX track on an axis the grid hugs. The kit check holds a grid frame's alignments to MIN, and the plugin places each layer in its cell by its `gridChildHorizontalAlign` and `gridChildVerticalAlign`, MIN, and never relies on `itemSpacing` there. The fake models these as rule 61, its grid reads changing nothing, and it keeps a HUG track's length at each layer sizing write.
- A text's `sizing` sets its auto resize. A text that hugs both ways is `WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT`, one whose width fills or is fixed and whose height hugs is `HEIGHT`, and one fixed both ways is `NONE`. The plugin writes, in one operation, the auto resize, then `layoutSizingHorizontal` and `layoutSizingVertical`, then a fixed width or height, then `textTruncation`, then `maxLines`. The label's font comes first, in an operation of its own, and its characters, or their link to the set's TEXT property, come before the sizing operation. On 2026-10-23 the owner's file held every filling text of the kit 0 wide, its characters one per line, written by a plugin that laid a new text out before its characters: an empty text under `HEIGHT` is 0 wide, and FILL never widened it, after its characters arrived or after FILL was written again, while a text given its characters first took the room (PZ). A filling text that reads less than a pixel wide and that the spec gives characters, as every filling text of a file applied before this order does, or one an interrupted Apply left empty, gets `resize(1, height)` after its auto resize in the same operation, which on the same file kept FILL and let the text take the room, and its sizing is written again after it, so one Apply repairs it and Plan lists the repair. On 2026-10-22 a real file turned a `WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT` text that filled a row into `HEIGHT`, kept a filling text `HEIGHT` when asked for `WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT`, and gave a resized `HEIGHT` text a fixed width with its height still on its lines. So a new auto resize moves the text's hug, and the operation writes the sizing and any fixed width or height after it whatever the text reads now, since a hugging text's size also follows a font the run writes first. On the same file a `maxLines` written while truncation was off changed nothing, truncating at the ending alone read one line, and `maxLines` null under the ending showed every line. So the plugin writes the maximum after the truncation, and writes it again whenever the truncation changes. The text's `align` sets `textAlignHorizontal` and `textAlignVertical` after that operation. On 2026-10-22 all 420 labels of a real file's Button and Icon button sets read `textAlignHorizontal` LEFT and `textAlignVertical` TOP, with `WIDTH_AND_HEIGHT` and truncation `DISABLED` (probe P0b). The plugin wrote no alignment when it created those labels, so a new text sets its lines from the top left, and a Plan writes no alignment on a published label.
- A frame's or an instance's `edges` placement takes it out of its parent's auto layout (`plugin/src/placing.ts`). The plugin writes `layoutPositioning` ABSOLUTE and the layer's constraints, MIN, MAX, CENTER, STRETCH or SCALE on each axis, before a frame's box and after an instance's sizing, since Figma refuses FILL on an absolute layer. Its parent places it once the parent's other layers are planned, in one operation: each axis's position, and on a `stretch` axis the frame's length, from the sizes the parent and its instances settle at when the operation runs (PI), since an axis other than `start` follows the parent's length. The plan compares each axis's distance, never `x` and `y` alone, which Figma keeps as the layer and its parent resize, a density switch included, so a wider label or another density plans nothing (N11 for MAX × CENTER, W5-P12 for MAX × MIN), and it writes the place whatever it reads wherever the run writes a field that can resize the parent. That write resizes a stretched frame, which turns both of its sizing modes FIXED, so a frame that hugs the other axis, as a placement wrapper hugs its height, takes HUG again after it. On 2026-10-22 a real file kept an absolute layer's place, size and constraints, threw `FILL cannot be set on absolute positioned auto-layout children` for it, left it out of a hugging parent's size, and scaled its width and stretched its height with its parent, in a variant and in an instance (P8). An instance placed this way lists `layoutPositioning` and `name` among its overrides, never `x`, `y` or `constraints`. A layer the auto layout places goes back to `AUTO`. The kit check refuses an absolute frame or instance that fills an axis. No real file has yet held a `stretch` beside a parent that hugs its text, or one whose end lies past the parent's edge.

- A frame's or an icon's `rotation` turns it. On 2026-10-22 a real file kept a 16px icon's box in its auto layout slot at 180, with its `absoluteBoundingBox` and its row's size unchanged, while its `x` and `y` moved from 34 and 0 to 50 and 16 with the transform's origin, and came back at 0 (PR). So the plugin writes and matches a layer's `rotation` alone, never its `x` and `y`, and two rotations a whole turn apart match. Figma lays a turned layer out by its turned box: on the same day a 20 × 10 frame at -90 made its hugging row 10 × 20 (PQ), where CSS's `rotate` leaves the layout as it was. So the kit check takes a half turn on any layer and a quarter turn only on a layer with two equal fixed sides, as every icon has, and refuses every other turn and a turned absolute frame, which the plugin places by an `x` and `y` that a turn moves.
- A frame's `pivot` placement places it by its centre. The plugin writes the place after the frame's turn and size, from the size those writes leave, and writes it whenever the run writes the frame's size, since a resize moves the centre about the frame's origin. Figma keeps a CENTER constraint's shift whole: an instance whose width changes by an odd number of pixels puts a centred layer 0.5px off, as a turned arrow centred at 20.5 in a 42px instance where 21 was its centre (W5-P11b). That one reading cannot tell which way Figma rounds, so the fake refuses to read a CENTER place whose shift since it was written is half a pixel, rather than approve a place the real file does not keep. No kit cell resizes a Tooltip instance by an odd width, since its popup's width changes only by its padding.
- A variant root sits in its component set, which has no auto layout, so it cannot fill. On 2026-10-22 a real file threw `FILL can only be set on children of auto-layout frames` for one. It also threw for hug on a frame without auto layout inside one, and for fill on a frame outside any auto layout. The kit check refuses a root that fills, and a frame or text that fills its parent's direction where the parent hugs it, since Figma would shrink that layer to its smallest size. Across the direction a fill in a hugging parent stretches to the length the parent's other layers set: on 2026-10-23 a real file stretched a frame that filled down a hugging row to the 40px of the fixed square beside it, followed the square to 60px, and kept the row hugging (PF). So the check passes such a fill beside a layer in the flow that sets that length, a fixed or hugging layer or an icon, or in a parent with a minimum on that axis, since on 2026-10-24 a real file drew a column that hugged from a 128px `minWidth` with its filling sections 128 wide (PM), and refuses it beside none.
- A text's `letterSpacing` holds the spec's percent of its font size. On 2026-10-23 a real file read a new text at 0%, kept -2.5% as written, and stored a pixel spacing as a 32-bit float (PL). The write needs the text's fonts, as every text write does. A label the spec gives no spacing plans none, so a published label keeps whatever spacing it holds.
- A text's `lineHeight` holds the spec's pixels, its variable, or its percent of the font size. A percent clears a binding the layer held. On 2026-10-23 a real file read 150% and 137.5% back as written, and the line box followed a font size bound to `label-text` in every density mode (PH).
- A text whose weight binds a variable binds `fontWeight` to it after its font, its characters and its glyph ranges. On 2026-11-18 a `fontWeight` bound to a 400/500 FLOAT variable showed Regular and then Medium as its mode switched, and a `fontName` written after it dropped the binding (PG8), so a run that writes the label's font binds the weight again. The glyph ranges of a weighted label, as `⌘` in `⌘K`, take the face the weight names in the layer's modes, Inter Medium at 500, before the binding: on 2026-11-18 a whole-text `fontWeight` binding written after `⌘` was set to Inter bound every range, and a mode switch moved `⌘` to Inter Medium and the rest to Roboto Medium (PG13). No probe has written a range's font after binding its weight, so the plugin takes a range font write to drop that range's binding, as a whole `fontName` write drops the text's, and a run that writes a glyph range's font binds the whole text again. It never binds a range's weight, so the weight binding comes after every font write. A label an instance shows, where the instance's TEXT property sets other characters, takes the same: the TEXT write resets the label to one style (G5), and the run then writes its glyph ranges in the face the weight names and binds the weight again. The run loads every face the binding can select, in the label's family and, where its characters or those an instance's TEXT property gives it hold a glyph, the glyph font's, before its first write, so a machine that lacks one, such as a face only another mode shows, stops the run before it writes.
- A text whose characters take colors of their own, as Code's do, writes its color as one `fills` write, which gives every character the text's color, then each range's with `setRangeFills`, its paint bound through `setBoundVariableForPaint`, as the typings say a range's fills bind. On 2026-11-19 a real file's bound ranges survived `fontSize` and `fontName` writes, followed the frame's light and dark modes, and showed on an instance, which followed later range writes on its main. A whole `fills` write reset them, and a `characters` write, even of the same text, gave every character the first character's fill. So the ranges come after every other write of the label, and a label any earlier operation of the run writes takes them all again. A label whose font the run replaces needs only the new font for them, so a run can repair a label in a font Figma cannot load. The plan reads the text's runs through `getStyledTextSegments(['fills'])` and writes nothing where each run shows the spec's color. Figma paints a line break in the fill of the character before it (live probe 2026-11-20 on `ab;\ncd`). Coloring `;` red read `[2,4]` red, the break included (NL1). `setRangeFills(3, 4, black)` on the break left it red and read it back red (NL2). Coloring only the break red was dropped, and the whole text read black (NL3). The character after a break kept its own fill (NL4). So the spec gives each break the color before it, and the fake shows that color on a break in every read.
- A text's `maxWidth` follows its sizing in the operation that writes its auto resize, so a hugging text wraps at it. The plugin removes one the spec drops, and the kit check holds a bound one to a FLOAT variable before any write, as it holds every bound field.
- A box's `limits` set `minWidth`, `maxWidth`, `minHeight` and `maxHeight`, each a literal or a bound variable, and the plugin removes a limit the spec drops. On 2026-10-22 a real file kept `maxWidth` on a hugging frame and on a text, which wrapped at it, kept `minHeight` on a column root, and read a bound `maxWidth` as the variable's value. The kit check refuses a limit on an axis the box fixes, because Figma clamps the fixed length to the limit and the plan would resize the box on every run. CSS resolves a `width` against its own `min-width` and `max-width`, so such a limit belongs in the fixed length, and the build folds it in there.
- A stroke's `dashPattern` holds a dashed stroke's dash and gap, and is empty for a solid stroke. On 2026-10-22 a real file kept `[4, 4]` on a frame with a 1px solid stroke, kept it once the stroke's color was bound to `border`, took `[2, 2]` with the binding kept, and cleared it with `[]` (PD). Writing the paint leaves the pattern, so the plugin plans the two apart, and a pattern matches only with the same lengths in the same order. A box the spec gives no stroke keeps the pattern it holds, since it draws no stroke.
- A stroke on some sides plans its four weights as one change, such as `stroke weights → 0 0 0 1`, when any side differs. On 2026-10-25 a new variant read every side at the default 1, a 0 written to one side set every side to 0, and after an explicit `strokeWeight` each side wrote alone (PS2). So the change writes `strokeWeight` at the largest side's weight, then every side, the ones that already matched included. A stroke on every side writes `strokeWeight`, or binds every side to one variable, so each side ends at the same weight.
- A bound shadow is written with the color its variable takes in the node, and matches only while it stores that color. Figma draws the color a binding stores. A new binding stores the variable's value, but an effect written again with the binding it already holds keeps the color written. On 2026-10-20, 28 focus rings bound to `ring` drew black where `ring` is gray.
- Each axis lists the recipe's default first, and the plugin keeps each set's variants in spec order. Figma makes the top-left variant the set's default, so an instance a designer places starts as the default Button.
- Figma keeps a variant property's values in an order of its own, which the plugin cannot change. A new value goes last, and moving the variants or renaming a value leaves it where it is, as a real file showed on 2026-10-20. A set the plugin creates lists its values in spec order. The test file's Button set gained xs and lg in a later run, so its size menu reads default, sm, xs, lg until the set is created again.
- The plugin draws each icon component from its markup with `createNodeFromSvg`. A run on a real file on 2026-10-20 showed that this returns a frame the size of the SVG, painted white and clipping, whose layers take their names from the elements' `id`s. The plugin moves the layers into the component, scales them to fit and centers them, and keeps a hash of the markup in shared plugin data, so a changed icon is drawn again. Each icon flattens to one vector named `glyph`, whose fill binds `foreground`, and the glyph scales with its component. The plugin sets an icon's size with `resize`, which applies the glyph's constraints, so an icon a designer scaled comes back to 16px with its glyph. Figma's reference pages say `resizeWithoutConstraints` leaves the children as they are, which would leave the glyph at the scaled size.
- An icon layer is an instance of an icon component, linked to the set's swap property, so a designer swaps the icon from the properties panel. The swap properties offer every icon in the library. The layer overrides its glyph's fill with the label's color, as a Fuse icon takes `currentColor`. Every glyph has the same name, so the override holds across a swap: on 2026-10-20 a destructive Button swapped from Plus to Star kept its glyph bound to `error`. The swap leaves the instance with explicit overrides of the icon's name and the glyph's fill, even after swapping back. Resetting the overrides of the nested icon through the Plugin API also drops the variant's color, and the glyph reverts to `foreground`.
- A set's BOOLEAN property shows or hides the frames and icons that link their `visible` to it. The plugin adds a property the set lacks with `addComponentProperty`, gives one it holds the spec's default, and links each optional frame's or icon's `visible` to the property's id, as it links a label's characters to a TEXT property. On 2026-10-22 a real file returned the property's name and an id from the add, kept the link on a layer in each of two variants, and listed the property on an instance at its default (PB). Setting it false on the instance hid the layer and its 8px gap, so the hugging instance went from 102 to 24px wide. A property that starts false hides the variant's own layer too: a hugging component of two layers and a 4px gap went from 54 to 20px wide (PQ). The probe environment sets `figma.skipInvisibleInstanceChildren`, under which an instance's `children` leaves a hidden layer out. The plugin never sets it, so it runs with the typings' default, false, and an instance lists the hidden layer, which reads `visible` false. So the walks that read sizes and find an instance layer's sublayers by key reach a hidden layer too, and the snapshot export stops at each preview instance. On 2026-10-25 an icon instance took a BOOLEAN's `visible` and an INSTANCE_SWAP's `mainComponent` together, hid with its gap while the property was false, and kept both links and the swap value through a swap and a hide (PI2). The plan finds each property's id by its name, so the kit check refuses a set that gives two properties one name.
- An instance layer is an instance of an earlier kit set's variant inside a variant, such as an Alert's action Button, so the composite stays linked to the set that draws the part. `walkLayers` stops at it, since the inner set owns what it holds. The plugin plans the sets in kit order, so the kit lists each set after the sets whose variants it holds. The kit check refuses an instance of the set itself, of a set the kit lists later or not at all, and of a variant the inner set lacks. It also refuses a property the inner set lacks or defines as another type, a swap to an icon the library lacks, and an override at a key path the inner variant lacks or on a layer that takes no such override.
- The plugin plans an instance layer in stages, each against what the stage before leaves: its main component, then its properties, then its overrides and sizing. It creates a new one with `createInstance`, and swaps one that shows another component with `swapComponent`, which on 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24 kept a nested instance's node id, its TEXT and BOOLEAN values, its visibility link, its exposure and its swap value (N1, NS). The properties stage reads `componentProperties`, matches each spec name to its `Name#id`, and sets every TEXT, BOOLEAN and INSTANCE_SWAP value that differs in one `setProperties`, a property the spec leaves out at its default. That operation reads each id from the inner set when it runs, so a property the run adds to that set works. A TEXT value writes the inner labels' characters, and an alignment override a label's lines, which needs every font a label holds, so the run loads the spec's fonts and each font an existing label holds, a designer's included, before its first write. A swap value replaced the nested icon and kept its glyph's override (NS), so a property write can replace the sublayers the last stage colors. So a stage waits for the operations before it: when the instance is new, swaps, gets a property write, or shows a set or icon the run changes first, such as an inner fill, a default property, or an icon the run draws again or whose glyph it paints, the later stages are planned when that operation runs, against the sublayers it leaves. An icon layer of a variant waits for its icon's draw or repaint the same way, since its glyph shows the component's paint where its color equals it (NS). Plan lists that operation, such as `plan again once Button's changes land`, and the rest compare now.
- In the last stage, an override finds its layer by the key of the inner variant's layer at the same place, and the box planners write its paint, stroke weights, corner or shadows, a text's face, the instance's own `primaryAxisAlignItems`, and a text's `textAlignHorizontal`, so a held override plans nothing. An override survived an edit of the same field in the main (N3), which keeps the composite's overrides through an in-place edit of the inner variant. `overrides` listed the fields an instance and its sublayers override (N1, N3), so the plan writes a paint, stroke weights, face, corner or shadow the spec dropped back to the inner variant's own value. A fill written equal to the main's is no override (NS), so that field follows the main again. A side's stroke weight written back stays listed (SW), and the next Plan compares the value. A text's alignment differs: on 2026-10-24 a CENTER written on an instance's label stayed through the main's alignment and character edits, and the field stayed listed in `overrides` after the main's own value was written on it (NA), so an alignment the spec drops goes back to the main's value and stays overridden, and the next Plan, which reads it equal to that value, lists nothing. An alignment along the instance's direction that the spec drops goes back to the main's value too, though no probe has yet shown whether that write clears the override or leaves it listed, as a text's alignment stays. The kit check refuses two overrides of one field on one layer, which the plan would write in turn on every run.
- An instance layer's nested values plan in the last stage. Each finds its instance by the kit's keys through frames and nested instances when its operation runs, since a variant switch gives nested sublayers new ids (PN), and sets the TEXT values that differ, with one change line per instance. Before any write, the plan loads the fonts the labels of each instance it writes hold, and of every instance it reaches where an earlier write, the main or the instance's own properties, runs first and can replace them. An instance whose values already hold loads none. The kit check refuses a nested value at a path the inner variant lacks, through an instance that is not exposed, ending at a layer that is no instance, or for a TEXT property the nested set does not define. A nested value the spec drops stays on the instance until a designer resets it.
- An instance layer can carry a `minWidth`, as a popup's widest row does. The plugin writes it on the instance's root, or the inner variant's own where the spec gives none, and binds it as it binds a frame's limits. On 2026-10-25 a real file listed `minWidth` and `minHeight` among the instance's overrides after a `minWidth` write alone, and kept both listed after a reset (PH3), so the plan reads neither as an override to drop.
- An instance layer can carry a `maxWidth` too, the widest width of the inner root built in place where only it among the root's limits differs, as a chip value's `max-w-56` caps its Button at 224px. The plugin writes it on the instance, or the inner variant's own where the spec gives none, as it writes a `minWidth`. Every other difference of the root's limits stays unmapped. On 2026-11-20 a real file listed `maxWidth` and `maxHeight` among an instance's overrides after a `maxWidth` write alone, and kept both listed after the main's value was written back (SW), so the plan reads neither as an override to drop. An override keeps the instance's value through a change of the main's (N3), so the plan writes the main's height cap, its value or its binding, on an instance that holds the `maxHeight` override. No probe has changed a main's height cap under one. No real file has run the plugin's write yet, so the owner's Apply is its check.
- An instance layer's sizing writes FIXED before a bound length, since a nested instance that hugged its height kept the hug height when its height bound `row-h` (N4), and writes FILL after any resize. An axis that turns FIXED held its hug length under a binding it already had (N4), so the plugin writes that axis's length or binding whatever it reads. A frame that hugged its height turned FIXED at `row-h`'s value instead (NS), so a frame keeps its order, the length and then the sizing. Its visibility links the outer set's BOOLEAN property as a frame's does. `isExposedInstance` read back true on an instance inside a component in a set, and an outer instance listed it in `exposedInstances` (N5).
- A change line shows what the layer holds after `holds`, so the list Apply shows for a field that does not stick tells what Figma kept.
- The page layout lays out each set's grid and reads every size when its operation runs. A plan that changes a component set therefore lays its page out again, and so does a plan that changes a set whose variants its instance layers show, or anything else on the page, as [Page layout](#page-layout) describes. The `Icons` page holds the library alone, which needs no layout.
- A set's grid and the page layout read the size of every instance inside a variant or a preview, the deepest first, before the size around it. Within one run an instance takes its main component's new size only when it is read, and a frame or component sizes from its instances as they stand. On 2026-10-23 an Apply that shrank variants stacked the previews by their old heights, and the next Plan placed them again on seven pages (PI). The same day a component that nested another set's variant kept its old width until the nested instance was read (N2).
- A preview instance whose frame's modes give it another size than its variant holds its own lengths. On 2026-10-26, after 531ea20d narrowed the Combobox sets to 366 and 374, the owner's comfortable previews kept the old 404 and 412 while the dense ones followed, and Plan read clean. A probe showed that such an instance lists `width` and `height` among its overrides once read, keeps a literal length when its main's changes, and moves only on a write of its own (G13). A bound length follows its variable, and an instance whose main unbinds keeps its last length unbound until a binding comes back (G14, G15). An instance reads its main's length binding whatever its modes (G16), and a swap gives an instance its modes hold the new variant's size (G17), while an instance resized itself keeps its lengths through a swap (G18). An instance that unbinds its own width keeps its length, reads unbound and lists `boundVariables`, `height` and `width`, and a HUG on it drops the binding (G19). So the plugin sizes each preview instance as it sizes an instance layer: an axis its variant root fixes is written FIXED first where the instance hugs or fills it, as N4 showed a hugging instance whose length or binding already read as the spec's. A literal length is then resized where it differs, or written whatever it reads on an axis that turned FIXED, a bound one is bound again where the instance reads no binding, and the other axis's sizing is written back.
- The plugin reads only the variable collections the kit names, and stops when two local collections share one of those names.
- Every node the plugin creates carries a key in shared plugin data under the `fuse` namespace. The plugin finds a node by its key first, so a designer can rename a layer. A node without a key matches by name once, and the plugin then writes the key onto it.
- A variant's key names its set and each axis value, such as `button/variant=default,size=sm,state=rest,icon=none`. An axis a definition lists in `addedAxes`, one the set gained after a file first held it, stays out of the key at its first value, so the variants the set held before keep their keys and the plugin renames them in place to name the new axis, while the variant name lists every axis. Select trigger's `width` is one: its hugging variants keep keys such as `select-trigger/size=sm,state=rest,content=placeholder,open=false`, and its filling variants carry `width=fill`. A real file kept a variant renamed that way under its id, and a designer's instance of it linked, reading the new axis at its first value, and `setProperties` on the new axis swapped the instance to the added variant (W5-P2). A new key would have deleted the variant and left those instances without their main. `figma:compare` reads a key that leaves an added axis out at the axis's first value.
- A layer inside an instance takes its key from the main component's layer at the same place. The plugin walks an instance's children beside its main's, through frames and nested instances, and stops when the two lists differ in length. A sublayer reads its main layer's shared plugin data (N3), but on 2026-10-25 the owner's Apply stopped on a swapped icon that read its new icon's key, `icon/CaretDown`, where the Icon button's layer at that place held `icon-only` (PK). The instance's children still stood one for one, in order, for its main's. Where a designer deleted the main, whose layers can no longer be read, each sublayer gives its own key.
- A frame layer's layers match among that frame's children alone, so a key needs to be unique only among its siblings, and a designer can rename a nested layer too. A layer the spec moves to another frame is one delete in the old frame and one create in the new one, so the layer gets a new node id.
- The plugin stops when two nodes share a key, such as after a designer duplicates a variant. It never deletes a node without a key, and warns about one that sits among the kit's variants, layers, icons or preview instances. It deletes a keyed node the spec no longer lists.
- It edits the component set and its variants in place, so their node ids and component keys survive and instances in other files keep following them.
- A kit page carries a key too, such as `page/button`, and the plugin finds a page by its key alone, never by its name. A page a designer named `Button`, such as one imported from another file, stays theirs, and the plugin creates its own `Button` page beside it.
- The plugin loads every kit page before it reads one, as the manifest's `dynamic-page` access requires. It finds a set, a preview or the icon library by its key on any kit page. A node it finds on another kit page moves to the page the spec puts it on, and Plan lists the move as one line, such as `move Button to the Button page`. The move keeps the node, so its id, its variants' ids and its component key survive. It deletes a keyed node the spec no longer lists on any kit page.
- A run that moves a node takes two phases, because a node reads its variable modes through its page, and a ring color or a variant's size can differ between pages. Apply first creates the missing pages, deletes the stale keyed nodes and makes the moves. Then it plans the rest of the kit against the file as moved and applies it, so one Apply converges. Plan writes nothing, so it lists the first phase and ends with `plan the rest of the kit again once the 3 moves land`. Apply's change list holds both phases. A run without a move takes one phase.
- A kit page the spec no longer lists, such as `Fuse components` (`page/components`), where the kit sat before each component got a page, or `Label` (`page/label`), where Label sat before Field's text parts joined it on `Field` (`page/field`), goes once the kit's nodes have left it. The plugin creates a missing page after the file's last page and never reorders pages, so on a file that held `Label` the new `Field` page follows `Icons` until a designer drags it into place. When it still holds a layer without a key, it stays, and the run warns. Figma refuses to remove the page it shows. On 2026-10-22 a real file threw `Removing this node is not allowed` for that page and removed it once another page showed. So before it deletes the page Figma shows, the plugin shows the first component page. After Apply, Figma shows the first component page.

`plugin/src/fake-figma.ts` is an in-memory file seeded with the synced variables, which the plugin's tests run `reconcileKit` against. It models the Figma behaviours above that real runs showed, and the ones the typings and reference pages document. It throws for any Plugin API call it does not model. `plugin/src/reconcile.test.ts` runs `reconcileKit` with one-variant kits and checks that each converges in one Apply and that a second Plan after Apply lists nothing.

`test/plugin-bundle.test.ts` loads the bundle in a `node:vm` sandbox whose only globals are the ones Figma provides, and checks that it opens the UI and answers plan, apply and unknown requests. A dependency that needs another global at load, such as `TextEncoder`, fails it.

These behaviours have not run against a real file yet:

- whether a glyph's fill override goes with the glyph when the plugin draws the icon again, which the in-memory fake assumes
- whether Figma measures an underline offset from the same line as CSS's `text-underline-offset`
- whether Figma draws a `STRIKETHROUGH` at the height and thickness Chromium draws an `auto` `line-through` in the same font
- whether an underline color bound to a role tint takes the tint's alpha as its opacity, as a bound fill does, which the in-memory fake assumes
- whether a published in-place edit reaches instances in another file with their ids intact, and what those instances show after a delete and recreate
- whether a published set moved to another page keeps its instances in other files
- how a hug or fixed size written on a text, or a fill down one, moves its auto resize, which the fake refuses to guess
- whether a text style write needs the style's font loaded, as a text layer write does, which the in-memory fake assumes for every type write. PT1 wrote a style's `fontName` without loading it, but in Inter, which Figma may load itself
- whether a literal `lineHeight` or `letterSpacing` write on a text style drops that field's binding, as PT1 saw a `fontSize` write do, which the fake assumes
- whether a style a designer duplicates copies its shared plugin data
- what linking a text style through `setTextStyleIdAsync` needs loaded and writes, which the fake takes from the typings alone: the text's fonts and the style's, and every character taking the style's font, size and line height
- what a new text style holds in the fields PT1 did not read, such as its letter spacing, case and paragraph spacing, which the fake takes as Figma's defaults
- whether writing a text's `hyperlink` needs the text's fonts loaded, which the fake assumes. A fresh run on 2026-11-19 wrote one without `loadFontAsync`, but the font may have stayed loaded from earlier
- whether a style link resets an underline's thickness and color, as it resets the decoration and its offset (DS2), which the fake assumes it does not. The docs link writes its whole underline after every style link, so either way converges
- what fill a line break that starts a text shows, which no probe wrote. The spec gives it the label's color as a cautious choice, and the fake shows the break's own fill
- what a range's fills become after a write to its text the 2026-11-19 probes did not make: a line height or letter spacing write, which the fake leaves alone, and a text style link, after which the fake gives every character the layer's fill. The plugin makes both moot by writing the ranges after every other write of a label the run touches

### Comparing with the browser

`figma:compare` checks the kit against the real Fuse components. It renders every instance in the preview frames with its Fuse component in Chromium and compares the two pictures cell by cell.

1. Build Fuse when `packages/fuse/dist` is missing or older than its sources, with `pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse build`. The comparison renders the components and loads `styles.css` and `themes.css` from that build, and it warns when the sources are newer.
2. Run the plugin and press Export snapshots. The plugin exports each instance in the preview frames of every kit page as a PNG at 2x, cut to its render bounds so that a focus ring outside the box is included. It records each instance's box and render bounds and the modes its preview frame resolves. Figma resolves a mode only for a collection the frame's layers bind or alias into, so the export adds each mode the frame or its page pins, such as the density a Badge preview pins though no Badge layer binds a density variable. The UI downloads the result as `fuse-kit-snapshots.json`. The export writes nothing to the file.
3. Run the comparison. pnpm runs the script in this package's directory, so pass an absolute path:

   ```sh
   pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:compare ~/Downloads/fuse-kit-snapshots.json
   ```

   The report goes to `.cache/figma-compare/` in this package, or to the directory `--out` names. `--threshold` sets pixelmatch's threshold, 0.1 by default. The run needs Playwright's Chromium, which `pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma exec playwright install chromium` installs.

The browser renders each preview on pages no taller than eight rows of cells, 3,728px (`MAX_PAGE_HEIGHT` in `compare/preview-page.ts`), three pages at a time. A page that held a whole preview was about 100,000px tall, past the tile memory Chromium rasterizes under, and on a 4-vCPU Linux runner a sixth of its crops came back blank or cut along a tile edge. Each page:

- The page stamps the preview's theme the way Fuse applies one: `data-theme` and `data-density` on `<html>`, and `data-theme-identity`, `data-theme-brand` and `data-theme-segment` on `<body>`. A brand mode maps to its brand by display name, and a density mode maps to its density. An `Auto` density mode, which older syncs wrote, renders nothing, since no identity implies a density.
- It names the layers in the order `styles.css` declares them, then loads Tailwind's preflight in the `base` layer, Fuse's standalone `styles.css` and `themes.css`, and sets `font-sans text-foreground` on `<body>` as the docs layout does. Preflight is compiled as a Tailwind-source host's build compiles it (`compilePreflight` in `components/utility-css.ts`), so each `--theme(--name, fallback)` it calls is the `var(--name, fallback)` a host ships. The browser applies that value and the capture records it, as the mono `font-family` on Code's `<code>`, where an uncompiled `--theme()` is a function no rule defines, which the browser drops. The page sets `--font-sans` and `--font-heading` on the themed `<body>` from `kit-fonts.ts`, as an app sets its fonts, and `--font-mono` on `:root`, where Fuse sets its own. Roboto comes from `@fontsource/roboto`, which serves Google's Roboto, the family Figma renders, in its upright and italic faces, and Roboto Mono from `@fontsource/roboto-mono`. The capture leaves the `:root` rule out as the page's own, so every capture records Fuse's `--font-mono` on `<html>`, and the build binds `font-mono` all the same. Preflight loads before `styles.css`, as in a Tailwind-source host, because its `border: 0 solid` and Fuse's base border rule share the `base` layer and the later one wins. In the other order the outline Badge draws its border in the text color. The run checks every element in a cell's measured element that holds text, an input's value or placeholder included, and stops when a face that text uses has not loaded, so the text never falls back to a system font. The one exception is a licensed family that neither the page nor the real file has, which `LICENSED_FAMILIES` in `compare/page-assets.ts` names with the reading behind it: Neo Sans, the heading font of Fjordkraft's branded themes. Its text passes where the next family in its stack has a loaded face, since both sides then draw that fallback, Roboto, and the cell compares it (`compare/font-check.ts`). Text a renderer draws around the measured element stays out of the check, since the comparison never shows it.
- `styles.css` holds only the classes Fuse's own source uses. A standalone-CSS host compiles the classes it passes to a component in its own Tailwind build, so a renderer names those classes in `hostClasses`. The run compiles them against `fuse.css` with the compiler the spec build uses, keeps only the rules that select them, and the page puts those rules in the `utilities` layer after `themes.css`, as the host's build would. Separator names its demo lengths this way, Skeleton its demo shapes, and Avatar its demo sizes.
- The run parses each variant key against the definitions in `components/kit-registry.ts`. Each cell is the element its definition's renderer builds from the Fuse build, rendered to static HTML. The page turns off animations and transitions, so a spinner stays at 0°.
- The page measures, forces and crops one element per cell: the one element in the slot whose `data-slot` is the definition's `slot`, at any depth. A radio renders inside its `RadioGroup`, so its root is not the slot's child.
- A manifest whose root takes no state of its own names the part that does in `statePart`, as Accordion's item names its trigger, or by its place in `statePlace` where the part carries no `data-slot`, as Number field names its `<input>`. The page forces hover and focus on that part inside the measured element. A group variant an element below the root names holds where an element above that one is its group, as the trigger is for the caret.
- A root a pointer hovers that takes focus only through a part inside it, which it reads through `has-focus-visible:`, names that part in `focusPart`, as File upload's dropzone names its trigger. The page forces a focus cell's focus on that part and a hover cell's hover on the root.
- A manifest whose root follows the state of a sibling before it through `peer-*` variants names that sibling in `statePeer`, as Sidebar's menu badge names its menu button. The page forces hover and focus on the sibling, which `[data-slot="…"]:has(~ measured)` selects.
- A root whose element carries no `data-slot` names the element in the definition's `element`, a tag and the attributes the element carries, each with a value or `true` for any value, and in `suffixes` those whose values end with a suffix, as `Menu group label` names `[data-slot$="-label"]`. The page measures the one element in the slot that selector matches, and `slot` names only the root layer. A root whose `data-slot` changes with the cell names its element the same way, as Pagination edge names the cell's one `<a>`, which carries `pagination-previous` or `pagination-next`, and its root layer is `pagination-edge`. Fuse gets no `data-slot` for the kit's sake, since a new one changes the DOM its consumers render.
- A preview places each preview cell at its BOOLEAN defaults, and then, for each BOOLEAN property, one instance of the first cell that shows the part, with the property at its other value, such as an Alert with `Action` on or an Item with `Actions` off. That instance's key ends in the value it sets, as `/Action=true`, and the plugin writes the value with `setProperties`. The kit check refuses a property no preview places so, so every optional part is compared in both states. The snapshot export records each instance's BOOLEAN values in `booleans`, and the comparison renders each instance with its own values: a renderer takes the properties that are on, default-true ones included, and renders exactly the parts they show, as Alert's renderer renders the action under `Action` and Item's its actions under `Actions`. An export from before the plugin wrote the values has no `booleans`, and its instances render at the defaults. A report row carries the instance's `booleans`, so the two instances of one variant stay apart.
- A composite renders and exports as one cell. Its renderer renders the whole composite, the page measures its root as any other, and the export stops at the composite's preview instance, whose PNG draws the instances inside it.
- The page forces `:hover` for `state=hover`, and `:focus` and `:focus-visible` for `state=focus` and `state=invalid-focus`, through the Chrome DevTools Protocol. It compares the styles of each forced element and its descendants with the same cell at `state=rest`, or at `state=invalid` for an invalid focus cell, whose markup draws the invalid state itself. When forcing changed nothing, it hovers or focuses that element for real before the screenshot, unless the cell is unchanged by design in the preview's theme, which the run decides before the page renders.
- An overlay renders its popup through a portal into a container it resolves in an effect, so static markup holds only the trigger, even inside `Root defaultOpen` with a `container`, as a check on 2026-10-24 showed for Popover and Dropdown menu. A popup part, a row inside a popup and an open composite therefore use a client renderer, a `ClientCellRenderer` in `compare/cell-renderers.ts`. Its `element(cell, turnedOn, container)` opens the overlay with `defaultOpen` and passes `container`, the cell's slot, to the overlay's `container` prop, so the popup lays out beside its trigger inside the slot.
- The run bundles every client renderer's module for the browser with rolldown, together with the Fuse build it imports and React (`compare/client-bundle.ts`), and the page loads the bundle. The bundle mounts each client cell in its slot with `createRoot` (`compare/client-mount.ts`), waits for the fonts its text uses and for two animation frames, in which Base UI places the popup, and then marks `<html>` with `data-compare-mounted`. Before it marks, it waits until no client slot's text holds U+2060 WORD JOINER, which Base UI appends to a live region's text on mount, as Combobox's Empty and Status do, and removes 200 ms later. The wait gives up after about five times that, and the mount fails naming the slot's set. A render that throws fails the mount with its error. The page waits for that mark before it reads a font or a style. Animations and transitions stay off, as for every cell.
- Base UI leaves state behind when an overlay opens with `defaultOpen`, as a check on 2026-10-24 showed. Select marks its selected item `data-highlighted`, and with several Selects on one page the last one opened keeps focus on that item, which Fuse's `focus:` row draws highlighted. Menu, Popover and Combobox move no focus and highlight nothing. A cell draws its highlight only where the page forces it, so the mount then blurs the element a client slot holds focus on, removes every `data-highlighted` in a client slot, and waits a frame. The page then checks that no client slot holds focus or a `data-highlighted`, and stops the run if one does. A Combobox needs a `LocaleProvider` around it, as Breadcrumb does.
- A cell whose definition's width and the padding on both sides pass one slot spans as many slots as they need, in a row of its own below the grid, so every other cell keeps its slot, as the 576px Command list spans two (`slotSpan`, `slotRects` in `compare/preview-page.ts`). A renderer whose host is wider than the root it measures names the host's width in `hostWidth`, and the cell spans the host: List detail's pane cell mounts the whole 768px demo and spans two slots, so the pane does not sit across the next cell's slot. Its client slot takes the width it spans. A cell whose viewport height and that padding pass a slot's 464px takes a row of its own that tall, as the Command dialog's 500px viewport takes a 516px row; only those cells grow, and every other row keeps its height.
- A client slot is as wide as the page's slot, since a popup shrinks to fit the slot that holds its portal (CSS 2.1 §10.3.7), and a slot as narrow as its trigger held a Dropdown menu popup to its minimum.
- A popup part or a row measures its root, as any other cell does. An open composite names the `data-slot` of its trigger and of its popup in `union`, and the page measures the box that holds both, as the composite's frame in Figma holds both instances. The page hides everything else in a client cell's slot, so a popup part's crop shows none of the trigger it anchors to. A client cell's crop must stay inside its slot, and the run stops when a popup grows past it. A renderer aligns its popup to the trigger's start, so the popup starts on the pixel grid. An open composite whose popup Base UI centers puts its trigger in the middle of a host as wide as the popup instead, as the open Select does.
- A set with a `state=highlighted` cell names in its renderer's `highlight` how the page shows the highlight. Fuse styles a Select or Dropdown menu item with `focus:`, so `highlight: "focus"` makes the page force `:focus` alone, with no keyboard ring. Fuse styles a Combobox or Command item with `data-highlighted:`, so `highlight: "data-highlighted"` makes the page write that attribute with no pseudo class. The page checks the highlighted cell against its resting twin as it checks a hover cell, and tries no real hover or focus when nothing changed, since a real focus on a menu item draws the keyboard ring. The run skips a highlighted cell whose renderer names no `highlight`.

The Button renderer, `compare/button-cell.ts`, renders both Button sets. `icon=start` adds `<Plus data-icon="inline-start" />` and `icon=end` adds `<ArrowRight data-icon="inline-end" />`. An icon button holds `<Plus />` and has an `aria-label`. Disabled sets `disabled` and pending sets `isPending`.

The Confirm button renderer, `compare/confirm-button-cell.ts`, renders `<ConfirmButton variant size>Delete</ConfirmButton>`, and disabled sets `disabled`. ConfirmButton arms only on a press, which a static render cannot make, and it writes its own `data-armed` after the props it passes to Button. So an `armed=true` cell passes `render={<button data-armed="true" />}`, whose props Base UI merges over its own. The root then carries `data-armed="true"` as a first press leaves it, and every other attribute stays as at rest, since the cell gives no armed label or `aria-label`. A disabled cell renders disarmed, as ConfirmButton does.

The Toggle renderer, `compare/toggle-cell.ts`, renders both Toggle sets. `pressed=true` sets `defaultPressed` and disabled sets `disabled`. `icon=start` and `icon=end` add a `<Star>` marked `inline-start` or `inline-end`. An icon toggle holds `<Star data-icon="inline-start" />` and has an `aria-label`.

The Toggle group renderer, `compare/toggle-group-cell.ts`, renders the docs Multiple demo, `<ToggleGroup.Root multiple defaultValue={["bold"]}>` at the cell's variant and size with the Bold, Italic and Underline items, at the default spacing.

The Tooltip renderers, `compare/tooltip-cell.ts`, are client renderers that mount the docs Basic demo's Tooltip open, `defaultOpen`, its popup portalled into the cell's slot and centred over its trigger, which a slot-wide host centres. The content cell measures the popup alone, and the page hides the trigger; the open cell measures the popup and the trigger together. Both stay centred, since Base UI writes the arrow's `left` from the trigger's centre.

A definition that declares a `viewport` gets a host as large as it on the compare page, `compare/viewport-host.ts`: a box whose `contain: layout` makes it the containing block of the fixed boxes inside it (CSS Containment 2 §3.2), which the overlay portals into, and the cell spans as many slots as the viewport's width needs. Media queries, `dvh` and `dvw` still read the page's 2400px window, so a renderer whose overlay reads `dvh` or `dvw` passes the host rules that restate those lengths against the viewport, which the host puts in a `<style>` of its own, `!important` over the utilities. The Alert dialog renderers, `compare/alert-dialog-cell.ts`, are client renderers that mount the docs demo's AlertDialog open, `defaultOpen`, in that host: the content cell measures the popup alone, and the page hides the scrim; the open cell measures the scrim and the popup together. The Dialog renderers, `compare/dialog-cell.ts`, do the same with the docs Basic and Footer close demos. The Command dialog renderers, `compare/command-dialog-cell.ts`, mount the docs Dialog demo's palette open in a 640 × 500 host, its scrim and palette measured together in the open cell, and restate the palette's `12dvh` top and `dvh` cap as 60px and 380px.

The Badge renderer, `compare/badge-cell.ts`, renders `<Badge variant size>Badge</Badge>`.

The Kbd renderers, `compare/kbd-cell.ts`, render `<Kbd>K</Kbd>` for `content=text`, `<Kbd><Backspace /></Kbd>` for `content=icon`, and `<KbdGroup><Kbd>Ctrl</Kbd><Kbd>K</Kbd></KbdGroup>` for the group.

The Radio icon button renderer, `compare/radio-icon-button-cell.ts`, renders `<RadioIconButton value="list" aria-label="List"><List /></RadioIconButton>` alone in a `<RadioGroup>`. `checked=true` sets the group's `defaultValue` to `list`, disabled sets the button's `isDisabled`, and invalid sets the group's `isInvalid`. `RadioGroup` renders its own `Field.Root`, so an invalid `Field.Root` around the group does not reach the radio.

The Radio renderer, `compare/radio-cell.ts`, renders `<RadioGroupItem value="a" aria-label="Option" />` alone in a `<RadioGroup>`. `checked=true` sets the group's `defaultValue` to `a`, disabled sets the item's `disabled`, and invalid sets the group's `isInvalid`, as for Radio icon button.

The Radio row renderer, `compare/radio-row-cell.ts`, renders `<Radio value="fixed">Fixed</Radio>` in a `RadioGroup` with no legend: a horizontal group around a `width=hug` row, and a vertical group in a `<div>` 384px wide around a `width=fill` row. `checked=true` sets the group's `defaultValue`, disabled the row's `isDisabled`, and invalid the group's `isInvalid`. The set names the Field root, the fieldset and the group around the row as its ancestors.

The Radio group renderer, `compare/radio-group-cell.ts`, renders `<RadioGroup>` in a `<div>` 384px wide with the docs Basic demo's legend and options, `Fixed` selected, and the Pending demo's description. Orientation sets `orientation`, disabled sets `isDisabled`, and invalid sets `isInvalid` and the Pending demo's `errorMessage`.

The Checkbox renderer, `compare/checkbox-cell.ts`, renders `<Checkbox aria-label="Accept" />`. `checked=true` sets `defaultChecked`, `indeterminate=true` sets `indeterminate`, and disabled sets `disabled`. Invalid wraps the checkbox in `<Field.Root invalid>`, which marks it `aria-invalid="true"` and `data-invalid`.

The Switch renderer, `compare/switch-cell.ts`, renders `<Switch aria-label="Notifications" />`. `size` sets `size`, `checked=true` sets `defaultChecked`, and disabled sets `disabled`. Invalid wraps the switch in `<Field.Root invalid>`, as for Checkbox. The page turns transitions off, so the thumb sits at its final place.

The Separator renderer, `compare/separator-cell.ts`, renders `<Separator orientation className="data-horizontal:w-64 data-vertical:h-4 data-vertical:self-auto" />`, with the lengths `separator-kit.ts` merges. Fuse's `styles.css` compiles none of those three classes, so the renderer names them as host classes, and the browser draws the lines 256 × 1 and 1 × 16.

The Skeleton renderer, `compare/skeleton-cell.ts`, renders `<Skeleton className="h-4 w-48" />` for a line and `<Skeleton className="size-10 rounded-full" />` for a circle, with the shapes `skeleton-kit.ts` merges. Fuse's `styles.css` lacks `w-48`, so the renderer names every shape class as a host class, and the browser draws a 192 × 16 line and a 40 × 40 circle. The page turns animations off, so no pulse runs and the block draws at full opacity, as the kit does.

The Loader renderer, `compare/loader-cell.ts`, renders `<Loader size aria-label="Loading" />`, as the docs demos label it. The label fills the visually hidden span, so the rendered element holds no client child. The page turns animations off, so the spinner sits at 0°, as the kit draws it.

The Label renderer, `compare/label-cell.ts`, renders `<Field.Root disabled><Field.Label>Email</Field.Label></Field.Root>`, with `disabled` set only on the disabled cell. The page measures the label, the one element with `data-slot="field-label"`. A disabled root marks itself and the label `data-disabled`.

The Field text renderers, `compare/field-text-cell.ts`, render `<Field.Description>Work address preferred.</Field.Description>` in a `<Field.Root>`, `<Field.Error>Enter a work email.</Field.Error>` in a `<Field.Root invalid>`, which stamps the error `data-invalid`, and `<Field.Legend variant>Notifications</Field.Legend>` in a `<Field.Set>`, each in a `<div>` 384px wide, the preview width the three fill as block boxes. `<Field.Title>Fixed price</Field.Title>` renders in a `<Field.Root>` with `disabled` set only on the disabled cell, with no host width, so the root and the title shrink to the text, as the kit's hugging title does.

The Input renderer, `compare/input-cell.ts`, renders `<Input>` in a `<div>` 384px wide, the preview width `field-box-kit.ts` gives Input and Textarea, since the page's slot gives the input's `w-full` no width to take. `content=placeholder` sets `placeholder`, and `content=value` sets `defaultValue`, which the markup holds as `value`, both to `name@example.com`. Read-only sets `readOnly`, and disabled sets `disabled`, which Base UI marks `data-disabled` too. Invalid wraps the input in `<Field.Root invalid>`, as for Checkbox, which marks it `aria-invalid="true"` and `data-invalid` and passes the root's `error` text color down. Each slot on the page is 528px wide, to hold Empty's 512px box, and 400px tall, to hold the open Popover, and the Popover popup below its hidden trigger, at the comfortable density, so every box fits with the crop's 8px padding on both sides, its ring stays clear of the next slot, and nothing a cell renders reaches another cell's crop.

The Input group renderer, `compare/input-group-cell.ts`, renders statically in a 384px host, since nothing portals while the inputs are closed. `form=caret` renders `<Combobox.Input>` in a `Combobox.Root`, and `form=clear` renders it with `showClear` and no trigger in a `multiple` root holding one selection, so Base UI shows the clear button beside the empty input. Both pass `w-full`, which replaces Combobox's `w-auto`, since the set draws `InputGroup.Root` as it is and the composites pass Combobox's own classes. `form=search` renders the parts `Command.Input` composes, an `InputGroup.Root` around an `InputGroup.Input` and an `InputGroup.Addon` holding MagnifyingGlass, without Command's own slot and classes. `form=caret-open` renders `form=caret` in a `defaultOpen` root, so Base UI marks the input and the caret open, and the popup, which portals in an effect, stays out of the static markup. Every cell shows the placeholder `Search…`. Disabled disables the input and the Combobox, which disables its buttons, and invalid wraps the group in `<Field.Root invalid>`, which marks the input and Combobox's caret `aria-invalid="true"` and `data-invalid`. Combobox reads its labels from `LocaleProvider`, so each cell renders in `en-US`.

The Text field renderer, `compare/text-field-cell.ts`, renders `<TextField>` in a `<div>` 384px wide with the docs Basic demo's label and description. `content=placeholder` sets the Basic demo's placeholder, and `content=value` sets the same text as `defaultValue`. Read-only sets `isReadOnly`, disabled sets `isDisabled`, and invalid sets `isInvalid` and the Error demo's `errorMessage`. The page forces focus on the `<input>`.

The Textarea renderer, `compare/textarea-cell.ts`, renders `<Field.Control render={<Textarea />}>` inside a `<Field.Root>`, as the docs' Field demo does, in a `<div>` 384px wide, as for Input.
