A plain `<textarea>` carries no `data-disabled` or `data-invalid`, while Base UI stamps both on a Field control. `Field.Control` marks its element `data-slot="field-control"`, so the renderer passes the textarea's own `data-slot="textarea"` back through it. No Fuse style reads either slot. `content=placeholder` sets `placeholder`, and `content=value` sets `defaultValue`, which the markup holds as the textarea's own text, both to `Tell us more…`. Read-only sets `readOnly`, and disabled sets the control's `disabled`, which Base UI marks `data-disabled` too. The root stays enabled there, since the kit draws no disabled Field around the textarea. Invalid sets the root's `invalid`, which marks the textarea `aria-invalid="true"` and `data-invalid` and passes the root's `error` text color down. Preflight sets `textarea { resize: vertical }` and Fuse's Textarea keeps it, so Chromium draws a resize grip in the bottom-right corner of a Textarea cell. The kit draws no grip.

The Textarea field renderer, `compare/textarea-field-cell.ts`, renders `<TextareaField>` in a `<div>` 384px wide with the docs Basic demo's label, description and placeholder. Read-only sets `readOnly`, disabled sets `isDisabled`, and invalid sets `isInvalid` and the Error demo's `errorMessage`. With the `Counter` property on, the field takes the Counter demo's `maxLength={120}`, so it renders `0/120`. The page forces focus on the textarea, which `Field.Control` marks `data-slot="field-control"`.

The Avatar renderer, `compare/avatar-cell.ts`, renders `<Avatar.Root className={size}>` with the size class `avatar-kit.ts` merges, and names the four size classes as host classes. A fallback cell holds `<Avatar.Fallback>AL</Avatar.Fallback>`. An image cell holds `<Avatar.Image keepMounted src={portrait} alt="" />` and no fallback, since Base UI's static markup holds no `<img>` without `keepMounted`, and a fallback beside it would put two filling parts in the circle. The `src` is `components/avatar-portrait.png` as a data URI, so the page needs no route for it and draws the pixels Figma draws, and the page's load waits for it to decode. The group cell renders `<Avatar.Group>` around one `<Avatar.Root>` per person of the docs Group demo, each holding `<Avatar.Fallback>` with the initials the kit's instances show.

The Meter renderer, `compare/meter-cell.ts`, renders `<Meter label="Storage used" mode value maxValue valueLabel />` with the docs Modes demo's value for the cell's level, in a `<div>` 256px wide, the preview width `meter-kit.ts` gives Meter, which the block-level root fills. Meter reads its locale from `LocaleProvider`, which throws without one, so the cell renders inside `<LocaleProvider locale="en-US">`, the locale the docs set. `valueLabel` holds the cell's text, such as `85%`, so the value is plain text after the icon and its space, where `Meter.Value` would wrap it in a span of its own. Base UI adds a visually hidden `<span role="presentation">` to the root, which the page draws nothing for.

The Pagination renderers, `compare/pagination-cell.ts`, render `<Pagination.Link href="#1" isActive>1</Pagination.Link>` with `isActive` only at `isActive=true`, `<Pagination.Previous href="#previous" />` or `<Pagination.Next href="#next" />` by direction, `<Pagination.Ellipsis />`, and the docs Basic demo's row, `<Pagination.Root>` around `<Pagination.Content>` with the previous edge, the links to pages 1 to 3 with 1 active and the next edge, each in a `<Pagination.Item>`. Every cell renders inside `<LocaleProvider locale="en-US">`. The root, the edges and the ellipsis read their copy from it, and it throws without one. The ellipsis holds its copy in a visually hidden span, which the kit draws no layer for.

The Breadcrumb renderers, `compare/breadcrumb-cell.ts`, render `<Breadcrumb.Link href="/">Home</Breadcrumb.Link>`, `<Breadcrumb.Page>Breadcrumb</Breadcrumb.Page>` and `<Breadcrumb.Ellipsis />`, each in a `<Breadcrumb.Item>`, and `<Breadcrumb.Separator />`, all inside `<Breadcrumb.Root>` and `<Breadcrumb.List>` as the docs demos place them, in `<LocaleProvider locale="en-US">`. The trail renderer renders the docs Basic demo's trail, and the Ellipsis demo's where `Ellipsis` is on, and the page measures the list. The list sets the type and the muted color each part inherits, and the item makes the part a flex item, so the page measures the box the trail gives it. The link and the page are 20px tall, the ellipsis is 20 × 20 and the separator is 14 × 14. The root and the ellipsis read their copy from the provider, and it throws without one.

The Select trigger renderer, `compare/select-trigger-cell.ts`, renders `<Select.Trigger size><Select.Value placeholder="Pick a fruit" /></Select.Trigger>` in a `<Select.Root>` whose one item's label is the same text, with no `<Select.Content>`. `content=value` selects the item, disabled sets the root's `disabled`, and invalid wraps the root in `<Field.Root invalid>`, as for Checkbox. `open=true` sets the root's `defaultOpen`, so Base UI stamps the trigger `data-popup-open`, `data-pressed` and `aria-expanded="true"` and the caret `data-popup-open`, as it does while the popup is open, and no popup renders, since the root holds none. A `width=hug` trigger hugs its value, so the cell needs no host width. A `width=fill` cell passes `w-full`, as SelectField does, in a `<div>` 384px wide.

The Select field renderer, `compare/select-field-cell.ts`, renders `<SelectField>` in a `<div>` 384px wide with the docs Basic demo's label, description, placeholder and plans as `items` and `Select.Item`s. `content=value` sets `defaultValue="pro"`, disabled sets `isDisabled`, and invalid sets `isInvalid` and the States demo's `errorMessage`. The popup portals in an effect, so the static markup holds none of it.

The menu renderers, `compare/menu-cell.ts`, render the shared parts. The Menu group label renderer is a client renderer, measured as the label. At `kind=plain` it is an open, non-modal `<Select.Root>` whose popup holds one `<Select.Group>` with `<Select.Label>Citrus</Select.Label>` and one row. At `kind=menu` and `kind=menu-inset` it is an open, non-modal `<DropdownMenu.Root>` whose popup holds one `<DropdownMenu.Group>` with the caption, `inset` at `menu-inset`, and one row. The caption sits in a `<div className="w-34">` with no `data-slot`, so the popup hugs it at Select's 144px. The Menu separator renderer is static: `<Select.Separator />` in a `<div className="box-border w-36 px-(--surface-pad-sm)">`, a popup that pads its rows, which needs no Select around it. In Select's own popup, which pads nothing, the separator's margins would put it 4px outside its container on each side.

The Select renderers, `compare/select-cell.ts`, are client renderers, each an open, non-modal `<Select.Root>` whose `<Select.Content alignItemWithTrigger={false}>` portals into the slot. The row renderer's popup holds one row, `Apple`, its value at `selected=true` and `disabled` at `state=disabled`, and the page forces `:focus` on a highlighted row. The popup renderer holds the Basic demo's rows or the Groups demo's groups with a `<Select.Separator />` between them, nothing selected. A row or a popup measures alone, so its popup aligns to the trigger's start. The open composite keeps Select's centered popup and centers the trigger in a 144px `flex justify-center` host, so the popup starts at the slot's start, and measures the trigger and the popup together.

The Dropdown menu renderers, `compare/dropdown-menu-cell.ts`, are client renderers, each an open, non-modal `<DropdownMenu.Root>` with its outline Button trigger, whose `<DropdownMenu.Content>` portals into the slot. The row renderers hold one row in a 160px `<div>` with no `data-slot`: `Profile` with its `⇧⌘P` shortcut, its tone and `inset` from the cell and a `<User />` where the cell turns `Icon` on, `Show toolbar` checked or not, or `Status bar` in a radio group whose value it is or not. The mounted page renders each cell with `Icon` off, and the turned-on rendering goes unchecked there: the icon is the renderer's own `<User />` child, which Fuse renders where the renderer writes it, so the check would read back the renderer. The popup renderer builds the docs demo the cell's `content` names from `components/dropdown-menu-text.ts`, the data the kit draws too. The sub content renderer opens the Submenu demo's submenu 24px from its popup and measures the submenu. The open composite renderer measures the Basic demo's trigger and popup together. The sub trigger renderer holds one `<DropdownMenu.Sub>` with the `Share` sub trigger, `disabled` at `state=disabled`. The page forces `:focus` on a highlighted row. At `state=open` the submenu opens, so Base UI marks the row `data-popup-open`, and its `<DropdownMenu.SubContent>` sits 24px from the popup, clear of the row's crop and its shadow. The demo's text lives in `components/dropdown-menu-text.ts`, which imports nothing.

The Combobox renderers, `compare/combobox-cell.ts`, are client renderers, each an open `<Combobox.Root defaultOpen>` in `LocaleProvider` at `en-US`, its `<Combobox.Input>` in a 384px host that the Input group's `w-auto` fills. Base UI anchors the popup to the `<input>` inside the group, so each popup is the `<input>`'s width plus 28px: 374px dense, as the kit draws it, and 366px comfortable. Its `<Combobox.Content>` portals into the slot and holds the docs demos' `<Combobox.Empty>` and `<Combobox.List>`. The row renderer lists one row, `Apple`, the value at `selected=true` and `disabled` at `state=disabled`, and the page writes `data-highlighted` on a highlighted row. The empty renderer lists no item, so Base UI marks the popup `data-empty` and the message shows. The popup renderer lists the Basic demo's rows, the Groups demo's groups with a separator between them, or no item, from `components/combobox-text.ts`, which imports only the import-free `kit-lengths.ts`. The open renderer measures the input and its popup together.

The Command renderer, `compare/command-cell.ts`, is a client renderer: an inline `<Command.Root>` in `LocaleProvider` at `en-US`, in a host carrying the palette surface's `text-popover-foreground`, holding the docs demos' `<Command.Input>` and a `<Command.List>` with one row, `New customer`, in a 320px `<div>` with no `data-slot`. The shortcut row holds `<Command.Shortcut>` with the Kbd group's caps, and the caret row is a `<Command.PageTrigger>` in a Root with a stack. The page writes `data-highlighted` on a highlighted row. The list renderer renders the Groups demo's palette in a 576px host, its rows' shortcuts `Alt` and their letters, measured as the list.

The Popover renderers, `compare/popover-cell.ts`, are client renderers, each the docs Basic demo's `<Popover.Root defaultOpen>` with its outline Button trigger, whose `<Popover.Content>` portals into the slot around the header and the two Fields. Each `<Input>` takes an empty `defaultValue`, which draws as the demo's empty input does. The popup renderer aligns the popup to the trigger's start and measures it alone. The open composite keeps the demo's centered popup, centers the trigger in a 288px `flex justify-center` host, so the popup starts at the slot's start, and measures the trigger and the popup together. The demo's text lives in `components/popover-text.ts`, which imports nothing, so the page's browser bundle takes it without the builder.

The Toast renderer, `compare/toast-cell.ts`, is a client renderer: the docs default demo's `max-w-sm` host, holding `<Toast.Provider>` and its `<Toast.Viewport>` in `LocaleProvider` at `en-US`, with a manager that raised the cell's toast before the provider mounted, with no timeout, so it stays. The page measures the toast's root.

The Tour renderers, `compare/tour-cell.ts`, are client renderers: the docs Beacon demo's `<Tour.Provider>` with a memory storage in `LocaleProvider` at `en-US`, holding its `<Tour.Popup>` with the header, description and footer, and the default demo's, holding its step card at the first of its two steps, each of which portals into the slot. A run shows a step only once the step's element is visible and has a size, and the page hides a client slot but for its measured parts, so the step's element is a `<Tour.Target>` rendering a 1px `<div>` with `visibility: visible`, in place of the Beacon demo's `<Tour.Beacon>` and the default demo's bordered `<div>`. The popup aligns to the element's start, below it, and the page measures the popup alone. A child of the Provider starts the run in an effect on mount, so the popup opens while the page mounts its cells. `useAutoStartTour` starts a run on a timer, even at `delayMs: 0`, which opens the popup after the page cleared focus from the slot, so the popup would hold focus.

The Empty renderer, `compare/empty-cell.ts`, renders `<Empty.Root variant>` around `<Empty.Header>`, which holds the docs Basic demo's `<Empty.Media variant="icon"><Tray aria-hidden /></Empty.Media>`, `<Empty.Title>No orders yet</Empty.Title>` and `<Empty.Description>Orders you create will show up here.</Empty.Description>`. The cell sits in a `<div>` 512px wide, the preview width the block-level root fills. The page is wider than `md:`, so the root takes its desktop padding, as the kit draws it. With the `Actions` property on, `<Empty.Content>` follows the header with the docs With actions demo's `<Button>Create order</Button>` and `<Button variant="secondary">Import orders</Button>`, and a preview, which keeps the property false, renders none.

The Card renderer, `compare/card-cell.ts`, renders `<Card.Root size>` in a host 448px wide, around `<Card.Header>` with the docs default demo's `<Card.Title>` and `<Card.Description>`, then `<Card.Content>` and `<Card.Footer>`, each holding the demo's `<p>`.

The Aspect ratio renderer, `compare/aspect-ratio-cell.ts`, renders `<AspectRatio ratio>` with its docs demo's `w-full max-w-*` classes in a host 448px wide, around an `<img>` of the portrait with the demos' `size-full rounded-lg object-cover`. Fuse's standalone `styles.css` holds `w-full`, `max-w-md` and `max-w-sm` but not `max-w-60`, `max-w-52` or `max-w-44`, so the renderer names the width classes as the host's own, and the page compiles the ones the standalone CSS lacks.

The Code renderer, `compare/code-cell.ts`, renders `<Code code>` with the docs default demo's source in a host 448px wide.

The Timeline list renderer, `compare/timeline-list-cell.ts`, renders `<TimelineList.Root>` in a host 512px wide, around the docs default demo's three `<TimelineList.Item>`s, each with its `<TimelineList.Title>`, `<TimelineList.Time date>` and `<TimelineList.Description>`.

The Action bar renderer, `compare/action-bar-cell.ts`, renders `<ActionBar.Root count={2}>` in an en-US `LocaleProvider`, in a positioned host 576px wide, around the default demo's count, Clear, separator and Export and Delete buttons. Its positioner centres the bar, which puts a bar of fractional width half a pixel off the page's pixel grid, so a rule of the host packs the bar at the positioner's start; the set draws the bar alone.

The Date field and Date picker renderers, `compare/date-field-cell.ts` and `compare/date-picker-cell.ts`, render the real `DateField` and a closed `DatePickerField` in an en-US `LocaleProvider`, in a host 384px wide, with the default demo's label and description, the Error demo's date for a filled cell, and `isReadOnly`, `isDisabled` or `isInvalid` with the Error demo's message for the other states. The Date range picker renderer, `compare/date-range-picker-cell.ts`, renders a closed `DateRangePickerField` the same way, with the default demo's label, description and range.

The Calendar day renderer, `compare/calendar-day-cell.ts`, renders one real `Calendar.DayGridCell` around its `Calendar.DayButton` in its week's `Calendar.DayGridRow`, the week's other days as plain empty cells, in a Calendar, or a RangeCalendar for a range cell, whose value, range, `minValue` or `isDateUnavailable` sets the cell's state. A cell at a week's edge shows a Sunday or a Saturday that is a 10th, so every cell keeps its number. Calendar marks today from the clock in a layout effect, which static markup never runs, so a today cell's button takes the `data-current` attribute Calendar writes on today's button, as File upload's drop-target cell passes the attribute its zone writes.

The Calendar and Range calendar renderers, `compare/calendar-cell.ts`, render the default demos' Roots in an en-US `LocaleProvider` around the demos' header and `Calendar.Months`.

The Sheet renderer, `compare/sheet-cell.ts`, mounts the No close button demo's Sheet open on the page, in a host as large as its viewport, with `showCloseButton={false}`, its header and its footer's outline `Sheet.Close`.

The Link renderer, `compare/link-cell.ts`, renders `<Link variant underline weight href="/invoices/1042">invoice 1042</Link>`, the link of the docs Router links example, at the cell's variant, underline and weight, which `linkLooks` in `components/link-kit.ts` reads for both the kit and the renderer. The page forces hover and focus on the `<a data-slot="link">` itself. An `always` link's hover changes only the underline's color, so the styles the page compares with the resting cell include `text-decoration-color`. The link sets no width and no type size, so the cell needs no host width, and it takes the page's 16px and preflight's line height.

The Item renderers, `compare/item-cell.ts`, render `<Item.Root variant size>` in a 384px host, which the row's `w-full` fills. An Item cell holds the cell's media, `<Item.Content>` with the title and, where `described` is on, the description, and `<Item.Actions>` around the docs Basic demo's `<span>Active</span>`. Icon media holds the Lightning. Image media holds an `<img>` whose `src` is `components/portrait.png` as a data URI, where the docs demo holds a one-pixel GIF, so the page draws the pixels Figma draws. An Item link cell renders the row through `render={<a href="#order" />}` around icon media and the title, as the docs As a link demo does, and its disabled cell marks the `<a>` `aria-disabled="true"`. The page forces hover and focus as pseudo classes, which `[a]:enabled-hover:` and `focus-visible:` read on a plain `<a>`.

The Alert renderer, `compare/alert-cell.ts`, renders `<Alert.Root variant>` in a 512px host, which the row's `w-full` fills, around the docs Variants demo's first `<Alert.Title>` and, where `described` is on, its first `<Alert.Description>`. Alert renders the tone's icon itself. With the `Action` property on, it passes `onAction` and the docs Action demo's `actionLabel="Retry"`, so Alert renders its Button. The preview places one Alert with `Action` on, and the compare page renders the action there alone.

The Accordion renderer, `compare/accordion-cell.ts`, renders one `<Accordion.Item>` in an `<Accordion.Root variant radius>` in a 384px host, around the docs Basic demo's first `<Accordion.Trigger>` and `<Accordion.Content>`. `open=true` names the item in the root's `defaultValue`, so Base UI opens it in static markup, and disabled sets the item's `disabled`.

The Sidebar renderers, `compare/sidebar-cell.ts`, render each row in the minimal context its classes read, without the panel's own chrome. A menu button cell renders `<Sidebar.MenuButton variant size isActive>` around the House and `<span>Dashboard</span>`, in a `Sidebar.MenuItem` in a `Sidebar.Menu`, in a 240px host that stands for the desktop `Sidebar.Root`: it carries the `group` marker, `data-collapsible="icon"` where the cell is collapsed and an empty one where it is not, as the Root stamps them, and the Root's `text-sidebar-foreground`. A sub button cell renders `<Sidebar.MenuSubButton href size isActive>` around `<span>Open</span>` in a 191px host, and the group label cell `<Sidebar.GroupLabel>Navigation</Sidebar.GroupLabel>` in a 240px host. A disabled row carries `aria-disabled="true"`. A badge cell renders `<Sidebar.MenuBadge>12</Sidebar.MenuBadge>` after a `hidden` `Sidebar.MenuButton` in a `Sidebar.MenuItem` in a 240px host. The badge reads the button's hover and `data-active` through its peer variants, and the button's fill would paint behind the badge's crop, which the set does not draw, so the button stays hidden. An active badge cell sets the button's `isActive`, and the page forces hover on it, the badge's `statePeer`. `absolute right-1` would put the badge's left edge at the item's width less a text width that is no whole pixel, off the pixel grid the page crops on, so the host's classes set it at the item's left edge with `!`, since Fuse's standalone `styles.css` writes its utilities in no cascade layer.

The Sidebar section renderers, in the same module, render each section in a host that stands for the desktop `Sidebar.Root` as the menu button's does, collapsed where the cell is. A group cell renders `<Sidebar.Group>` with `<Sidebar.GroupLabel>Navigation</Sidebar.GroupLabel>` and the icon collapse demo's five `Sidebar.MenuButton` rows, `Orders` active, in a 256px host. A header cell renders `<Sidebar.Header>` around `<Sidebar.Input placeholder="Search orders">`, and a footer cell `<Sidebar.Footer>` around a `Sidebar.Menu` with one `<Sidebar.MenuButton size="lg">` holding the User icon and `Reodor Felgen`, each in a 256px host. A sub menu cell renders `<Sidebar.MenuSub>` around the rich menu demo's three `<Sidebar.MenuSubItem>`s, each a `<Sidebar.MenuSubButton href size isActive>` around its `<span>`, in a 240px host. An item cell renders the rich menu demo's first `Sidebar.MenuItem`, its `Orders` row, active where the cell is, and `<Sidebar.MenuBadge>12</Sidebar.MenuBadge>`, in a 240px host, where the badge's crop is the item's.

The Sidebar panel renderer, in the same module, renders `<Sidebar.Root variant collapsible="icon" className="h-full">` around the header, a `Sidebar.Content` holding the group and a `<Sidebar.Separator>`, and the footer, each as its section renderer renders it, inside `<Sidebar.Provider className="min-h-full">`, open where the cell is expanded, inside a `LocaleProvider`, since the Provider reads its labels from it. The host stands for the demo frame. It is 448px tall and contains its paint, as the frame's `contain-paint` does, so the container, `fixed` in the browser, takes the host's height. `min-h-full` is a host class, since Fuse's own source never uses it. The page's slots are 464px tall to hold the panel and its padding.

The Navigation menu renderers, `compare/navigation-menu-cell.ts`, render each part in a `<NavigationMenu.Root>` whose List holds one `<NavigationMenu.Item value>`. A bar trigger cell renders `<NavigationMenu.Trigger>Customers</NavigationMenu.Trigger>` in a horizontal Root, and a vertical trigger cell `<NavigationMenu.Trigger>Electric car</NavigationMenu.Trigger>` in a `<NavigationMenu.Root orientation="vertical" side="right" align="end">` in a 288px host. `open=true` names the item in the Root's `defaultValue`, so Base UI stamps the trigger `data-popup-open`, `data-pressed` and `aria-expanded="true"`, as it does while the popup is open. The item holds no `NavigationMenu.Content`, and the Root renders its popup through a portal, which static markup leaves out, so no popup renders. A disabled cell sets the trigger's `disabled`. A bar link cell renders `<NavigationMenu.Link href active>Overview</NavigationMenu.Link>` in a horizontal Root, and a row link cell `<NavigationMenu.Link href active>Electricity</NavigationMenu.Link>` in a vertical Root in a 256px host. The bar parts hug their content, so their cells need no host width. The popup renderer, `compare/navigation-menu-popup-cell.ts`, is a client renderer: a `<NavigationMenu.Root defaultValue>` whose List holds only the item the cell's `content` names, its trigger and its `<NavigationMenu.Content>` with the demo's list of links, so the trigger and the popup Base UI aligns to its start begin at the slot's left edge. Fuse's `styles.css` holds no `w-64`, so the renderer names the lists' classes in `hostClasses`. The page measures the popup.

The Number field renderers, `compare/number-field-cell.ts`, render `<NumberField>` in `LocaleProvider`, which it reads its stepper labels from, holding the value `4`. A stepper cell renders the field with no label and measures its `Increase` button; the disabled cell sets `maxValue` to the value, so Base UI disables the stepper at its bound, and the read-only cell sets `isReadOnly`. A field cell renders the Basic demo's label and description in a `<div>` 384px wide; read-only sets `isReadOnly`, disabled `isDisabled`, and invalid `isInvalid` with the Error demo's `errorMessage`, and the `Denomination` property sets `kWh`.

The File upload renderer, `compare/file-upload-cell.ts`, renders the docs Compact demo, `<FileUpload.Root multiple>` around its dropzone with a small `Button` trigger and the description, in a `<div>` 512px wide. A disabled cell sets the Root's `disabled`. A drop-target cell passes `data-drop-target` to the zone, the attribute the zone writes while a file drag is over it, which static markup cannot hold.

The Toolbar renderer, `compare/toolbar-cell.ts`, renders the docs Basic demo, `<Toolbar.Root aria-label="Invoice">` in the cell's orientation, holding the File group's Download and Duplicate buttons, `Toolbar.Separator` and the disabled Delete button, each a ghost small `Button` with its start icon.

To compare a new component set:

1. Add its manifest and preview cells to `KIT` in `components/kit-registry.ts`. Name an interactive axis `state`, with `rest` among its values, so the page can force hover and focus and find the resting twin. A set with no state, such as Badge, leaves the axis out, and the page forces nothing on its cells.
2. Add a renderer module beside `compare/button-cell.ts`. It exports a `CellRendering` whose `element(cell, turnedOn)` returns the real Fuse element for a parsed cell, with the part behind each BOOLEAN property rendered exactly where `turnedOn` names the property, a property that starts true included. Set the states the markup draws, such as disabled, pressed or checked, through props. Name in `hostClasses` any class the cells pass that Fuse's own source does not use, such as a docs demo's length. A popup part, a row inside a popup or an open composite exports a `ClientCellRendering` instead, whose `element` also takes the slot to pass as the overlay's `container`, with `union` for a composite and `highlight` for a set with highlighted rows. Pass `modal={false}` to a Select or a Menu: modality only traps focus, locks scroll and hides the content outside, none of which a cell draws, so check that no class reads a modal-only attribute.
3. List the module in `compare/cell-renderers.ts` with the definition's key and the `dist/` entry the module imports. The run checks that the entry exists before it loads the module. A client renderer's entry names its module once, as `{ set, entry, module: { url, name } }` with the URL of the module beside `cell-renderers.ts` and the export's name, and both the loader and the page's bundle read it.
4. Run `pnpm exec turbo run test:compare --filter=@elmeragroup/fuse-figma`, which builds Fuse first, and capture the set as "Building a set from its capture" describes.

`report.html` opens from disk. It starts with a summary per preview and then lists every cell worst first. Each cell shows three pictures: Figma's export, the browser's crop, and the diff, which marks differing pixels red and anti-aliasing yellow. Figma's export and the browser's crop sit over the theme's background with the boxes' top-left corners aligned. Filters select a preview, a value of each variant axis, or only the flagged cells. `report.json` holds the same rows.

- Check box sizes first. Δ is Figma's box minus the browser's, in pixels. The `size` flag marks a side that differs by half a pixel or more, and such a cell goes to the top. `subpixel-size` marks a smaller difference, beyond Chromium's 1/64 px layout rounding.
- Mismatch is the percentage of the browser box's area, at 2x, whose pixels differ. The tolerant count leaves out the pixels that pixelmatch takes for anti-aliasing, and the raw count includes them. Figma and Chromium draw text differently, so a matching cell still shows some mismatch along the label. Mismatch away from text and edges points at a real difference.
- A Textarea or Textarea field cell differs in its bottom-right corner, where Chromium draws a resize grip and the kit draws none. That mismatch is expected.
- `png-size` marks an export whose size differs from its render bounds. `state-by-interaction` marks a cell that needed a real hover or focus. `state-missing` marks a cell whose state the browser could not show, so its browser picture is wrong. `no-figma-image` marks an instance Figma drew nothing for. Such a cell goes to the top. A skipped client cell keeps its diagnostics in `report.json`'s `skipped` entry.
- A hover or focus cell whose built variant equals its resting twin's is unchanged by design, such as a pressed Toggle on hover: a pressed Toggle fills with `toggle-pressed` through `aria-pressed:`, which outranks the `muted` hover tint, so its hover cell looks like its resting twin. The comparison takes each `Fuse tokens` color both variants bind at its value in the preview's theme, from the theme catalog, so a cell whose colors differ only by name is unchanged by design in a theme that gives the two tokens one color: a hovered Sidebar menu badge paints `sidebar-accent-foreground` where it paints `sidebar-foreground` at rest, and the neutral themes give both one color. The page tries no real hover or focus on such a cell. When the browser shows no change for it, the cell gets no `state-missing` flag, and the report notes it as unchanged by design. The run builds the component sets with `buildComponentSets()` in its own process, so the expectation follows the recipes the browser renders.
- The report lists an instance it cannot render as skipped, such as one whose variant has an axis the comparison does not know.

The first comparison of the test file, on 2026-10-20, covered 756 cells in the 12 previews:

- Every height matched, and every Icon button matched to the pixel apart from anti-aliasing.
- Every Button was wider in Figma, by less than a pixel: 0.41px at 12px type, 0.47px at 14px and 0.61px at 18px. Figma rounds an auto-width text layer up to a whole pixel. Its Roboto matches the browser's, so three labels in a row are 125px wide in Figma and 124.59px in Chromium. The rounding moves the right edge and nothing else. The worst cell differs by 2% of its pixels, all of it that edge and text anti-aliasing.
- A fixed label width could match the fraction, but then the label would not grow when a designer edits it, so the kit keeps auto width.

At the comfortable density, the Combobox popup and its rows are 8px narrower in the browser than in the kit: 366px and 358px, against the kit's 374px and 366px. Base UI sizes the popup from the `<input>` inside the Input group, and the caret addon beside the `<input>` is 8px wider at comfortable. A Figma width follows density only through a synced variable, and a variable for this width would bake in the kit's 384px Input group, so the kit draws the dense widths at both densities.

`figma:compare --self-check` tests the comparison itself. The package's `test:compare` task runs it, and CI's `figma-compare` job runs that task. It renders every cell of the kit's preview frames in each of two themes and saves them as a fake snapshot whose render bounds reach 4 pixels past each box, each PNG holding every device pixel its render bounds touch, so a 3px ring on a box of fractional width keeps its last column, and so does a line Tabs trigger's active underline, whose bottom sits 4px below the trigger. A client cell's render bounds reach the browser crop's whole 8px padding instead, so a popup's shadow stays in its PNG, as Figma's export holds a node's effects. It paints a square into the kit's first hover cell and widens the box of its first focus cell by a pixel, in registry order, and then it compares the snapshot with a second render. It passes when those two cells lead the report and every other cell matches exactly. The kit's client cells, popups and open composites among them, go through the same mount, measure and forced-state paths twice. A `Select item` cell at rest must also show no fill just inside its start edge, and a highlighted one the theme's `accent`, as Fuse's built `focus:bg-accent` paints it. On 2026-10-24, hand-written overlay fixtures that the kit's Popover, Dropdown menu and Select sets have since replaced showed two failures. Without the mount's settle step and the page's check of it, the highlighted Select item read `state-missing`, since its resting twin kept the focus Base UI gave it. With the Dropdown menu item's `focus:bg-accent` and `focus:text-accent-foreground` rules removed from the built `styles.css`, the self-check flagged the highlighted item `state-missing` in both themes.

The run is bound by Chromium's rendering. On 2026-11-16, in Playwright's Linux image held to 4 CPUs as a CI runner is, the task took about 2 minutes, and 6 minutes held to 1 CPU. CI runs it in a `figma-compare` job of its own beside the browser job, which renders only when a path in `.github/figma-compare-inputs.txt` changed since the pull request's base or the commit a push to `main` started from, and on every manual run. Otherwise the job passes without setting up Playwright. On a pull request, turbo also replays the cached result until the task's inputs change: the package's sources and scripts, the color package's sources, and the Fuse build, whose hash covers Fuse's sources. An edit to this README, to `test/` or to `plugin/` leaves the cache valid. A failed run names every cell that differs, with its mismatch, size difference and flags, and every skipped cell with its reason, and prints the path of `report.html`. The `figma-compare` job then uploads `report.html`, `report.json` and `cells/` as the `figma-compare-self-check` artifact, kept for 7 days.

### Building a set from its capture

Every kit entry carries a `manifest`, and the build makes its set from the browser. A manifest is an allowlist, and `capture/manifest.ts` lists its fields by kind. It names:

- the Fuse component page and parts the set draws (`api`);
- the matrix, the axis values it cannot show together (`impossible`), and the sample content: the text, whether it starts with a space, as Meter's value does after its icon, and a picture, as Avatar's portrait;
- the property names;
- the size of the host the sample renders in, a plain width from `SAMPLE_HOST_WIDTHS`, never one computed from a component's own CSS;
- the parts by key and `data-slot`, or by place;
- the part that takes the set's states, by its `data-slot` in `statePart`, or by its place in `statePlace` where it carries none, as Number field's `<input>` sits at `field/1/0/0`;
- the instance parts and the cell each shows.

An open composite adds the `data-slot` of each element its cells measure (`union`), and an anchored one its popup's `sideOffset`, as the component's `api.json` declares its default, and `alignItemWithTrigger`, as the sample's renderer passes it: Select's renderer passes `false`, where the default is `true`, since the kit draws the popup beside its trigger only. A manifest names no class, selector, layout value, fact, omitted state or measurement: what the browser measures, such as a popup's widest row, the capture measures. `captures/<set>.json` holds what Chromium made of each cell, and the build reads it through `capture/capture-cascade.ts`, which gives the mapper the `BoxCascade` of `components/box-cascade.ts` and the `LayerStyle` of `components/layer-style.ts`.

1. Build Fuse, as `figma:compare` needs.
2. Capture the switched sets, or the ones you name:

   ```sh
   pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:capture [<set>…] [--repeat 3] [--check]
   ```

   `--repeat` captures each set that many times and fails unless every run writes the same bytes. `--check` writes nothing and fails where a fresh capture differs from the kept file.

3. Probe the sizing:

   ```sh
   pnpm --filter @elmeragroup/fuse-figma figma:probe [<set>…]
   ```

How the capture reads a cell:

- It renders every cell of the set's matrix on the compare page, in the neutral, light, dense Fjordkraft private theme, with every optional part and icon turned on, a client cell's too, and forces each cell's state as `figma:compare` does. It reads the document once before it forces anything: DevTools numbers the nodes anew on each read, and its CSS agent keeps a forced state by node id.
- A cell measures one root, or an open composite's two, in its manifest's `union` order: an anchor and its popup, or the Backdrop and a modal popup.
- For each element from `<html>` down, DevTools lists the rules Chromium matched. The capture resolves each longhand's winner itself, by origin, layer, importance, specificity and order, and maps logical longhands to physical ones on the left-to-right page. It names each winner by the class it selects through, on the element or an ancestor. It matches no selector.
- It checks each element's winners against the browser: forcing all of them onto the element as important inline declarations must change no computed longhand they win. A winner the browser disagrees with fails the capture, and so does a forced state that no rule written for it shows: some rule whose selector names the forced pseudo-class, or the `data-highlighted` the page writes, must match an element of the cell, winning or losing, where none matches in the cell at rest. A state may leave every computed style as at rest, as a pressed Toggle's hover does, whose fill outranks the hover's, and still show its rule. Nothing else in the read counts, so an attribute a renderer stamps with the state proves nothing.
- It records where each custom property a winner reads is set: on the element, some elements up, by a theme layer, or nowhere. It records the winners that leave every longhand at its initial value, such as the zero margin Fuse's base layer sets on every element. Where a winner's value names `revert`, it records the value the browser's default style sheet gives that longhand on the element (`revertsTo`), which `revert` rolls the cascade back to (CSS Cascade 4 §7.3.3), and the build reads that value in its place, as a Table caption's `var(--table-caption-align, revert)` takes Chromium's `-webkit-center`.
- It records each class of a root's tree that a rule of the page's stylesheets is written for and that no such rule matched there or below. A rule is written for the first class its selector names.
- An `<input>`'s `::placeholder` rules come from the placeholder element in its own shadow root, which DevTools lists apart from the input.
- It keeps the page's `body` rule and the host's compiled utilities as the page, and leaves out the theme layers and the rules that place, hide and steady the cells.
- A fact measured in every mode holds in each mode a designer can pick for a kit frame, which the page stamps in turn: each color scheme and density, on `<html>`, with each legal theme, on `<body>`. A host rule keyed on `[data-theme="dark"]` changes a length or a level in its mode as a rule keyed on the density does. The page reads every other fact, as `oneLine` and `trackFits`, in its own mode only.
- It measures a few facts that hold on any platform, each only where it holds by a margin no font moves: the width of the element around a root that the root fills and that its own text does not change, as a popup's `min-w-36` sets it, and never a positioned element with an auto width, as Base UI's positioner, which shrinks to its content; the widest row of a root as wide as its own content, at its `min-width` or past it, more than half a pixel ahead of the next; text, or a wrapping row's items, on one line under a `max-width`; an element under its `max-height`; an element as wide with its `max-width` 24px lower, so the maximum never narrows it, a table box whose rows, cells or one child tile it exactly, a cell that sets a height and no width counting as tiling where its own text does not wrap and keeps it at that height and at the width it takes as a block at its max-content width, and a row or row group of a table that collapses its borders counting its border box, since those borders sit on the grid lines (CSS 2.1 §17.6.2), a table row whose table collapses its borders and whose borders no box sharing their edges contests (`bordersCollapse`), a flex line that leaves its items' auto margins at least 24px, a flex line that fits its items with those margins at zero and a pixel to spare, each with its items packed at its start and on a flex box that wraps in no mode, and a flex row's `inline` items on its one line in source order (`inlineRow`), a flex row whose growing items each sit at their content width for the text it renders (`itemsAtContent`), and the length a root's percentage height resolves against kept as declared (`basisAsDeclared`), each measured in every mode; how many of the elements around a root lie exactly behind it, each padding box the root's border box with no other child the page counts as reaching the root, as `capture/coinciding-around.ts` decides from the boxes the page reads. A child paints where it has a background color whose alpha is above zero, a background image, border, shadow, outline, backdrop filter or text, and wherever it draws content of its own: a replaced element such as an `<img>` or a form control, SVG or MathML, a list item, an element that `content` replaces, an open shadow root, or a generated `::before` or `::after` box. The page returns each background color as Chromium computes it, and `capture/clear-fill.ts` reads its alpha through `@elmeragroup/color`, as it does for `pseudoBoxes`, so an opaque `rgb(255, 0, 0)` paints. A color the parser cannot read counts as paint. A child that paints counts as reaching the root where a box inside it meets the root's box, as a box overflowing an empty child can, and wherever it lies where anything inside it holds text, is SVG or MathML, is a list item with a marker, is replaced through `content`, hosts an open shadow root, generates a `::before` or `::after` box, or has a shadow, filter, backdrop filter, border image, reflection or outline. A replaced element or form control counts only where its box meets the root's. The element's own text, marker, `content`, open shadow root and generated box count as a child that reaches the root, and so do its inset shadow, border image and outline. The page checks this list and measures no paint, so paint outside it, as a closed shadow root's, goes unseen until issue #766 measures the fact from Chromium's pixels; an element whose line box adds nothing to its one inline-level child, its content box's top and bottom edges the child's margin box's at its own line height and at 2px more, in every mode, a perturbation the measurement checks took; an anchored popup whose anchor is wider than its `min-width`; and a one-line ellipsis whose box is at least a pixel wider than its first character and the ellipsis together; and a grid with one column as wide as its content box, whose column at the grid's min-content width is at least a pixel narrower (`trackFits`); an element with an aspect ratio and an `auto` height whose border box is as tall as its width times the ratio and keeps its width, height and ratio in every mode, recorded as that width and height (`ratioHeight`); on a grid of two or more columns, the columns as wide as their widest item at max-content, and whether every column is at least as wide as its items at min-content, each read by writing that one track as `max-content` or `min-content` in every mode (`gridHugs`, `gridHolds`, `capture/grid-facts.ts`), and the item that sets each column's width and each row's height at its own length (`gridWidest`, `gridTallest`), and how many items the grid lays out and each item's place among them (`gridItems`, `gridItem`), only where the grid and the wrappers inside it add no text or pseudo-element of their own, and where every computed column is a length, so a subgrid, whose columns compute to `subgrid` and line names, records none of them; an element that generates no box and adds nothing to its parent's flow but its children's boxes (`noBox`, `capture/no-box.ts`); and each of an element's `::before` and `::after` that paints its box and, measured in every mode, is an absolute box laid out against the element, its `content` generating a box, its element positioned, and its size and offsets resolved to used pixels (`pseudoBoxes`, `capture/pseudo-boxes.ts`). It records an absolute element whose static parent spans its containing block in every mode, as the paragraph on absolute parts describes (`parentSpansBlock`, `capture/parent-spans-block.ts`). On an absolute element whose containing block lies above its parent, it records how many steps up that block sits, where in every mode the element paints as the block's last layer would and sits on its offsets with the block grown (`blockSteps`, `capture/block-frame.ts`). It records the axes along which an absolute element is one length, within a 64th of a pixel, in every mode (`oneLength`). It records an element whose `transform` computes to the identity matrix in every mode (`identityTransform`), as Chromium resolves any transform list to a matrix. On a block whose `width` is `100%` of a parent block that fits its content, it records the widest max-content width of the parent's other children, where every mode meets those conditions and measures one length within a 64th of a pixel (`spansBeside`), as Calendar's header records the 252px of its month grid. It records where an element's own text sits among its children.
- It keeps no measured pixels outside the facts above, of which only `ratioHeight` records a box in pixels. Around a root it keeps only the custom properties of a `style` attribute, since a host or Base UI writes the rest from what it measured. It drops the room, anchor height, transform origin and translation that Base UI's positioner measures. It records `--anchor-width` as the anchor's width, and the offset Base UI gives a popup's arrow along its side as the anchor's centre, which the build resolves. It records Toast's `--toast-height` and `--toast-frontmost-height` as `auto` (`NATURAL_HEIGHT_PROPERTIES` in `capture/run-capture.ts`). Base UI writes each as the root's `auto` height for one page's text and mode, and measures again when either changes, as `capture/natural-height.chromium.test.ts` checks on a mounted Toast. Read through the root's `h-[var(--height)]`, `auto` hugs. Drawer's `--drawer-height` and `--drawer-frontmost-height` stay as measured, since Drawer reads the popup's height without setting it to `auto`.
- It numbers the ids Base UI and React generate per cell and keeps each declaration once in a table. Captures taken on macOS and in Playwright's Linux image write the same bytes.

How the build reads a capture:

- The page's elements around the root pass down what inherits, their synced variables left out, so a `var()` keeps binding. The component's own elements around the root cascade every declaration they win, each border shorthand split into its sides, and pass down what inherits; a declaration nothing reads drops with the element's reason. The capture records which longhands each of them leaves at its initial value, so their resets drop out as the root's do. `<html>` takes the browser's initial `medium` font size, 16px, which no rule declares, so a root whose classes set no size, as Link's, inherits it.
- A declaration a child wins through its parent's class by a rule of the shape `:where(.-space-x-2 > :not(:last-child))`, which `exceptLastChildClass` in `css-selector.ts` reads, is the parent's: the children leave it out, and the parent reads a negative `space-x-*` as its item spacing, as Avatar group overlaps its avatars. Only where every child but the last wins all of it and the last child none does it draw as the item spacing; otherwise its rows stay unmapped.
- An anchored composite's root is one shared rule's. Base UI puts the popup on the side its `data-side` names, `sideOffset` pixels from the anchor, aligned as its `data-align` names. The root is a column for `top` and `bottom` and a row for `left` and `right`, with the popup first on `top` and `left`, its gap the offset and its counter alignment the popup's. An anchor that is block-level with an auto width stretches across. The root clips nothing. A manifest that opens the popup over its anchor (`alignItemWithTrigger`) stops the build.
- A modal composite's root is the captured Backdrop. The popup's own fixed place lays it out through `fixed-placement.ts`: centred, or a length below the viewport's top and centred across, as Command's `max(1rem, 12dvh)` is 60px.
- The anchor's width is the anchor's as the build maps it: the composite's host width where the anchor fills it, and the anchor instance's spec width where that set fixes it. A popup set alone takes the width of the host its sample's anchor fills, where the manifest names one. An anchor that hugs its text has a width the spec does not hold, so the popup takes its own `min-width`, 144px for Select. Base UI makes it `max(anchor, minimum)`, so that is right only for an anchor no wider than the minimum, and the kit cannot draw a wider one, since a composite's popup instance carries no `min-width` of its own. A guard holds the premise: the capture marks a popup whose anchor is more than half a pixel wider than its `min-width`, and a cell of a hugging anchor and a popup whose `width` reads `--anchor-width` with that mark stops the build, naming the popup and its `min-width`. Its width never follows its rows. The anchor's centre resolves for a popup centred on its anchor only.
- A root whose height is a percentage, as a Number field stepper's `h-full`, takes the content height the component's elements around it set, where its used height provably is the declared one. `percentHeightBasis` in `components/percent-height.ts` reads it: from the root up, each `100%` takes its parent's content height, until an element sets a height of its own, less a `border-box` element's vertical padding and the borders it draws. Every box on the way must be in flow and take its height, set no `min-height` or `max-height`, and sit in a block container, in a flex row, where the height is its cross size, or in a flex column only where it neither grows nor shrinks and its height is its basis, or where the capture measured the element that sets the height exactly that tall in every mode (`basisAsDeclared`), as Tabs list, an item of the Tabs column with a `min-height` of its own, keeps its `control-h-md`. The root's own limits leave the basis as it is, and its height folds them in. A `calc()` of the whole basis and lengths, as a Tabs trigger's `calc(100% - 1px)`, binds the variable whose formula is the same length, against the root's basis and against a parent of a fixed height alike, across a flex box's direction only, since along it a layer's length is a basis it grows or shrinks from beside its margins and its siblings, at `flex-basis: auto` with no `flex` shorthand, since any other basis replaces the height (CSS Flexbox 1 §9.2). The stepper's chain is two flex rows inside a block: its steppers' row and the field box, `h-[var(--field-h,var(--control-h-md))]` with a 1px border, give it `calc(var(--control-h-md) - 2px)`, which binds `field-box-content-h`, 34px with each variable at its smallest. Those declarations land as read. Anything else leaves the root's height unmapped: an absolute or fixed root, whose percentage takes its containing block's padding box; a flex column's item that can grow or shrink, or whose basis replaces its height; a grid item; a `display: contents` element; a height of `auto` or another percentage on the way; and edges that can outgrow the height in some mode, whose content height then floors at zero.
- A root whose manifest names no host width takes the width the capture measured around it, 376px for a Combobox row and 384px for its empty message. A measured widest row hugs its text, and the rows beside it fill; measured one-line text and an element under its `max-height` let a wrapping row drop `flex-wrap` and a capped box hug, as the paragraphs above describe.
- A static root that carries Base UI's `data-side` sits in Base UI's positioner, an absolute box with its size, which is the containing block of its absolute parts, such as a Tooltip's arrow.
- A part is the element below its box with its `data-slot`, or at its place, such as `field/1/0`; sibling parts claim their elements in order. An icon is an `<svg>` the box holds, or one inside a wrapper that marks its side with `data-icon`. A box counts the element children its element holds. An icon, and a part, must draw on the side of the box's own text that the manifest's `side` and `after` name, by its place in the markup and its `order`, or the build stops; a part out of the flow is exempt.
- An instance part's classes, on its inner root and the elements below it, are the inner set's where its own capture shows them in the same inner cell, and the composite's otherwise. An optional part or icon the composite turns off comes from the inner set's own capture, as the instance draws it hidden.
- A root clips only where some cell's root casts a shadow, which Figma draws only from a clipping frame, or where CSS clips it.
- A reset from preflight, the base layer or the browser that leaves its longhands at their initial values applies nowhere, so a box reads them as unset.
- A `list-item`, as the browser's default style makes an `<li>`, lays out as the block box it is where it draws no marker: `list-style` splits into its longhands and inherits, as CSS passes it down, and the item's own resolved `list-style-type` and `list-style-image` must both be `none`, as preflight's `list-style: none` on the list leaves them. A CSS-wide keyword in `list-style`, as `inherit`, sets all three. The capture records an item's `::marker` only where it sets a `content` other than `normal` or `none`, which draws whatever the type and image say. An item that draws a marker, or whose marker the cascade cannot resolve, leaves its `display` unmapped. An item whose one child is an inline flex or inline grid box at `width: 100%`, as List detail's rows hold their `w-full` Buttons, stacks it as a block where the capture measured that the item's line box adds nothing to the child in every mode (`lineFits`): the child spans the item's one line (CSS 2.1 §10.3.9), and the line is exactly as tall as the child. An emptied label is a known limit: an empty `inline-flex` box takes its baseline from its bottom edge, so the strut hangs below it and Chromium draws a 36px Button's item 43px tall in a 24px line, where Figma keeps the row as tall as the Button.
- A label held in an element of its own, as a Sidebar row holds its text in a `<span>`, cascades that element where a rule of a class above it reaches it, as `[&>span:last-child]:truncate` does, so the label truncates as the element does. The element is the box's child that holds the text and no `data-slot`, and the label is the box's last layer.
- A root that paints no fill takes the fill of the element around it that paints behind it, among those the capture counts as lying exactly behind the root, by the paint it checks: the innermost whose background paints. A transparent background shows what lies further out, so the search goes on past it. An opaque color is the fill, as the Sidebar Provider's wrapper paints `bg-sidebar` behind an expanded inset panel's container, and so is a synced variable where nothing further out among them paints. A translucent color, a background image, or a variable over another paint composites more than one layer, which one fill cannot draw, so those backgrounds stay unmapped and the root takes none. So does a background that an element from the root out to the one that paints it reshapes, by an opacity below 1, a rounded corner, a filter, a mask, a clip path or a blend mode: at `opacity: 0.5` Chromium paints a red wrapper's root (255, 127, 127) over white, and at a 20px radius leaves its corners white, and one opaque rectangular fill draws neither. An element with padding around the root, as a popup's content around its rows, does not lie exactly behind it, and neither does a wrapper narrower than the root, as the collapsed rail's 66px container overhangs its 64px wrapper by Fuse's own 2px (#759). The search stops at the root element of a set whose manifest holds this set as an instance part, by every `data-slot` that root carries: its own in each cell, and the one an outer set's instance part stamps on it, since that set draws the paint around its instances itself: Select item's sample renders one row in an unpadded `select-content`, which Select popup roots, so the row takes no fill, and neither does Combobox empty inside `combobox-content`. The Sidebar Provider's wrapper roots no set that holds the panel, so the panel keeps `bg-sidebar`.
- A `width: 100%` box alone in a parent that hugs it, with no margin across and its padding and border inside its width, hugs: CSS shrinks the parent to fit the box's content and resolves the percentage against that (CSS Sizing 3 §5.2.1), as the Navigation menu popup's `size-full` viewport spans the popup that hugs its `w-64` list. That holds only where nothing else sets the parent's width: a `min-width` or `max-width` of its own, or room around it that could bind, as a parent at `min-width: 400px` makes Chromium's `100%` 400px around a 256px list. There the percentage stays unmapped.
- A `width: 100%` box in a parent that fits its content beside other blocks, where the capture measured those blocks' widest max-content width as one length in every mode (`spansBeside`), hugs with that width as its minimum. CSS shrinks the parent to fit its widest child, the percentage counting as `auto` meanwhile (CSS Sizing 3 §5.2.1), and the box then spans the parent, so it is as wide as its own content or that width, whichever is wider. On 2026-11-19 a hugging row with a 252px minimum stayed 252px around narrower content and grew to hold wider content, on an instance, with a TEXT override and through a density switch (CAL2), where a row whose middle layer fills never grew past its minimum (CAL1). The parent centers its layers across where every other block is a row that centers its content and draws no fill, stroke, shadow or padding across, and spans the parent in CSS too, block-level at an `auto` or `100%` width with no width limit or horizontal margin: a hugging column that centers its layers centered a fixed 252px frame under a 341px row (CAL3). A block at `fit-content` or `max-content` sits at the parent's start, so the parent keeps its alignment beside it.
- A form control's text takes its placement from the captured element, as HTML renders the control: an `<input>` on one line, centred down its content box, and a `<textarea>` from its top left, wrapped. A manifest names no placement.
- A part whose element wins a `scale` in some cell, as Switch's thumb does while off, draws as a slot around a dot in every cell. The build finds each part's element by its slot or its place, as the cascade does.
- The shipped CSS writes `rounded-full`'s `calc(infinity * 1px)` as `3.40282e38px`, the largest float, which reads as the same infinite radius.
- A drawn box's `::before`, `::after` and `::marker` cascade over the box as a layer of their own, as the browser applies them, shorthands, `var()`s and the base layer included. A `::before` or `::after` the capture measured as an absolute box (`pseudoBoxes`), which paints, with every longhand it sets one the build reads, draws as a frame part of the box keyed `::before` or `::after` (`components/pseudo-layers.ts`), first or last among the box's parts, the tree order CSS generates them in, and maps as a part does: its fill, stroke, corners, size and place. Its `content` drops where it is an empty string, and any other `content` lands unmapped, since the layer draws no text. Every other pseudo-element's declarations land in the coverage table, since the kit draws no layer for it, and its resolved style decides the row (`capture/pseudo-elements.ts`): dropped where its `display` is `none`, which generates no box (CSS Display 3 §2.5), and dropped where it paints nothing, with empty content, no fill, no border wider than zero in a style that draws, each side read from the longhands its shorthands set, with `medium`, `none` and `currentcolor` for a part they leave out, no shadow and no outline, as a control's `after:` hit area does, and unmapped where it paints or sets a property the build does not read, such as a `backdrop-filter`, which blurs what lies behind it with no paint of its own. An instance part's inner root lists them as the instance lists its other rows.
- A class whose rules won nothing in any cell lands in the coverage table as dropped. A class written for a state no cell shows, whose rules matched no element of any cell, lands as `unshown`, a coverage outcome of its own, unless it is a transition's frame: a `data-starting-style`, `data-ending-style`, `starting` or `motion-*` variant, or a motion utility.

### The build's oracle

The build answers to checks that need no second build. Each states a property the capture build must hold by its own definition, so none compares the build with an earlier run of itself.

1. The probe holds every box's sizing to the browser. `figma:probe` measures every box of each root four times: as rendered, with the element around the root 120px wider, with the box's own text longer, and with it far longer. A box that widens with its container fills it; one that widens with its text hugs it; one with text that does neither is fixed. A box as wide as the parent that hugs it agrees with a layer fixed at the width the probe measured, where its longer text never widened it, as a calendar day's `size-9` button in its cell. Text counts there only where lengthening it could widen the box: the box's own, or text in elements that each widen with it or are as wide as their parent's content box. Text in a box of a set width, as an avatar's initials, or in a visually hidden label, as a Loader's or Breadcrumb's ellipsis's, widens no box above it. The probe treats such a box as having no text before applying text-dependent inference. A box without text that does not widen does not fill, unless it is as wide as a parent that did not widen either, which shows nothing. A box counts as wide as its parent where its margin box spans the parent's content box, as a block with an auto width does (CSS 2.1 §10.3.3), so the Sidebar separator's `mx-2` keeps it there. A box that widens with its text and stops at the room its parent leaves it fits its text, as `w-fit` does, which agrees with hug or fill, since the probe cannot tell them apart. That room is the parent's content box, less, in a flex row on one line, the row's other in-flow items and the gaps between them, as File upload's description stops beside its trigger. An absolute or fixed box is out of the flow, so the row takes nothing beside it. The parent must keep its width as the text grows. A parent that widens with the text, as a `max-content` row does, sets no cap, so a box in it does not fit. A container that shrinks to its content, a positioned one with an auto width as Base UI's positioner is, or one whose width follows the root's own text, is left as it is, since widening it shows nothing the page would do. The kind the capture build maps must not contradict what the probe infers. A fixed absolute layer whose horizontal constraint is `SCALE` or `STRETCH`, as Meter's fill, resizes with its parent, so it agrees with a box that fills, a layer that hugs only layers of a fixed width, at any depth, cannot widen with its text, so it agrees with a box that kept its width where the width that hug draws, its layers' widths with its padding and gaps, each length at the probe page's density, held to its limits, is the width the probe measured, as the Navigation menu popup's 264px around its 256px list, and a filling layer held at its literal maximum width, as Empty's content at `max-w-sm`, agrees with a box that kept its width. A contradiction fails `figma:probe` unconditionally: no file classifies one, and no set's sizing is exempt. For an anchored composite the measured gap between anchor and popup must equal `sideOffset`. The probe prints what it compared and keeps no measurement. Every box agrees, with no exception.
2. No row is `unmapped`. `unmapped` means CSS that wins in the browser and reaches no Figma field, so `capture/oracle.test.ts` fails on any `unmapped` row that `oracles/coverage.json` does not keep with a reason. The ledger keeps 30. Seven are the collapsed Sidebar row's: its ellipsis, in its own set and the three that hold it, which has no room for its first character, and the clip of its label span, in its own set and the two that show its default and sm sizes, where the row's end padding lies between the span's end and the row's clip edge. Three are the `background-clip` of Sheet content, Calendar and Range calendar, whose border is translucent in the neutral dark theme. Twelve are a Dropdown menu row's shortcut margin in the popup, open and sub content sets, which the capture does not prove fits (#773): the popup is as wide as its widest row, which leaves no pixel, and a row whose shortcut the demo leaves out holds no auto margin in the popup's markup to measure. There the row instance also cannot carry the packed row's sizing, alignment and layout. Eight are Data table filter bar's Status chip: Remove's end corners, `calc(var(--radius-md) - 1px)`, which no synced variable holds, beside the `rounded-none` that squares every segment, the chip's `items-stretch`, which the segments' `h-full` already draws, the strip's fade mask, which hides nothing while the strip does not scroll, and the value span's truncation, which only a summary wider than 224px reaches.
3. No row loses a binding. The synced variables come from the token sync, not from the build, so a row that maps to a literal while its value reads a synced variable's `var()` fails, unless the ledger's `unbound` list keeps it with a reason. It keeps 9, such as the outline Button's shadow, whose blur and spread are `max()` and `min()` over `--button-outline-width`, which no Figma variable holds.
4. Each `unshown` row is named. The ledger's `unshown` map names, by set, part and class, each class written for a state no cell of the set's matrix shows. A new one fails until the ledger names it, and one a cell starts to show fails until the ledger drops it.
5. The captures match the browser. `figma:capture --check` fails where a fresh capture differs from the kept file, on macOS and in Playwright's Linux image. A forced state reaches a rule written for it that its resting twin does not match, so every state the matrix names shows, or another cell of the set reaches that state, which shows the page forces it there while this cell's rules leave it unstyled, as a selected calendar day's hover.
6. Plan finds nothing after Apply. `plugin/src/capture-plan.fake.test.ts` applies the kit, its 54 text styles included, to a fake file and checks that Plan then lists nothing.
7. The manifests stay manifests. The oracle test rejects a manifest field the allowlist lacks, a sample host width outside `SAMPLE_HOST_WIDTHS`, and an anchored manifest whose `sideOffset` differs from its component's `api.json`.
8. Every captured declaration lands in the coverage table, by its own element. Each row the build records carries the captured declarations it accounts for: the cell, the element's place, as `0/root/1/0`, and the declaration's index. `unaccountedDeclarations` walks each element a set's capture records and checks that every author declaration on it is one a row of the set's own coverage accounts for, so a class another element carries does not stand in for it. Below an instance part's inner root, a declaration the inner set's own capture wins at the element that stands for this one, matched by `data-slot` or tag and ordinal under each instance around it, is that set's, which its own check accounts for, but only where that check reaches it: an inner element that renders nothing, or a declaration the inner check leaves out as the browser's own or a reset, lends nothing. One no inner set wins there is the composite's, which passes it. The browser's own style sheet and a reset to the initial value are left out, and so is an element that renders nothing visible, with everything below it: `display: none`, or a `clip-path: inset()` whose opposite insets meet on an element that generates a box, as `sr-only` and Base UI's visually hidden inputs clip theirs to `inset(50%)`. A `display: contents` element generates no box, so its clip hides nothing and its children count. A pseudo-element whose own `display` is `none` generates no box either, so its declarations are left out, as a Table body's `::before` outside a frame, and an inner set's hidden pseudo-element accounts for nothing a composite's shown one sets. A declaration on an element the mapping never draws fails the check until the build gives it a row or the ledger names it.
9. Every label shows the text the browser rendered. Where the build maps a label, it compares the label's manifest copy, the TEXT property's default or the characters its cell shows, with the text the capture recorded for it in that cell: the attribute a control holds its text in, compared as it is, or else its element's text nodes and those of the elements below it that draw no layer, in order, each where it is visible. Text nodes compare as the label's `white-space-collapse` renders them (CSS Text 3 §4.1.1): `collapse` folds runs of spaces, tabs and line feeds, `preserve-breaks` keeps the line feeds, and `preserve` and `break-spaces` keep every character, and a no-break space is no white space. A difference fails the build with `KitUnmappable`, naming the variant and the label, and so does a painted label whose text the capture cannot resolve. A label none of whose text paints may differ, as an outside calendar day's hidden button holds its own date (`components/label-text.ts`).

`capture/oracle.ts` reads `oracles/coverage.json`, the coverage ledger. Its `unmapped` and `unbound` lists name each row by set, part, class and property with the reason the kit keeps it, its `unshown` map lists each set's unshown rows by part and class, and its `unreached` list names each declaration check 8 finds with no row, by set, element name, class and property, with `at` listing every cell and element it covers, as `button/state=rest 0/root/0`, or `0/root/0::after` for a declaration on its pseudo-element, so an exception for one state, one element or one of its boxes excuses no other, such as the classes a composite passes to an instance, which #754 tracks, Base UI's positioner and portal, the sample's host, and Tailwind's preflight `border: 0 solid`, whose zero width draws nothing. The test also fails on a ledger entry that names a set the kit does not build, in any of the four lists.

A literal value is uncovered on purpose: a row that binds no variable and maps a literal, such as a wrong padding, font size, color or text alignment, passes every check above. Their reference is `figma:compare` on a snapshot export of the applied kit, which compares every cell with the real Fuse component in Chromium (#761).

`oracles/plan.json` is a frozen record, not a check. It lists the exact lines Plan changes when the owner applies the capture kit to the real file, which still holds the kit the recipes built: 108 entries, each with its verdict, its reason and the lines it covers. No test can recompute it without the recipe build, so it is a record for that Apply, and it is deleted once that Apply has run (#762).

`test:compare` is no oracle for the build. Its self-check renders browser cells and checks the comparison tool itself, and reads no build output.

CI runs `test:capture` in a `figma-capture` job of its own: `figma:capture --check`, `figma:probe`, the oracle tests and the Plan round trip. Like `figma-compare`, it runs only when a path in `.github/figma-capture-inputs.txt` changed.

| -------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |

| `file-variables.ts`                          | What the file holds, with Figma ids, and the changes one write makes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `sync-plan.ts`                               | Diffs a variable set against a file and builds the batch. It is pure.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `figma-api.ts`                               | The REST adapter: auth, decoding, encoding, retries and errors                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `token-sync.ts`                              | Plans, applies and verifies a sync against a `FigmaApi`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| `plural.ts`                                  | Counted nouns for the messages of `cli.ts` and `token-sync.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `components/build-kit.ts`                    | Builds the components plugin's kit spec and coverage table                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `components/component-spec.ts`               | The component definitions and the pure utilities over them: the variant matrix, the cells a set can show, variant keys and the classes a definition uses                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `components/axis-vocabulary.ts`              | The names a set's Figma-only axes may take and their values; an axis that mirrors a Fuse prop takes the prop's own name and values, and `isInvalid`, true at `state=invalid` and `state=invalid-focus`                                                                                                                                       |
| `components/component-builder.ts`            | The assembly of a set from the root of each cell: build order, coverage, clipping and the set's properties                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `components/layer-style.ts`                  | The cell ledger, each layer's computed style, inheritance, `var()` substitution and shorthand expansion                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

| `components/auto-margins.ts`                 | The auto margins a flex box's layers set that read as its alignment: an auto start margin that spaces two layers or ends a row after its filling text, an auto end margin on a row's first of three parts or an auto start margin on its last that sets that part apart from the rest, and four auto margins that centre a column's one text |
| `components/box-flow.ts`                     | How a box lays out its flow: direction and alignments, the placement its auto margins give it, which `components/auto-margins.ts` reads, a column's stretch, a grid in one column, a list item that stacks blocks, the alignment a row's parts set through `align-self`, by each layer's key, and a flex row's order-modified document order |

| `capture/pseudo-boxes.ts`                    | Which `::before` and `::after` paint and are absolute boxes laid out against their element, as the page reads them                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `capture/parent-spans-block.ts`              | Whether an absolute element's static parent spans its containing block, as the page reads it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `capture/block-frame.ts`                     | How many steps up an absolute element's containing block sits, where it can draw as that block's last layer, as the page reads it                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `capture/containing-block.ts`                | An absolute element's containing block above its parent, its padding box, how a box stacks, and the grown-block reading, which `parent-spans-block.ts` and `block-frame.ts` share                                                                                                                                                            |
| `capture/judged-facts.ts`                    | The readings the page returns that `clear-fill.ts` judges, and the facts they become                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
